# GDPR Article 30(1) Record of Processing Activities (RoPA)

This is the canonical Register of Processing Activities maintained by **Jikigai** in its capacity as **data controller** for the Soleur project, as required by Article 30(1) of Regulation (EU) 2016/679 (GDPR). It is the working document a supervisory authority (in our case the CNIL, France) would request during an audit under Article 58(1)(a).

## 0. Controller Identification (Art. 30(1)(a))

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Controller name | Jikigai SARL |
| Legal form | SARL (Société à responsabilité limitée), France |
| Registered office | 25 rue de Ponthieu, 75008 Paris, France |
| Controller contact | legal@jikigai.com |
| Representative (Art. 27) | Not applicable — controller is established in the Union |
| DPO (Art. 37) | None designated. Designation is **not mandatory**: Soleur does not carry out (i) processing as a public authority, (ii) large-scale systematic monitoring of data subjects as a core activity, or (iii) large-scale processing of Art. 9/10 special-category data. Threshold to be re-assessed quarterly. |
| Joint controllers (Art. 26) | None |
| Register custodian | Soleur Chief Legal Officer role (CLO agent) — review cadence: quarterly + on any new processing activity / new sub-processor / new third-country transfer |

**In-scope test (Art. 30(1) membership predicate).** Processing is in scope of this register when **either** limb holds, **regardless of which machine or infrastructure it runs on**:

1. **Purpose limb** — Jikigai determines the purposes of the processing (Art. 4(7)), including where Jikigai observes, measures or learns from another party's content for Jikigai's own product, research or metrics purposes; **or**
2. **Credential limb** — the processing is effected under a **Jikigai-held credential or account** (a Jikigai `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, a Jikigai vendor account, a Jikigai-held access grant to a third party's system), whoever's machine executes it; **or**
3. **Infrastructure limb** — the processing runs on **Jikigai-operated infrastructure or under a Jikigai account**, whoever determines the purpose and whoever's credential pays for it. This limb is what carries the hosted Web Platform, where a user's own content is processed on Jikigai's servers under the user's own BYOK key for the user's own purposes.

**All three limbs, not two.** An earlier draft of this re-key stated only limbs 1 and 2 and prefixed them *"regardless of which machine or infrastructure it runs on"*. That was a correction that overshot: the old carve-out's host conjunct was wrong as an **exclusion**, but dropping host as an **inclusion** limb is a different move, and it silently dropped the largest surface in the corpus out of the register — a tester connecting their repository to `app.soleur.ai` satisfies neither limb 1 nor limb 2. The three-limb form matches what every sibling artifact already states (`audits/2026-08-06-alpha-tester-controller-processor-determination.md` §2: *"a Jikigai machine, a Jikigai credential, or a Jikigai account"*).

**This predicate was re-keyed on 2026-08-06 (#7331) and the re-keying is the point.** It previously read as a **list of surfaces** plus a host-based carve-out: *"The locally-installed Soleur Claude Code plugin is out of scope — it processes no personal data on Jikigai infrastructure and Jikigai is not a controller for it."* **Both conjuncts of that carve-out are false** for an operator-assisted run: the operator ran Soleur against an alpha tester's repository content on the operator's own machine under a **Jikigai** Anthropic key, and the same run served a Jikigai dogfooding purpose — so Jikigai was a controller for that limb (PA-34). Appending one more surface to a list was the remedy applied at #7100 (`jikig-ai/operator-digest`) and it left the defect intact for a third occurrence; keying on **who determines the purpose, or whose credential effects the processing**, is what closes the class. A surface list cannot see a Jikigai-keyed run on a non-Jikigai machine, and that is exactly the shape that escaped twice.

**Surfaces currently in scope under that test (illustrative, not exhaustive — the test governs, not this list):** Soleur Web Platform (`app.soleur.ai`); Soleur documentation site (`soleur.ai`); Soleur GitHub repository (`jikig-ai/soleur`); **`jikig-ai/soleur-marketplace`**, a public Jikigai-controlled repository holding the plugin marketplace manifest, which resolves installs by `git-subdir` to the `plugins/soleur` subtree of `jikig-ai/soleur` — in scope under **limb 3** (a repository operated under a Jikigai account). It was added **alongside**, not in place of, the `jikig-ai/soleur` marketplace entry, which remains live and in scope; see PA-32 §(f) **[surface added 2026-08-12 (#7471)]**; **`jikig-ai/operator-digest`**, a separate private Jikigai-controlled repository whose weekly workflow runs under the Jikigai `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (PA-33 member (7)) **[surface added 2026-08-01 at review (#7100)]**; **operator-assisted Soleur runs against a third party's content under a Jikigai Anthropic key** (PA-34); and **Jikigai's collaborator access to an alpha tester's private repository, read for Jikigai's own metrics** (PA-35).

**Still out of scope — and note this is now a conclusion of the test, not an exception to it:** the locally-installed Soleur Claude Code plugin running **on the user's own machine, under the user's own API key, for the user's own purposes** (Posture A). Neither limb holds: Jikigai determines no purpose and no Jikigai credential is involved. That is why `docs/legal/data-protection-disclosure.md` §"The Soleur Plugin Is Not a Data Processor" and `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md` §4.1 remain **accurate for the configuration they describe** — they are narrower than, not contradicted by, the operator-assisted case. See `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-08-06-alpha-tester-controller-processor-determination.md` §4.

**Art. 30(2) processor records** live in the separate `knowledge-base/legal/article-30-2-register.md` — this file is scoped to Jikigai's **controller** capacity only.

**Out-of-scope (independent controllers):** Anthropic (when the end-user supplies their own Anthropic API key under BYOK); GitHub (repository hosting); the user's own Claude Code installation.

## Processing Activity 1 — Account & Authentication

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Create, authenticate, and maintain end-user accounts on `app.soleur.ai`; OAuth identity federation and account-linking on verified email; provisioning of per-user workspaces; secure storage of user-supplied (BYOK) third-party API keys. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | End-users of the Web Platform (registered account holders). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | Email address; hashed password (bcrypt via Supabase GoTrue); OAuth provider user ID, display name, profile picture URL (Google / Apple / GitHub / Microsoft); session tokens (JWT); MFA enrolment data where enabled; user-supplied third-party LLM API keys (BYOK), stored encrypted with AES-256-GCM. |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(b) — performance of the Terms of Service contract. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Supabase Inc (processor — auth + database); Hetzner Online GmbH (processor — hosting plane on which app servers run, incl. web-2 cpx22 in `hel1` — an out-of-band standby at serving-weight 0 currently processing no personal data); Cloudflare Inc (processor — TLS termination, edge proxy). **Bullet Train Ltd (Flagsmith)** (processor — runtime feature-flag identity-trait evaluation for `team-workspace-invite`; the workspace-invite UI/API gate calls `getIdentityFlags(identifier, { role, orgId }, /*transient*/ true)` against `edge.api.flagsmith.com`; only the pseudonymised `identifier` + traits `{role, orgId}` egress per `knowledge-base/legal/data-processing-agreements/flagsmith.md`; PA-1 first egresses on PR-2 of umbrella #4456). |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **No transfer for the Supabase + Hetzner plane.** Supabase project deployed to `eu-west-1` (Dublin, Ireland). Hetzner CX33 in `hel1` (Helsinki, Finland — web-1); a second web host **web-2** (cpx22 — 2c/4g x86/AMD, `hel1` — Helsinki, **Finland**; RE-ADDED 2026-07-24 by PR #6919 / ADR-143 as a fresh out-of-band cattle standby at serving-weight 0, replacing the *different* `fsn1` web-2 that was RETIRED 2026-07-17 by #6538) runs on the **same Hetzner EU account/region — no new sub-processor and no new third-country transfer** (both web hosts are in `hel1`, an EU datacentre of the same Hetzner account; `var.web_hosts` is validation-pinned to EU DCs `nbg1`/`fsn1`/`hel1`, verified against `apps/web-platform/infra/variables.tf` web-2 = location `hel1`, server_type `cpx22`, private_ip `10.0.1.11` — repinned from `cx23` on 2026-07-26 by #6966/#6967 when the entire cx line went unorderable in EU; same account/region, so no transfer or sub-processor change). web-2 currently processes no personal data (serving-weight 0; the sole-copy `/workspaces` volume is web-1's per ADR-119 / ADR-143 D2). **Multi-host /workspaces split (#5274 Phase 2, ADR-068):** a second Hetzner host `soleur-git-data` (CPX22 — 2c/4g x86/AMD, `hel1`; repinned from the unorderable ARM `cax11` on 2026-07-27 by #6570, same account/region) is **DECLARED in IaC but NOT YET PROVISIONED** — verified absent from the live Hetzner account 2026-08-01 (4 servers: `soleur-web-platform`, `soleur-web-2`, `soleur-registry`, `soleur-inngest`). **WHEN BORN** it will store the per-workspace BARE git data (objects/refs) reached only over a private `hcloud_network` — same Hetzner account + EU region, **no new sub-processor and no new third-country transfer**; public ingress is deny-all (firewall) and transport is private-net-only with a git-shell-restricted key. Cloudflare TLS termination is global CDN — international transfers covered by EU-US DPF certification + SCCs + Global CBPR. **Flagsmith plane:** UK-based controller (Bullet Train Ltd, England and Wales no. 12353266) currently covered by EC adequacy decision (EU) 2021/1772; UK IDTA + EU SCCs Modules 2+3 incorporated as belt-and-suspenders (per data-processing-agreement-template §11.2) against adequacy review-lapse and to flow down to Flagsmith's own US sub-processors. Managed-SaaS region not pinned (private cloud not in use). **[2026-08-20 TWO ITEMS RECORDED (#7624), neither decided here.** **(i) Transfer-test divergence (#7670):** the "no transfer" position above rests on the Supabase project being deployed to `eu-west-1`. PA-7 §(e) states that a Chapter V transfer arises from the **importer's identity, not the location of the bytes** (EDPB Guidelines 05/2021, criterion 3), and Supabase Inc is US-incorporated. The two cells therefore apply different tests to two US-incorporated processors. The contracting entity on our Supabase account — Supabase Inc or an EU entity — is the fact this turns on and is recorded nowhere; establishing it is the first step at #7670. **(ii) Unrecorded bucket (#7671):** `apps/web-platform/infra/workspaces-luks-header.tf` provisions a second Cloudflare R2 bucket, `soleur-workspaces-luks-header` (`prevent_destroy`), holding LUKS header key material bound to the volume serving per-workspace git data. Custody is Cloudflare Inc (US). It appears in no Processing Activity, no DPA Schedule 2 row and no published document. Whether a LUKS header is Art. 4(1) personal data is open: it identifies no one and decrypts nothing without the passphrase, but it is bound 1:1 to a volume holding identifiable users' data. Recorded explicitly — as PA-7 §(d) does for FreeTSA — so a future reader does not have to re-derive the exclusion, and so the gap is visible if the answer turns out to be that it IS in scope. Tracked at #7671.]** |
| **(f) Retention** | Account data retained for the life of the active account; deleted on user-initiated deletion (cascade delete via Supabase FK). Encrypted BYOK API keys deleted on workspace deletion. Session tokens expire per Supabase auth defaults. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) TLS 1.2+ in transit. (2) Encryption at rest at storage layer (Supabase managed). (3) BYOK keys encrypted application-side with AES-256-GCM before persistence. (4) Supabase Row-Level Security policies enforce per-`user_id` isolation on `public.users` (`auth.uid() = id`) and `public.api_keys` (`auth.uid() = user_id`) — RLS enabled in migration `001_initial_schema.sql`. (5) **Per-user JWT mint via `getFreshTenantClient(userId)`** for tenant-scoped reads/writes against `users` and `api_keys` — PR-C #3854 migrated ~34 call sites across `apps/web-platform/server/` away from RLS-bypassing `createServiceClient()` so RLS is evaluated under the requesting user's identity (layered control implementing Art. 25(1) data-protection-by-design: the JWT-scoped client and the RLS predicate must both be defeated to cross a tenant boundary, satisfying Art. 32(1)(b) ongoing confidentiality). (6) **BYOK plaintext-key zeroize-on-finally via `runWithByokLease(userId, async (lease) => …)`** in `cc-dispatcher.realSdkQueryFactory` (`server/cc-dispatcher.ts:889`) — plaintext API keys reside in V8 heap only within the lease's `AsyncLocalStorage` scope and are wiped by `zeroize(buf)` (`server/byok.ts:120`) in `finally` on success **or** throw — added PR-C #3854 / Closes #3392. (7) `.service-role-allowlist` + CODEOWNERS pin restricts residual service-role usage to permanent exceptions only (e.g., `auth.getUser` handshake, `sum_user_mtd_cost` RPC, attachments injection); allowlist shrunk 16 → 14 entries by PR-C #3854. (8) bcrypt password hashing (GoTrue). (9) MFA available. (10) Audit logging via Supabase + pino. (11) Signed authentication cookies (HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite). (12) **Fail-open detection on tenant-client mint** in `refreshSubscriptionStatus.tenantFor` (`apps/web-platform/server/ws-handler.ts:111` helper; `:565` caller) — when the per-user JWT mint returns `null` for a suspended/downgraded user, `reportSilentFallback` mirrors the event to Sentry and the 60 s cap-drift self-evict tick bounds the window in which a user could retain stale `users.subscription_status` / `users.plan_tier` entitlement to ≤ 60 s; added PR-C #3854 (billing-adjacent single-user-incident control per PR #3854 user-impact matrix). (13) **LUKS-at-rest on the git-data volume — DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE (activates at the #5274 Phase-3 `GIT_DATA_STORE_ENABLED` cutover; multi-host /workspaces, ADR-068):** **This measure is a prior-disclosure (Art. 13(3) advance-notice pattern) of a technical measure that is NOT in force today; it asserts NO present-tense encryption at rest of the bare per-workspace git data.** Current state, verified against the code rather than the design: `git-data-bootstrap.sh` sets `REPO_ROOT="$GIT_DATA_ROOT/repositories"` with `GIT_DATA_ROOT=/mnt/git-data`, and `git-data-provision.sh` / `git-data-transport-wrapper.sh` both default `REPO_ROOT=/mnt/git-data/repositories` — the **PLAINTEXT** `hcloud_volume.git_data`. The LUKS volume (`hcloud_volume.git_data_luks`, `cryptsetup luksOpen` → `/mnt/git-data-luks`) is opened and mounted on FIRST boot only — `luksOpen` lives in cloud-init `runcmd`, which is once-per-instance; there is no `crypttab` and the fstab entry carries `nofail`, so a reboot silently skips it (ADR-115 bars this host from the reboot primitive for the same reason). Even then it is a **mounted-but-empty cutover target**: no repository is written to it until the cutover REPOINTS the mount. The host is additionally **unborn** as of 2026-07-27, so no git data of any kind exists yet. **Corrected by #6982 before the first birth, because the birth is the event that converts a premature claim into a FALSE one** — a reader cannot be misled about a store that does not exist, but can be about one that does. The plaintext store is carried as a ledgered encryption exception tracking #6897 (`scripts/encryption-posture-ledger.json`), `disclosed_as: not-publicly-claimed`. **On activation (and not before) this measure becomes present-tense:** the fresh Hetzner block volume backing the bare per-workspace git data (objects/refs) is LUKS-encrypted at rest, replacing the prior unencrypted git-data volume at the 3.D cutover. **Re-classify this measure from DRAFTED to active when the MOUNT is repointed — `findmnt -no SOURCE /mnt/git-data` reads `/dev/mapper/git-data` — and NOT at the `GIT_DATA_STORE_ENABLED` flip.** The flag does not change the host's mount topology: `git-data-cutover.sh`'s `repoint_luks_mount` (step 5) does, and the flag flip is step 6. The flag is separately the sole WRITE gate, so a flag-only enablement outside the cutover workflow would put real user git data on the PLAINTEXT volume — marking this measure active on that event would re-create the exact false present-tense claim it exists to remove. Do NOT treat it as describing a live control before the mount fact is observed. (14) **One-way TLS host↔host proxy — DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE (same unborn #5274 Phase-3 git-data plane as (13); asserts NO present-tense measure):** inter-host traffic between the web hosts and the git-data host is TLS-encrypted in transit against a pinned self-signed CA (`rejectUnauthorized: true`), so a proxied git-data leg cannot be silently downgraded or MITM'd. (15) **Per-workspace membership-gated git-data fetch authorization — DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE (same unborn #5274 Phase-3 git-data plane as (13); asserts NO present-tense measure):** a git-data fetch is authorized only when the requesting principal is a member of the target workspace (membership check at the fetch-authz boundary), so a host cannot pull another workspace's git data. (16) **Membership re-verify on proxied sessions — DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE (same unborn #5274 Phase-3 git-data plane as (13); asserts NO present-tense measure):** when a session is proxied host→host, workspace membership is re-verified on the proxied leg (not trusted transitively from the origin host). (17) **LUKS-at-rest on the live workspaces volume (ADR-119, #6588) — ACTIVE, verified:** `/mnt/data` on the serving Hetzner host runs on the LUKS-encrypted mapper `/dev/mapper/workspaces` (backed by the Terraform volume `hcloud_volume.workspaces_luks`, which is itself a plain block device — the encryption is guest-side), so a seized, RMA'd or snapshot-imaged block volume is unreadable without the Doppler-held passphrase. Cutover certified 2026-07-23; most recently re-asserted 2026-08-02 (`workspaces-luks-verify` run 30749271370: `device_type=crypto_LUKS`, `mount_source=/dev/mapper/workspaces`, escrow ok, header readable, `workspace_count=8 expected=8`). **Verification is DAILY AND AUTOMATIC as of #6808** (`.github/workflows/workspaces-luks-verify.yml`, `schedule: 41 4 * * *`), not the operator-dispatched spot-check it was when this measure was first recorded — so the present tense of this entry rests on a standing control rather than on a dated past observation. A failing run files a `ci/luks-verify` issue classified `drift` (the at-rest claim), `readiness` (an ops/inventory event) or `unavailable` (nothing proven), and a run that never happens at all is caught by the `workspaces-luks-verify` Sentry Crons monitor **once `apply-sentry-infra.yml` has applied `sentry_cron_monitor.workspaces_luks_verify` on merge** — that apply is automatic on push to main but is a SEPARATE workflow that can fail, so treat the monitor limb as active only when the monitor reads `active` in Sentry. The `schedule:` limb and the failing-run alarm are code-backed at the merge commit and need no such qualifier. **Durable evidence query (no SSH, no dashboard):** `gh run list --workflow=workspaces-luks-verify.yml --event=schedule --limit 40 --json databaseId,conclusion,createdAt` — a `conclusion: success` row within any trailing 30-day window is what discharges the counsel attestation's `claim_decay_trigger`; `gh issue list --label ci/luks-verify --state all` is the corresponding record of every failure. **Residual, recorded not concealed:** the superseded pre-cutover plaintext volume `hcloud_volume.workspaces` (`format = "ext4"`, `apps/web-platform/infra/server.tf` at `resource "hcloud_volume" "workspaces"`) is retained attached-unmounted and un-wiped as the ADR-119 rollback backstop pending a soak that is currently blocked on **#6808**; it is ledgered as a `plaintext-exception` in `scripts/encryption-posture-ledger.json` (`tracking_issue: "#6897"`, `expires_on: 2026-10-22` — an internal commitment, never published). *(Rebase correction 2026-08-01, #6588: items (14)-(16) describe the #5274 Phase-3 git-data plane, whose host `soleur-git-data` remains UNPROVISIONED — verified absent from the live Hetzner account 2026-08-01. They are re-scoped from present-tense assertions to DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE, matching the treatment #6982 already applied to (13), rather than deleted as originally drafted by this PR: #6570 is now CLOSED and the host is orderable (repinned `cax11` → `cpx22`), so "never realised" would itself be inaccurate. Item (17) records the workspaces-volume measure that IS live.)* |

## Processing Activity 2 — Conversation Data (Messages, Turns, Usage Telemetry)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Provide conversational-AI interactions with Soleur domain-specific agents; persist user prompts, assistant responses, and tool-call metadata so users can resume, audit, and export their work; preserve aborted-turn outputs so users have a faithful record of what was produced and billed; record per-turn cost telemetry for usage transparency and billing-dispute resolution. **Turn-summary sub-purpose (feat-reasoning-chat-boxes #5370, migration 105):** on the Concierge cc-router conversation runtime, emit a durable per-turn plain-language outcome summary (the `summarize` MCP tool) so the user retains a readable record of what each successful turn accomplished — persisted as ONE `public.messages` row with `message_kind='turn_summary'`, `role='assistant'`, `user_id=founderId`, `workspace_id=founderId` (solo-pin), redacted content, written only on successful turns (dropped if the turn aborted/stopped). The summary text is the user's OWN data and inherits this activity's existing `messages` retention, Art-17 erasure, and DSAR export (see (f) and (g)). The transient live status line (`narrate` MCP tool) is **not persisted** — a live-only ephemeral frame mirroring the existing debug stream — and introduces no new personal-data category. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | End-users of the Web Platform. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | Conversation metadata (domain leader, status, timestamps); message content (`messages.content`) containing user prompts and assistant responses — content **may incidentally contain personal data the user chooses to provide** (free-text input); tool-call payload metadata; aborted-turn status markers; per-turn cost telemetry (`messages.usage` jsonb column, see below). **Attachments (PR-D #3244 §4, 2026-05-16):** `message_attachments` rows (`filename`, `content_type`, `size_bytes`, `storage_path`) FK'd to `messages.id`, plus user-uploaded file content stored in the private `chat-attachments` Storage bucket — image (PNG / JPEG / GIF / WebP) and PDF up to 24 MB. File content **may incidentally contain personal data and Art. 9 special-category data the user chooses to upload** (e.g., a brand-guide PDF that references health-related operations, an incorporation document including beneficial-owner identifiers). Filenames are sanitised (path-separator strip + C0/DEL + U+2028/U+2029 strip + 255-char cap) before persistence so a crafted filename cannot smuggle a forged "another attached file" line into the assistant's prompt context. |
| **`messages.usage` column — added by PR #3648 (2026-05-12)** | New `jsonb` column on the `messages` table populated for cc-router rows when the `CC_PERSIST_USAGE` Doppler flag is `true` in `prd` (current state: enabled). **Shape on cc-router complete turns: `{ cost_usd: number }`** — minimised to cost-only per **Art. 5(1)(c) (data minimisation)**. The legacy agent-runner path emits a full `UsageSnapshot` on aborted turns only. No additional personal data is introduced by this column; it is per-turn telemetry already attributable via the parent `messages` row. |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None systematically. Users are advised not to submit Art. 9 data via prompts or attachments (see Acceptable Use Policy); incidental ingress through either `messages.content` or `chat-attachments` content is treated as Art. 9 if discovered and triggers the `gdpr-gate` regulated-data surface rule (`hr-gdpr-gate-on-regulated-data-surfaces`). PR-D follow-up: explicit AUP warning for attachment Art. 9 upload (tracked separately). |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(b) — performance of the Terms of Service. For `messages.usage` cost telemetry: same contractual basis (necessary for billing transparency and Terms § 5.5 partial-turn consumption record). Attachments are sub-objects of a conversation (same lawful basis, same controller, same retention cascade) — no separate processing activity. For the `turn_summary` row (#5370): same Art. 6(1)(b) contractual basis (it is a record of the conversational work the user requested). **Art. 22 negative determination (#5370):** the `turn_summary` feature involves NO automated decision-making producing legal or similarly significant effects on the data subject under Art. 22 — it is a descriptive narration of work the user requested, with no decisional output about the user. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Supabase Inc (processor — `conversations` + `messages` + `message_attachments` tables + `chat-attachments` Storage bucket); Hetzner Online GmbH (processor — application compute, including transient workspace mkdir/write of downloaded attachment bytes during a turn; incl. web-2 cpx22 in `hel1` — an out-of-band standby at serving-weight 0 currently holding no workspace files); Cloudflare Inc (processor — edge proxy in front of `app.soleur.ai`). **Bullet Train Ltd (Flagsmith)** (processor — runtime feature-flag identity-trait evaluation for `byok-delegations`; the BYOK key-routing decision inside the conversation runtime calls `getIdentityFlags(identifier, { role, orgId }, /*transient*/ true)` against `edge.api.flagsmith.com`; only the pseudonymised `identifier` + traits `{role, orgId}` egress — message content does NOT egress to Flagsmith — per `knowledge-base/legal/data-processing-agreements/flagsmith.md`; PA-2 first egresses on PR-2 of umbrella #4456). **Workspace co-member recipients (mig 068 #4318, 2026-05-25):** when `TEAM_WORKSPACE_INVITE_ENABLED` flips ON, co-members of a shared workspace are recipients of each other's `messages.content` (gated by mig 059 RLS on `messages`) AND of each other's uploaded attachment bytes (gated by mig 068 widened `storage.objects` SELECT predicate via `is_attachment_path_workspace_member`). Dual-layer enforcement: the row-level RLS on `messages` (mig 059) and the Storage-object-name RLS (mig 068) must BOTH grant co-member visibility for an attachment to be reachable end-to-end. Writes remain narrow (`(storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text` via the mig 068 INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy split); co-members cannot upload INTO another user's path. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **No transfer of message bodies or attachment bytes.** Supabase EU region (eu-west-1, Ireland) holds both relational rows and Storage bucket objects; Hetzner Finland (`hel1`, web-1) holds transient per-conversation workspace files; the re-added **web-2** (`hel1`, cpx22 — repinned from cx23 by #6966/#6967; RE-ADDED 2026-07-24 by PR #6919 / ADR-143 as a fresh out-of-band cattle standby, replacing the *different* `fsn1` web-2 RETIRED 2026-07-17 by #6538; same Hetzner EU account, no new sub-processor or third-country transfer) currently holds **none** — at `replicas=1` the sole-copy `/workspaces` volume is web-1's and web-2 serves no traffic (serving-weight 0, ADR-143 D2). Cloudflare edge metadata (request headers / IP) crosses borders under DPF + SCCs + CBPR. Signed-URL presign / fetch traffic is end-to-end TLS to the EU Storage origin (Cloudflare proxies do not see attachment bytes). **Flagsmith plane (effective on PR-2 of umbrella #4456 merge):** UK-based controller (Bullet Train Limited, England and Wales no. 12353266) currently covered by EC adequacy decision for the UK; UK IDTA + EU SCCs Modules 2+3 incorporated as belt-and-suspenders (per data-processing-agreement-template §11.2) against adequacy review-lapse and to flow down to Flagsmith's own US sub-processors. Managed-SaaS region not pinned (private cloud not in use). Message content / attachment bytes / BYOK keys do NOT egress to Flagsmith — only the pseudonymised `identifier` + traits `{role, orgId}` egress per the gate at the BYOK key-routing decision. |
| **(f) Retention** | Conversations and messages retained while the user account is active. Cascade-deleted on account deletion or per-conversation deletion request. Aborted-turn rows follow the parent-conversation retention. Erasure requests under Art. 17 honoured at row granularity. **Attachments:** `message_attachments` rows cascade with `messages` (FK `ON DELETE CASCADE` on `message_id`); messages cascade with `conversations` (FK `ON DELETE CASCADE` on `conversation_id`); Storage objects in `chat-attachments` are explicitly removed in `account-delete.ts:152` and quarantined per the per-conversation deletion path. Storage bytes do NOT cascade via FK — the application removal step is load-bearing. |
| **Workspace co-member data category (feat-team-workspace-multi-user, migrations 053+058-061, 2026-05-21)** | When `FLAG_TEAM_WORKSPACE_INVITE=1` flips ON, a conversation row's `user_id` (the message author) and the workspace's other `workspace_members.user_id`s become a **new data subject relationship**: every co-member of a shared workspace is a recipient of the message author's metadata for the in-workspace conversations they participate in. Each co-member retains their independent Art. 15 / 17 / 20 rights over their own identifiable rows (rows where `messages.user_id = <co-member>.id` OR `audit_byok_use.founder_id = <co-member>.id`). Cross-member visibility is enforced by the `is_workspace_member(workspace_id, user_id)` RLS predicate (migration 053 §1.1.5); inviter-of-record consent ledger lives on `workspace_member_attestations` (migration 058, WORM). Art. 17 erasure cascade for departed members runs via the FK-reverse RPC chain `anonymise_workspace_member_attestations` → `anonymise_workspace_members` → `anonymise_organization_membership` in `server/account-delete.ts` steps 3.90–3.92 BEFORE `auth.admin.deleteUser`. Departed-member Art. 15 / 17 / 20 over their identifiable rows in former-shared workspaces is served by `server/dsar-export.ts` chains for `organizations` / `workspaces` (joinVia workspace_members) / `workspace_members` / `workspace_member_attestations`. Legal scaffolding shipped in PR #4289 (2026-05-22): ToS 2.2.0 §3b Workspace Members, AUP §5.5 owner attestation, DPD §2.3(u) co-member data category + §4.2 processor carve-out, Privacy Policy §4.11 dual-perspective disclosure, Side Letter template + register. Remaining precondition: Doppler `FLAG_TEAM_WORKSPACE_INVITE=1` + `TEAM_WORKSPACE_ALLOWLIST_ORG_IDS=<jikigai-org-id>`. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) TLS in transit. (2) Encryption at rest (Supabase managed). (3) Supabase Row-Level Security policies scoping `public.messages`, `public.conversations`, `public.team_names`, and `public.user_concurrency_slots` to the owning `user_id` (FK-join policy on `messages` via `conversations.user_id`); RLS enabled in migrations `001_initial_schema.sql` (messages, conversations), `018_team_names.sql`, and `029_plan_tier_and_concurrency_slots.sql` (user_concurrency_slots). (4) **Per-user JWT mint via `getFreshTenantClient(userId)`** for tenant-scoped reads/writes against `messages`, `conversations`, `team_names`, `user_concurrency_slots` — PR-C #3854 migrated call sites across `session-sync`, `api-messages`, `conversation-writer`, `conversations-tools`, `lookup-conversation-for-path`, `current-repo-url`, `kb-document-resolver`, `kb-route-helpers`, `ws-handler`, and `cc-dispatcher` from RLS-bypassing `createServiceClient()` to per-user JWT clients so RLS evaluates under the requesting user's identity (layered control implementing Art. 25(1) data-protection-by-design: the JWT-scoped client and the RLS predicate must both be defeated to cross a tenant boundary, satisfying Art. 32(1)(b) ongoing confidentiality). (5) **Write-boundary `assertWriteScope(userId, conversationId)` sentinel** in `cc-dispatcher` (`server/cc-dispatcher.ts:521` definition; `:1382` call site) — fail-closed runtime assertion that halts user-message persistence when the dispatch scope does not match the expected `userId`/`conversationId`; added PR-C #3854. (6) Data minimisation enforced on `messages.usage` (cc-router stores `cost_usd` only — not token counts, not prompt text). (7) Append-only audit trail via Supabase logs. (8) Cost-only schema reviewed against Art. 5(1)(c) before PR #3648 merge. (9) Cross-tenant RLS-deny integration tests per file × table tuple under `apps/web-platform/test/server/*.tenant-isolation.test.ts` (gated `TENANT_INTEGRATION_TEST=1`) — added PR-C #3854 to detect regression of the per-user-JWT path. (10) **Attachment-storage isolation (PR-D #3244 §4, 2026-05-16):** per-user folder prefix `${userId}/${conversationId}/` enforced at the Storage object name boundary; `storage.objects` RLS policies for `bucket_id='chat-attachments'` scoped by `(storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text` — migration 019 (SELECT) + migration 045 (INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE), load-bearing post-PR-D; defense-in-depth application-layer path-prefix validation at `apps/web-platform/server/attachment-pipeline.ts:83-86` (rejects path-traversal and out-of-prefix references); content-type allowlist via `ALLOWED_ATTACHMENT_TYPES` (image/PNG, image/JPEG, image/GIF, image/WebP, application/PDF); filename sanitisation (strip C0/DEL + U+2028/U+2029 + path separators; cap 255); uploads via service-role presigned URL (`createSignedUploadUrl`) with conversation-ownership verification at the presign route; `message_attachments` RLS policy joining `messages.conversation_id → conversations.user_id = auth.uid()` (migration 019 SELECT + migration 045 INSERT); cross-tenant `attachment-pipeline.tenant-isolation.test.ts` deny tests added PR-D #3244 (gated `TENANT_INTEGRATION_TEST=1`); `cc-dispatcher.ts` removed from `.service-role-allowlist` (14 → 13 PERMANENT entries) by PR-D. (11) **Art. 15(4) author-only redaction (#4319, PR #4351, manifest schema 1.1.0):** `redactRow` helper at `apps/web-platform/server/dsar-export.ts` (adjacent to `assertReadScope`) nulls all free-text personal-data + namespace-leakage columns on messages authored by a non-subject within a subject-owned conversation; canonical list at the same file's `MESSAGE_REDACT_FIELDS` constant currently covers `content`, `tool_calls`, `usage`, `draft_preview`, `action_class`, `tier`, `source`, `owning_domain`, `urgency`, `trust_tier`, `source_ref`, `leader_id`, `template_id`; CI sentinel test `apps/web-platform/test/dsar-message-redact-fields-sweep.test.ts` requires each new free-text / namespace-leaking column on `messages` to be classified as redact-or-allowlist before the migration that adds it can merge; cascades to `message_attachments` via allowlist semantic on `subjectAuthoredMessageIds` (orphan / foreign-author parents → fail-closed). Per-bundle salt-scoped pseudonym (`member_<hex12>`) via `pseudonymiseUserId(rawUserId, crypto.randomBytes(32))` replaces raw `user_id` of non-subject author — salt held in closure of `exportSqlTable`, NEVER persisted to manifest, audit, or log emission (AC5 grep enforcement). Manifest `redactions[]` field (`{path, reason: "art-15-4-rights-of-others", count}`) discloses count + reason + path to the subject per EDPB Guidelines 01/2022 §176. Legacy `user_id IS NULL` rows REDACTED under fail-closed default (`LEGACY_NULL_IS_SUBJECT = false`) because the post-mig 059 `messages_workspace_member_insert` RLS policy gates on `is_workspace_member(workspace_id, auth.uid())` — not on `user_id` matching conversation owner — and every server INSERT path omits `user_id` (defaults to NULL). PA-2 sub-purpose: Art. 15 fulfilment minimisation under Art. 15(4) rights-of-others narrowing. Internal post-redaction log line `feature: "dsar-export" op: "redact-foreign-author"` carries counts + `userIdHash` only (no PII); long-horizon WORM extension to `dsar_export_audit_pii` tracked at #4359. Companion sibling-PR gap: subject-authored messages in foreign-owned workspace conversations remain Art. 15 incomplete, tracked at #4358. (12) **Workspace co-member attachment visibility (mig 068 #4318, 2026-05-25):** `storage.objects` SELECT policy widened from own-folder-only (mig 045 `FOR ALL USING ((storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text)`) to own OR workspace co-member; co-member branch derives `workspace_id` from the path's conversation-id segment via the SECURITY DEFINER plpgsql helper `public.is_attachment_path_workspace_member(conv_id, user_id)` (SELECT `conversations.workspace_id`, delegate to `public.is_workspace_member`). Helper returns false on NULL inputs and on missing conversation; segment-2 UUID-regex precedes the `::uuid` cast for fail-closed behavior on malformed paths. Write narrowing preserved: `storage.objects` FOR INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policies enforce own-folder-only (the mig 045 invariant per `security-issues/2026-04-18-rls-for-all-using-applies-to-writes.md`). Path-physical-stability framing: the `{userId}` path segment is a technical identifier (storage-key sharding) and not itself a recipient grant — the SELECT predicate's authorisation derives from the conversation-id segment + workspace membership, not from the user-id segment match. Art. 17 disposition for the path-segment user-id: the user-id path segment is NEITHER pseudonymised NOR rewritten on member-removal / account-delete; the byte-level row is preserved under controller's Art. 6(1)(f) shared-asset retention legitimate interest, with uploader-identity unreachability achieved at the SQL layer by nulling `messages.user_id` (`public._anonymise_authored_messages_internal`, called from `public.anonymise_departed_user_across_workspaces` for account-delete AND from inside `public.remove_workspace_member` atomically for member-removal). FK to `auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` rules out synthetic-pseudonym mints (Phase 0 emergent finding E-1 of #4318 worklog). DSAR co-uploader byte-redaction enumeration resolved by PR #4486 (#4445): manifest schema 1.2.0 with `redacted: true`, `redaction_reason: "art-15-co-uploader"`, `uploader_pseudonym: "member_<hex12>"` fields on `ManifestFileEntry`; co-uploader Pass 2 enumeration in `dsar-export-co-uploader.ts` via 3-step query chain (workspace_members → conversations → messages → message_attachments); per-row-WHERE lint isolation in separate file; per-bundle salt shared with `exportSqlTable` for cross-table pseudonym consistency. Post-deletion co-member attachment Storage purge resolved by PR #4486 (#4444): step 3.9015 in `account-delete.ts` enumerates co-member `message_attachments` in departing-user-owned conversations and removes from `chat-attachments` bucket; non-fatal (identity already severed at step 3.901). Lawful-basis annotation in mig 068 SQL preamble: `Art. 6(1)(b) workspace collaboration contract + Art. 6(1)(f) shared-asset retention legitimate interest`. Migration-shape lint at `apps/web-platform/test/supabase-migrations/068-attachments-workspace-shared.test.ts` (33 assertions) covers AC1(a)-(g) + F1-F3 invariants offline. Empirical helper-resolves-from-storage-RLS-context verification at the Phase 0 R-9 spike (transactional dev-pooler test under simulated tenant JWTs; results in worklog `knowledge-base/project/specs/feat-attachments-rls-bundle-pr2-4318/phase-0-worklog.md`). (13) **Pre-merge orphan-path audit (TOM, mig 068 #4318):** before mig 068 applies to prd, an operator-pinned SQL query asserts zero malformed Storage paths: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM storage.objects WHERE bucket_id='chat-attachments' AND ((storage.foldername(name))[1] IS NULL OR (storage.foldername(name))[1] !~ '^[0-9a-f-]{36}$' OR (storage.foldername(name))[2] IS NULL OR (storage.foldername(name))[2] !~ '^[0-9a-f-]{36}$') = 0`. Non-zero blocks merge; on prd at mig 068 apply time, both segment-1 and segment-2 of every existing path were verified UUID-shaped (Phase 0 PROBE-B + OQ5, 2026-05-25). Post-flag-flip orphan regression detection is filed as a daily-cron TOM follow-up (P2, NOT blocking PR-2). (14) **Turn-summary emit-boundary redaction (feat-reasoning-chat-boxes #5370, migration 105):** the `summarize` / `narrate` MCP tool outputs pass through `formatAssistantText` + the redaction probe at the tool emit boundary BEFORE the string is buffered or persisted; **drop-on-trip** — if a recognized secret shape survives the scrub the entire narration is DROPPED (fail-loud to Sentry) rather than emitted; length caps applied at the same tool boundary; the SAME redacted string feeds both the persisted `turn_summary` row and the buffered live frame, so the live `narrate` frame is at least as protected as the persisted summary. The redaction probe scrubs paths and secret-shapes, NOT cross-tenant prose (see (15)). Migration 105 is additive + nullable (no NOT-NULL sweep); the `messages_message_kind_chk` CHECK pins `turn_summary` rows to `role='assistant'`. (15) **Cross-tenant prose control (the durable-breach risk — another tenant's data named in agent prose persisting into an exportable record):** (a) the RLS tenant-client `getFreshTenantClient(founderId)` with solo-pin `workspace_id`; (b) a system-prompt directive forbidding the agent from naming any entity/org/person/file path/skill/issue outside the user's own context; (c) `user-impact-reviewer` at PR time. NOTE: `assertWriteScope` is a **no-op forward-compat seam, NOT a control today**; the redaction probe (14) does NOT scrub cross-tenant prose — the directive (b) is the operative control, with the residual risk recorded in `knowledge-base/legal/compliance-posture.md`. (16) **`message_kind` allowlist classification + Art. 15(4) un-redacted own-export (#5370):** `message_kind` is classified in `MESSAGE_NON_REDACT_ALLOWLIST` (`apps/web-platform/server/dsar-export.ts`) — a structural discriminator describing the row's own shape, NOT third-party content; the new column is gated by the CI sentinel `apps/web-platform/test/dsar-message-redact-fields-sweep.test.ts`. The summary CONTENT lives in `content` (already a `MESSAGE_REDACT_FIELDS` redact-field). Because the Art. 15(4) author-redaction (11) keys on `user_id` (NOT `role`), and the `summarize` insert sets `user_id=founderId`, the summary content is kept UN-redacted in the user's OWN export and erased by the existing `messages` FK cascade on conversation delete AND account delete. (17) **LUKS-at-rest on the git-data volume — DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE (activates at the #5274 Phase-3 `GIT_DATA_STORE_ENABLED` cutover; multi-host /workspaces, ADR-068):** **This measure is a prior-disclosure (Art. 13(3) advance-notice pattern) of a technical measure that is NOT in force today; it asserts NO present-tense encryption at rest of the bare per-workspace git data.** Current state, verified against the code rather than the design: `git-data-bootstrap.sh` sets `REPO_ROOT="$GIT_DATA_ROOT/repositories"` with `GIT_DATA_ROOT=/mnt/git-data`, and `git-data-provision.sh` / `git-data-transport-wrapper.sh` both default `REPO_ROOT=/mnt/git-data/repositories` — the **PLAINTEXT** `hcloud_volume.git_data`. The LUKS volume (`hcloud_volume.git_data_luks`, `cryptsetup luksOpen` → `/mnt/git-data-luks`) is opened and mounted on FIRST boot only — `luksOpen` lives in cloud-init `runcmd`, which is once-per-instance; there is no `crypttab` and the fstab entry carries `nofail`, so a reboot silently skips it (ADR-115 bars this host from the reboot primitive for the same reason). Even then it is a **mounted-but-empty cutover target**: no repository is written to it until the cutover REPOINTS the mount. The host is additionally **unborn** as of 2026-07-27, so no git data of any kind exists yet. **Corrected by #6982 before the first birth, because the birth is the event that converts a premature claim into a FALSE one** — a reader cannot be misled about a store that does not exist, but can be about one that does. The plaintext store is carried as a ledgered encryption exception tracking #6897 (`scripts/encryption-posture-ledger.json`), `disclosed_as: not-publicly-claimed`. **On activation (and not before) this measure becomes present-tense:** the fresh Hetzner block volume backing the bare per-workspace git data (objects/refs) is LUKS-encrypted at rest, replacing the prior unencrypted git-data volume at the 3.D cutover. **Re-classify this measure from DRAFTED to active when the MOUNT is repointed — `findmnt -no SOURCE /mnt/git-data` reads `/dev/mapper/git-data` — and NOT at the `GIT_DATA_STORE_ENABLED` flip.** The flag does not change the host's mount topology: `git-data-cutover.sh`'s `repoint_luks_mount` (step 5) does, and the flag flip is step 6. The flag is separately the sole WRITE gate, so a flag-only enablement outside the cutover workflow would put real user git data on the PLAINTEXT volume — marking this measure active on that event would re-create the exact false present-tense claim it exists to remove. Do NOT treat it as describing a live control before the mount fact is observed. (18) **One-way TLS host↔host proxy — DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE (same unborn #5274 Phase-3 git-data plane as (17); asserts NO present-tense measure):** inter-host traffic between the web hosts and the git-data host is TLS-encrypted in transit against a pinned self-signed CA (`rejectUnauthorized: true`), so a proxied git-data leg cannot be silently downgraded or MITM'd. (19) **Per-workspace membership-gated git-data fetch authorization — DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE (same unborn #5274 Phase-3 git-data plane as (17); asserts NO present-tense measure):** a git-data fetch is authorized only when the requesting principal is a member of the target workspace (membership check at the fetch-authz boundary), so a host cannot pull another workspace's git data. (20) **Membership re-verify on proxied sessions — DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE (same unborn #5274 Phase-3 git-data plane as (17); asserts NO present-tense measure):** when a session is proxied host→host, workspace membership is re-verified on the proxied leg (not trusted transitively from the origin host). (21) **LUKS-at-rest on the live workspaces volume (ADR-119, #6588) — ACTIVE, verified:** `/mnt/data` on the serving Hetzner host — which holds the workspace checkouts this PA's conversation data references — runs on the LUKS-encrypted mapper `/dev/mapper/workspaces` (backed by the Terraform volume `hcloud_volume.workspaces_luks`, which is itself a plain block device — the encryption is guest-side); cutover certified 2026-07-23; most recently re-asserted 2026-08-02 (`workspaces-luks-verify` run 30749271370). **Verification is DAILY AND AUTOMATIC as of #6808** (`schedule: 41 4 * * *`), with a `ci/luks-verify` issue on any failure **of a scheduled run** (the alarm is schedule-gated, so a failing operator dispatch deliberately files nothing) and — **once applied on merge by `apply-sentry-infra.yml`** — a Sentry Crons monitor covering the run that never fires; durable evidence via the `gh run list` query recorded at PA-1 (g)(17). **Residual:** the superseded pre-cutover plaintext volume `hcloud_volume.workspaces` is retained attached-unmounted and un-wiped as the ADR-119 rollback backstop pending a soak blocked on **#6808**; ledgered as a `plaintext-exception` (`tracking_issue: "#6897"`, `expires_on: 2026-10-22`, internal). *(Rebase correction 2026-08-01, #6588: items (18)-(20) describe the #5274 Phase-3 git-data plane, whose host `soleur-git-data` remains UNPROVISIONED — verified absent from the live Hetzner account 2026-08-01. Re-scoped to DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE matching #6982's treatment of (17) rather than deleted: #6570 is CLOSED and the host is orderable, so "never realised" would itself be inaccurate. Item (21) records the workspaces-volume measure that IS live.)* |
| **Cross-host workspace replication between web hosts — DRAFTED / NOT-YET-ACTIVE (activates at Phase 6; ADR-068 Phase-3 GA)** | **This entry is a prior-disclosure (Art. 13(3) advance-notice pattern) of a processing activity that is NOT occurring today; it asserts NO present-tense inter-host transfer of personal data.** Current state (`replicas=1`, ADR-068): there is **no** inter-host transfer of personal data between the web hosts. The re-added web-2 (`hel1`, cpx22 — repinned from cx23 by #6966/#6967, PR #6919 / ADR-143) is an out-of-band standby at serving-weight 0 (ADR-143 D2): it serves no traffic and holds no workspace files, and the sole-copy `/workspaces` volume is web-1's (ADR-119). Pre-Phase-3 web-1 failover is a **volume-preserving reprovision (detach → recreate host → reattach → `luksOpen`), explicitly NOT replication** (ADR-143 D5) — the single volume mounts to one host at a time, so no user workspace code crosses between hosts. **Future activation:** a Phase-6 concurrent-serving topology — externally blocked on shared git-data (#6570: git-data was pinned to an unorderable `cax11` ARM type — repinned to `cpx22` on 2026-07-27, so the type is no longer the blocker; the host is still unborn and has no birth route, #6977) and gated on the ADR-068 Phase-3 GA flip — WOULD introduce inter-host replication of user workspace code (bare git objects/refs, which may incidentally contain personal data the user places in their workspace) between web hosts so that more than one host can serve the same workspace concurrently. **On activation (and not before), this entry becomes present-tense with:** (c) data subjects = End-users of the Web Platform; (c) categories = workspace file content (git objects/refs), same category already covered by PA-2's transient per-conversation workspace files; (d) recipient = Hetzner Online GmbH only (same EU account, `hel1`/`fsn1`/`nbg1` per the EU-pinned `var.web_hosts` — **no new sub-processor**); (e) third-country transfers = **none** (both replication endpoints remain EU Hetzner hosts under the `var.web_hosts` EU-DC validation pin); lawful basis = Art. 6(1)(b) contract performance (same as PA-2); (g) safeguards = the existing PA-2 (g)(17)–(20) controls (LUKS-at-rest on the git-data volume, one-way TLS host↔host proxy against a pinned self-signed CA, per-workspace membership-gated fetch authorization, membership re-verify on proxied sessions) extended to cover the inter-host replication leg, plus the ADR-143 Phase-4 fresh-boot LUKS path for web-2's own `/workspaces` volume (tracked #6931 — web-2's `for_each` volume is plaintext today per ADR-143 R3). **Re-classify this row from DRAFTED to active — and re-run the transfer / balancing analysis against the then-current infrastructure — at the ADR-068 Phase-3 GA flip; do NOT treat it as describing live processing before then.** |

## Processing Activity 3 — Subscription & Billing

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Manage paid-tier subscriptions for the Web Platform; collect payment; issue invoices; comply with French tax law. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Paying end-users of the Web Platform. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | Customer email address; Stripe customer ID; subscription status; plan + price metadata; invoice IDs. **Card data never reaches Jikigai infrastructure** — handled exclusively by Stripe Checkout (PCI SAQ-A scope). |
| **Special categories** | None. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(b) — contract performance; Art. 6(1)(c) — legal obligation (FR Code de commerce Art. L123-22, 10-year accounting record retention). |
| **(d) Recipients** | Stripe Inc (processor — payment processing); Supabase Inc (processor — stores subscription status/metadata only). |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | Stripe processes in the **US** — covered by EU-US Data Privacy Framework (adequacy decision) + Standard Contractual Clauses Module 2 as supplementary safeguard. Stripe is PCI DSS Level 1 certified. |
| **(f) Retention** | Subscription status retained while account active. Invoice and payment records retained **10 years** per FR Code de commerce Art. L123-22. |
| **(g) TOMs** | TLS 1.2+ in transit; Stripe Checkout (server-side session, client-side redirect) keeps PAN/CVC out of Jikigai scope; Stripe webhook signatures (HMAC) verified; subscription metadata isolated in Supabase under RLS. |

---

## Processing Activity 4 — Knowledge-Base Document Sharing (Public Share Links)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Allow authenticated users to share individual knowledge-base documents via public URLs; serve shared documents to unauthenticated viewers; allow revocation. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | (i) Sharing users (account holders); (ii) unauthenticated viewers of shared links. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | **Sharing user:** document ID, sharing `user_id`, creation timestamp, share token. **Viewer:** IP address, user-agent, request timestamp (server access logs only — no cookies set for unauthenticated viewers; no analytics). |
| **Special categories** | None. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(b) — contract performance for share-link records (the user activated the feature); Art. 6(1)(f) — legitimate interest for viewer access logs (infrastructure security, abuse prevention). |
| **(d) Recipients** | Supabase Inc (processor — share-link records); Cloudflare Inc (processor — access logs at edge); Hetzner Online GmbH (processor — origin logs). |
| **(e) Third-country transfers** | Cloudflare edge processing — DPF + SCCs + CBPR. Origin (Supabase, Hetzner) EU-only. |
| **(f) Retention** | Share-link records retained while the link is active; deleted on owner revocation or account deletion. Cloudflare access logs follow Cloudflare retention defaults (rolling window). |
| **(g) TOMs** | TLS in transit; `noindex` meta tag on shared pages; share tokens are cryptographically random; instant-revocation supported (post-revocation requests return error); no tracking/analytics on shared-page viewers. |

---

## Processing Activity 5 — Web Push Notifications

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Deliver browser push notifications to authenticated users when an AI agent requires their input (review-gate events) and the user is not actively connected to the Web Platform. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | End-users who have explicitly granted browser-level notification permission. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | Push subscription endpoint URL (from the browser's push service); encryption keys `p256dh` and `auth`; created-at and last-used timestamps; association to `user_id`. |
| **Special categories** | None. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(a) — consent (granted via the browser permission prompt; revocable in browser settings at any time). |
| **(d) Recipients** | Supabase Inc (processor — stores subscription records); the user's browser's **push service** (Apple APNs, Google FCM/Web Push, Mozilla autopush, Microsoft WNS) — acts as an independent infrastructure intermediary keyed by the user's browser, not contracted by Jikigai. Encrypted payloads only (RFC 8291 message encryption). |
| **(e) Third-country transfers** | Push-service endpoints resolve to provider infrastructure (typically US-based for FCM/APNs/WNS). Jikigai transmits **only an encrypted payload** to a user-controlled endpoint; the user's own browser/device vendor governs that infrastructure. Documented as user-directed transmission rather than a Jikigai-controlled transfer; counsel to confirm characterisation. |
| **(f) Retention** | Retained while account active. Expired/invalid endpoints (HTTP `410 Gone` from the push service) auto-deleted. Cascade-deleted on account deletion. |
| **(g) TOMs** | Payload encryption per RFC 8291 (p256dh + auth secret); VAPID identification of the application server; subscriptions scoped to `user_id` via Supabase RLS; revocation honoured via browser settings. |

## Processing Activity 6 — Newsletter & Waitlist Subscription

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | (i) Send periodic newsletter emails about Soleur updates, features, and content to opted-in subscribers; (ii) notify early-access/waitlist subscribers once when the hosted platform opens (the `pricing-waitlist` Buttondown tag). Both are single-purpose, consent-scoped marketing lists. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Newsletter subscribers and early-access waitlist subscribers (may or may not be Web Platform users). |
| **Collection surfaces** | Marketing-site newsletter form; pricing-page waitlist form; **shared-document CTA banner** (`apps/web-platform/components/shared/cta-banner.tsx`, anonymous public viewers) — all post to the same Buttondown endpoint. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | Email address (actively provided); IP address, referrer URL, subscription timestamp, browser/device metadata (auto-collected by Buttondown at subscription). |
| **Special categories** | None. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(a) — consent (double opt-in via Buttondown confirmation email) for the email address; Art. 6(1)(f) — legitimate interest (service operation, abuse prevention) for the auto-collected technical metadata. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Buttondown (processor); Buttondown sub-processors (current list at `buttondown.com/legal/subprocessors`). |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | Buttondown is **US-based**. Transfers under EU Standard Contractual Clauses (Commission Implementing Decision (EU) 2021/914, Module 2 — Controller-to-Processor), incorporated by reference into Buttondown's DPA. Buttondown DPA applies to all plan tiers including free. |
| **(f) Retention** | Email retained until unsubscribe; aggregate anonymised retained per Buttondown defaults. Technical metadata per Buttondown's retention practices. |
| **(g) TOMs** | TLS in transit; double opt-in confirmation (consent verification); unsubscribe link in every email (consent withdrawal); no subscriber-data sharing for advertising. |

## Processing Activity 7 — Contributor License Agreement Signatures

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Maintain an enforceable, auditable record of contributor IP-license grants to protect the integrity of Soleur's licensing framework. Both Individual CLA and Corporate CLA records are in scope. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Individual contributors signing the ICLA; authorised signatories of corporations signing the CCLA. **Additionally, and not by design — see the capture predicate below:** (i) **any GitHub account that comments on a pull request in this repository while that pull request's `license/cla` commit status is `success`**, whether or not it ever signs anything — the evidence-record write gates on that status alone, so a reviewer, a maintainer or a passer-by becomes the `actor` of an evidence record; (ii) **natural persons named on the CLA-action allowlist** at `.github/workflows/cla.yml`, whose `allowlist/` bypass records are written on allowlist membership alone — `isAllowlistBypass()` tests login membership and never signature status, so a record is written for such a principal irrespective of whether they have signed. **Today the only natural person on that allowlist is `deruelle`, who has also signed (2026-02-27, pull request 328) and is one of the two signers counted above, and who is the controller's own operator — so this population presently contains no third party and the exposure recorded here is prospective, arising the moment a natural person is allowlisted who has not signed.** The remaining allowlist principals are bot accounts and are not data subjects; (iii) **the operator recorded as `admin_actor`** in an erasure tombstone, and any data subject identified by that tombstone's `gdpr_request_ref`. **[2026-09-03 WIDENING (#7625): this cell previously read "Individual contributors signing the ICLA; authorised signatories of corporations signing the CCLA." That named the population the activity was designed for and omitted three it actually processes.
