The under-inclusion is consequential rather than cosmetic: a categories-of-data-subjects cell determines who is owed Art. 13/14 transparency, who may bring an Art. 15 request, and who must be offered the Art. 21(1) objection route that Art. 6(1)(f) processing requires — and population (i) holds no account with Jikigai and appears in no published document. Population (ii) is presently occupied only by the operator, who has signed; it is recorded because the code writes its records on membership alone, so the category exists whether or not it is occupied. The additions were established by reading the workflow conditions in this review: they are a finding against the implementation, not the repair of a prior mis-statement, which is why this is a widening and not a correction. An Art. 30(1) record-keeping incompleteness is not an Art. 4(12) personal-data breach and triggers no Art. 33/34 notification. Recorded rather than silently repaired, per this register's amendment-history convention.]** **[2026-09-03 CORPUS DIVERGENCE (#7625): `docs/legal/gdpr-policy.md` §3.4, `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md` §§4.5, 5.11 and 8.1, and `docs/legal/data-protection-disclosure.md` §2.3(n) all frame this archive as processing the data of contributors who sign, and disclose no population beyond them. Four of their statements are superseded by this cell and by "Lawful basis": (i) that an evidence record is written "for each signature" (DPD §2.3(n)) — it is written for any comment on a pull request whose `license/cla` status is green; (ii) that bypass records are for "allowlisted bot accounts (`dependabot[bot]`, `renovate[bot]`, `claude[bot]`)" (privacy-policy §5.11, DPD §2.3(n)) — the allowlist at `.github/workflows/cla.yml` also carries `deruelle`, a natural person, and `soleur-ai[bot]`, neither of which those enumerations name; (iii) that the Art. 6(1)(f) balancing published at gdpr-policy §3.4 covers this archive — that section scopes its declaration to processing "when contributors sign" while conducting its balancing under the heading "Three-part balancing test (off-site evidence archive)", so the non-signer fraction is neither covered nor excluded and the published balancing cannot be read onto it; and (iv) — **the one statement in the corpus that DENIES rather than omits what this register records** — that the erasure tombstone "preserves the integrity of the timestamp chain … without retaining any personal data beyond the SHA-256 hash of the removed object and the GDPR request reference" (gdpr-policy §3.4 sub-bullet (3)). Shape (4) at §(c) records that the tombstone additionally carries `admin_actor`, an identified natural person, and `override_reason`, operator-authored free text which "Special categories" records as a second Art. 9 surface. Privacy Policy §8.1 additionally provides no Art. 21(1) route for the involuntary population identified above, while providing one for three comparable populations. The corpus correction is tracked at #7812. Until that lands, this cell governs. Recorded here rather than left to the register-to-corpus sentinel, which is not built — that work is #7669.]** |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | **Four record shapes.** **(1) Public canonical record** — `origin/cla-signatures:signatures/cla.json`, one entry per signer: `name` (GitHub login), `id` (GitHub numeric account id), `comment_id`, `created_at` (signature timestamp), `repoId`, `pullRequestNo`. (The `SignerRow` interface at `apps/web-platform/scripts/cla-evidence/backfill.ts` is the backfill reader's view and declares `name`, `id`, `pullRequestNo`, `comment_id`, `created_at`, optional `signedOnPR` — it does **not** declare `repoId`, which the live branch content carries. Do not read it as the record's full shape.) **(2) Off-site evidence record** — `signatures/<sha256-of-payload>.json`, `EvidenceRecordSchema` at `apps/web-platform/scripts/cla-evidence/schema.ts`: `schema_version`; `comment_id`; **`comment_body` — the verbatim text of the comment, unbounded free text authored by the data subject** (`string \| null`); `comment_body_sha256`; `actor.{login, id, type}`; `pr_of_record.{number, repo}`; `cla_doc.{path, git_sha, content_sha256}` (a hash of Jikigai's text, not the subject's); `signed_at`; `capture_method` (`live \| live-degraded \| backfilled \| backfilled-pre-existed`); `workflow_run_id`; and, on a fetch failure or a deletion, `comment_body_fetch_failed`, `fetch_error`, `first_pr_signed_against`. On an **edit**, a second record is written carrying the edited body; on a **delete**, a `live-degraded` record is written with `comment_body` null. Neither withdraws the earlier record, which the §(g) floor forbids. **(3) Allowlist-bypass record** — `allowlist/<sanitised-principal>/<quarter>.json` (`sanitizePrincipal` maps `[bot]` to `-bot`, so `dependabot[bot]` is keyed `dependabot-bot`), `BypassRecord` at `apps/web-platform/scripts/cla-evidence/allowlist-bypass.ts`: `schema_version`, `principal`, `principal_safe`, `db_id`, `quarter`, `first_seen_at`, `first_pr`, `allowlist_source`. Most allowlist principals are bot accounts and are not natural persons, so those records carry no personal data; the allowlist at `.github/workflows/cla.yml` also names `deruelle`, a natural person, and that principal's records **are** personal data of an identified individual. **(4) Erasure tombstone** — `tombstones/<prior_sha>.deleted.json`, written by `apps/cla-evidence/scripts/gdpr-override.sh`: `schema_version`, `deleted_at`, `admin_actor` (the operator, an identified natural person), `gdpr_request_ref` (may identify the requesting data subject), `prior_object_sha`, `override_reason` (operator-authored free text). Recorded here rather than treated as an out-of-scope Art. 17 artefact: it is personal data held in the same bucket, for the same purpose, under the same §(g) floor, and Art. 17 machinery is itself processing. **Capture predicate — wider than "signature data".** The evidence-record write step in `.github/workflows/cla-evidence.yml` fires on any `issue_comment` (`created`, `edited`, `deleted`) on a pull request whose `license/cla` commit status is `success`, and `wait_cla` reads that status as it persists on the head SHA rather than as a property of the triggering comment. Nothing on the path tests whether the comment is the sign comment. Consequently `actor` and `comment_body` can carry a commenter who signed nothing and text that is not a signature. Recorded as a finding against the implementation; the lawful-basis consequence is at "Lawful basis" and the Art. 9 consequence at "Special categories". **Corporate CLA:** signatory name, corporate email and corporate identity, supplied out-of-band to <legal@jikigai.com> per the `custom-notsigned-prcomment` instruction in `.github/workflows/cla.yml`; no automated record is written for this route. **[2026-09-03 WIDENING (#7625): this cell previously read "GitHub username; signature timestamp; pull-request reference associated with the signing event. For Corporate CLA: signatory name + corporate email + corporate identity." That was a closed enumeration, incomplete about the one record shape it named and silent about three others. It omitted `id`, `comment_id` and `repoId` from the public branch record; the entire off-site evidence record introduced by #3201, including the verbatim `comment_body`; the `allowlist/` bypass record; and the erasure tombstone — the last two already named at §(g), so the register described object prefixes whose contents it did not record. The under-inclusion mattered beyond bookkeeping: a §(c) cell is what a DSAR scope and a breach-scope assessment are read against, and a reader relying on the prior text would have under-reported on both. It also left the internal governing record **narrower than the notice published to the data subject** — GDPR Policy § 3.4, Privacy Policy § 4.5 and DPD § 2.3(n) have disclosed the verbatim comment body since #7624 (2026-08-20) — which is the wrong direction for the two to differ. Separately, and not a prior mis-statement: the capture predicate recorded above was established for the first time in this review and is a finding against the implementation, not a repair of the record. An Art. 30(1) record-keeping incompleteness is not an Art. 4(12) personal-data breach and triggers no Art. 33/34 notification; this widening is the record-keeping discharge. Recorded rather than silently repaired, per this register's amendment-history convention.]** |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | **None sought; may arrive unsolicited — and here nothing filters it.** The off-site evidence record persists `comment_body`, the verbatim text of a GitHub pull-request comment authored by the data subject (`EvidenceRecordSchema.comment_body`, `string \| null`, in `apps/web-platform/scripts/cla-evidence/schema.ts`). **No component on the capture path compares that text against the required sign phrase** (`custom-pr-sign-comment` in `.github/workflows/cla.yml`): the write step's only gate is `steps.wait_cla.outputs.cla_state == 'success'`. A signer — or any other commenter, see the capture predicate at §(c) — can therefore place Art. 9 content into an indefinitely-retained, write-once archive. **Art. 9(2)(e) is available but does not carry this cell.** A comment posted on a pull request in a public repository is manifestly made public by its author, so the gateway is open at the moment of processing. Per `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-08-counsel-review-7100.md`, Art. 9(2)(e) is a special-category **gateway and not an Art. 6 basis**; it is not relied on in the balancing recorded at "Lawful basis". Two conditions bound it. (i) It holds only while the pull request sits in a **public** repository — `pr_of_record.repo` is unconstrained in the schema, so publicness is a present fact and not a structural invariant. (ii) It is assessed at the moment of processing, whereas §(f) is indefinite behind the §(g) write-once floor — an author who deletes the comment from the public surface removes the publicity that opened the gateway while the archived copy persists, and the `deleted` path writes a further `live-degraded` record without withdrawing the earlier one. **No filter, and no organisational control on this surface:** AUP § 4.7 is scoped by its own heading and opening sentence to the hosted Web Platform at `app.soleur.ai` (prompt field and `chat-attachments`) and does not reach a GitHub pull-request comment. Of this register's other cells that reason about unsolicited Art. 9 arrival, only **PA-27** names a control this path lacks — a system-prompt omission instruction, a statutory fast-path that never invokes the LLM, and non-persistence of bodies. **PA-32** names none, and **PA-35** denies rather than reasons. **PA-31** and **PA-33** each name AUP § 4.7 as an organisational measure over repository submissions, and **PA-17** rests `None by design.` on the same citation and repeats it inside its Art. 6(1)(f) balancing — four sites that overstate § 4.7 for the reason just given. Those are defects of their own activities' records, are not amended here, and are tracked at #7815; PA-7 relies on § 4.7 in no form. **The erasure tombstone is a second and distinct Art. 9 surface.** `apps/cla-evidence/scripts/gdpr-override.sh` writes `override_reason` — operator-authored free text stating why an erasure was granted — alongside `gdpr_request_ref`. A reason of the form "subject asked us to remove a health disclosure" is itself Art. 9 content, is written **about** a data subject rather than by them (so Art. 9(2)(e) is structurally unavailable), and is the one object in this bucket that an Art. 17 erasure **creates** rather than removes. Gateway relied on there is Art. 9(2)(f) — establishment, exercise or defence of legal claims — the same ground as the Art. 17(3)(e) carve-out at §(f). **Named residual (accepted at present scale, not as a steady state):** erasure of an Art. 9 disclosure runs only through `gdpr-override.sh`, which §(g) itself records as "not absolute". The signer population is two. Two remediations are recorded as options and are **not** adopted here, being engineering decisions: a sign-phrase equality check before the evidence write, and a closed vocabulary for `override_reason`. A GitHub login, numeric account id and contributor identity are **not** Art. 9 special categories. **No Art. 10 data** is sought or expected on this path. **[2026-09-03 CORRECTION (#7625): this cell previously read "None." That was not sustainable. The evidence record persists an unbounded free-text field authored by the data subject, no filter compares it to the sign phrase, and retention is indefinite behind a write-once floor — the conditions under which four of this register's own cells — PA-27, PA-31, PA-32 and PA-33 — reason rather than deny. The strongest argument for the prior text, that the comment is manifestly made public, is Art. 9(2)(e): a gateway that concedes the point rather than a denial that avoids it, and one whose two conditions are recorded above. Recorded rather than silently repaired, per this register's amendment-history convention.]** |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(f) — legitimate interest in maintaining an enforceable record of IP-license grants. **Balancing test — signature records (shapes (1) and (2) where the actor is a signer):** (i) the data is publicly disclosed by the signer through the act of contributing on a public pull request; (ii) processing is the minimum necessary to evidence the grant; (iii) **the signer is informed before signing, and not by the receipt** — the upstream CLA action posts `custom-notsigned-prcomment` (`.github/workflows/cla.yml`) linking to the published CLA at `https://soleur.ai/pages/legal/individual-cla.html`, built from `plugins/soleur/docs/pages/legal/individual-cla.md` — the mirror of `docs/legal/individual-cla.md`, which is the artefact `cla_doc.git_sha` binds and whose § 0 discloses both copies of the record, the US-established processor, the indefinite retention and the erasure route, and the required sign phrase ("I have read the CLA Document and I hereby sign the CLA") is an attestation of having read that document; the field-level detail, including that the verbatim sign-comment body is retained, sits in Privacy Policy § 4.5 ("the bucket contains a content-addressed record per signature (doc-hash, verbatim sign-comment body, PR-of-record, signed_at, capture_method)") and GDPR Policy § 3.4, both of which § 0 cross-references — § 0 names Privacy Policy §§ 4.5, 5.11 and 10 and GDPR Policy §§ 3.4 and 6, and does **not** cross-reference the DPD, so DPD § 2.3(n) carries the same disclosure but is not part of the notice chain relied on in this limb; (iv) no automated decision-making. **The receipt comment is not the informing mechanism and must not be treated as one.** The "Post receipt comment" step in `.github/workflows/cla-evidence.yml` carries `continue-on-error: true`, and its `if:` requires `github.event.action == 'created'` while the record-writing step also fires on `edited` and `deleted` — so on an edit or a delete a record is written and no receipt is posted at all, a structural absence rather than a soft failure. The `pull_request_target` bypass path posts no receipt ever. The receipt's text names the CLA document's git SHA and a retrieval command; it does not describe the record's fields. **The balancing test does not reach the non-signer capture.** Where the `actor` of an evidence record is a commenter who signed nothing (see the capture predicate at §(c)), the record evidences no grant, so limb (ii) fails on **necessity** and the analysis stops there, before balancing, in the manner of PA-32. No other Art. 6 basis is available for that fraction of the processing: there is no contract with such a commenter, no consent is sought, and no legal-obligation, vital-interests or public-task ground applies. Recorded as a finding against the implementation, not as a defect in the interest; the remediation is an engineering change and is not decided in this register. **Art. 9(2) gateways, where Art. 9 content arrives** (see Special categories): Art. 9(2)(e) for a comment manifestly made public by its author on a public pull request, subject to the two bounds recorded there; Art. 9(2)(f) — establishment, exercise or defence of legal claims — for the tombstone `override_reason`, which the data subject did not write and to which (2)(e) is therefore unavailable. Neither is an Art. 6 basis and neither is offered as one. **[2026-09-03 CORRECTION (#7625): limb (iii) previously read "signer is informed of the record at signing time", which named no mechanism. The mechanism is the pre-signature notice comment and § 0 of the CLA document, not the post-hoc receipt — the receipt is soft-failing and structurally absent on `edited`, `deleted` and the bypass path, and could not have carried the limb had it been the mechanism. The limb is restated on the mechanism that actually exists, and the balancing is split: it holds for signers and is unavailable for the non-signer capture recorded at §(c), where it fails on necessity. The corpus divergence this creates is recorded once, at the `(c) Categories of data subjects` cell, and is not restated here. Recorded rather than silently repaired, per this register's amendment-history convention.]** |
| **(d) Recipients** | GitHub Inc (the `cla-signatures` branch is hosted in the public GitHub repository — i.e. record is public-by-design). **Cloudflare Inc** (processor — object custody of the off-site evidence sidecar in the `soleur-cla-evidence` R2 bucket, introduced by #3201; same Cloudflare Customer DPA instrument as the edge-proxy role, see the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping). **NOT a recipient: FreeTSA.** The monthly RFC 3161 exchange transmits a SHA-256 digest of the bucket-state manifest and receives a signed token. The digest is taken over an aggregate manifest rather than over any individual's identifier, so it cannot be used to single out a data subject — the pre-image is a whole file, which distinguishes it from a hashed-identifier case where a candidate space can be enumerated. No signature record, identity or timestamp payload egresses to the TSA. The HTTPS submission necessarily discloses the GitHub-hosted runner's egress IP to FreeTSA; that address belongs to GitHub's ephemeral Actions fleet and to no data subject of this activity, so it does not create a recipient relationship either. Recorded explicitly so a future reader does not have to re-derive the exclusion. **[2026-08-19 CORRECTION (#7601): Cloudflare was ABSENT from this cell, and PA-7 was absent from Cloudflare's row in the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping, whose Activities column read "1, 2, 4, 5 (edge proxy)". The omission dated from #3201 and was surfaced by the CLO ruling on #7597, not by the outage itself. The processing was contractually covered throughout the omission period: Cloudflare has been engaged as a processor under the self-executing Customer DPA with DPF + SCCs + CBPR since before #3201 (verified 2026-03-19 — see `compliance-posture.md` Vendor DPA Status), and the same instrument covers the R2 role as the CDN role. What was incomplete was the Art. 30 record of a lawfully-safeguarded transfer, not the safeguard itself. An Art. 30(1) record-keeping incompleteness is not an Art. 4(12) personal-data breach and triggers no Art. 33/34 notification; this correction is the record-keeping discharge. Recorded as a correction rather than silently repaired, per this register's amendment-history convention.]** |
| **(e) Third-country transfers** | GitHub is US-based — transfers under GitHub's published SCCs / DPF mechanisms (see GitHub's Global Privacy Practices). **Cloudflare Inc (R2 object custody) — third country: the United States.** Safeguards: EU-US DPF + SCCs + CBPR, the same instrument as the edge-proxy activities. **The transfer arises from the identity of the importer, not from the location of the bytes:** making personal data available to Cloudflare, Inc., a US-established processor subject to US jurisdiction, is a Chapter V transfer irrespective of where the objects rest (EDPB Guidelines 05/2021, criterion 3). **The bucket is additionally NOT jurisdiction-restricted, and this cell must not record data localisation as a safeguard.** `cloudflare_r2_bucket.cla_evidence` sets `location = "WEUR"`, which is a **placement hint, not a jurisdiction** — the bucket sits on Cloudflare's `default` (standard, non-EU-tier) jurisdiction, as recorded at `apps/cla-evidence/infra/iam.tf` and encoded in both token resource-strings as `…<account>_default_<bucket>`. The safeguard for this transfer is therefore the DPF/SCC mechanism alone. **Re-evaluation trigger:** migrating the bucket to Cloudflare's EU jurisdiction tier would change the data-residency position and the token resource-strings in `iam.tf`. It would **not**, on its own, convert this cell to a no-transfer posture — that would additionally require the contracting entity and the access path to cease being US-established. **[2026-08-19 CORPUS DIVERGENCE (#7601): `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md` (§5.11 and the storage-location bullet), `docs/legal/data-protection-disclosure.md` (§2.3(n), §4.2 table, §6.4) and `docs/legal/gdpr-policy.md` (§3.3 processor bullet, §3.4) currently represent this archive as EU-region and state that it introduces no third-country transfer. Those statements are superseded by this cell and are tracked for correction at #7624. No tenant DPA had been executed under that template at the date of correction (`tenant-dpa-register.md` Rows table empty), so the correction carries no amendment tail; the Schedule 2 Cloudflare R2 row in the same template also mis-attributed the Supabase Storage bucket `chat-attachments/*` to Cloudflare R2, and that attribution is corrected to Supabase. **The preceding CORPUS DIVERGENCE block is preserved unaltered as the audit trail of the divergence period and must not be read as a description of the corpus as it now stands.** **Not discharged, and deliberately carried forward:** the Art. 6(1)(f) balancing test at `gdpr-policy.md` §3.4 was **re-derived** on the corrected facts rather than patched, because two of its load-bearing premises (an EU bucket jurisdiction, and a ten-year retention ceiling) were false and its proportionality conclusion did not survive their correction. Its proportionality limb now records retention as indefinite and withdraws the limitation-period calibration as a proof of proportionality. **Whether a retention ceiling should be adopted is a controller decision about the processing itself, not a documentation question, and is tracked at #7668.** §(c) field omissions remain tracked at #7625 and are untouched by this correction.]**  **[2026-08-20 OPEN QUESTION (#7670): this cell states importer-identity as the governing Chapter V test. That divergence PRE-DATES this correction — the importer-identity sentence was already in this cell on 2026-08-19 — but propagating the rule to PA-12 §(e) and to the published corpus makes it conspicuous, so it is recorded rather than left for the next reader to re-derive. Whether the two cases genuinely differ (a Supabase EU contracting entity would distinguish them) or the Supabase posture is wrong is a controller determination, tracked at #7670. **Until that resolves, this cell governs PA-7 only and must not be read as having decided the Supabase question either way.**]** |
| **(f) Retention** | **Indefinite**. License grants under the CLA are irrevocable and survive any withdrawal of the signature record. Art. 17 erasure of the record will be honoured on request, but grants made under the CLA before deletion continue in full effect (this carve-out is stated in the CLA itself — see `docs/legal/individual-cla.md` and `docs/legal/corporate-cla.md`). |
| **(g) TOMs** | **Two layers.** (1) **Canonical, public-by-design:** append-only record on a dedicated git branch; signature integrity protected by git's content-addressed commit hashes; access control over the write path via GitHub branch protections. (2) **Off-site evidence sidecar (R2, introduced by #3201):** the `soleur-cla-evidence` bucket carries a Cloudflare **R2 Lock Rule** (`cla-evidence-10yr-retention`, Age condition, `maxAgeSeconds = 315360000` — a 10-year retention floor) applied bucket-wide via `prefix: ""`, so the floor covers `signatures/`, `allowlist/`, `tombstones/` and `timestamps/` alike; the apply-time assertion in `apps/cla-evidence/infra/object_lock.tf` re-reads the returned rule list and fails unless at least one Age rule meets the floor, so a malformed PUT cannot silently void the WORM property. **Conditional PUT (`If-None-Match: *`) on every evidence write:** both the content-addressed `signatures/<sha256-of-payload>.json` key and the per-quarter `allowlist/<principal>/<quarter>.json` key go through `apps/cla-evidence/scripts/r2-conditional-put.sh`, so a re-run of either path is a first-writer-wins no-op (`412`, or `409`/`403` carrying `<Code>ObjectLockedByBucketPolicy</Code>` once the Lock Rule is the enforcing layer) rather than a rewrite — this is what makes the record tamper-evident, and it is the property the #7597 exposure measurement turned on. The monthly `timestamps/<yyyy-mm>/` objects are written unconditionally and are sealed by the Lock Rule alone. **RFC 3161 trusted timestamping** on a monthly cadence (`.github/workflows/cla-evidence-timestamp.yml`): the timestamped pre-image is a manifest of `{key, etag, size, last_modified}` over every object under `signatures/`, `allowlist/` and `tombstones/` — not the archive bytes — but because each signature key *is* the SHA-256 of its own record, timestamping the manifest binds the content of every signature record. The run verifies the returned token with `openssl ts -verify` against the bundled FreeTSA chain and fails unless it reports `Verification: OK`, and a **≥180-day CA/TSA certificate-expiry preflight** fails the run rather than timestamping under an expiring chain. **Least-privilege object-write token** scoped to this single bucket (R2 tokens do not support prefix-level scoping — bucket is the finest available grain); `prevent_destroy` on the bucket in Terraform. **No IP-allowlist** — a deliberate decision recorded at `apps/cla-evidence/infra/iam.tf`, on the basis that the bucket holds already-public GitHub identities. **Art. 17 interaction (reconciles with §(f)): the WORM floor is not absolute.** `apps/cla-evidence/scripts/gdpr-override.sh` is the controlled admin-override path for a verified erasure request where the Art. 17(3)(e) carve-out does not apply: a one-hour admin token is verified, the live Lock Rule list is snapshotted, a temporarily-relaxed list is PUT, the object is deleted using the S3-compat HMAC credentials (never the bearer admin token), the byte-equal snapshot is restored, restoration is re-verified against the live bucket, a tombstone is written, and the admin token self-revokes; a failed restore exits on a distinct code that writes an incident sentinel rather than exiting clean. The override path is itself a TOM, and it is what makes §(f)'s erasure commitment operable under a 10-year floor. |
| **(h) DSAR (Art. 15 / 20)** | **Reachable, unlike PA-32 and PA-33 — and the difference is recorded rather than left to be assumed.** `DSAR_TABLE_ALLOWLIST` (`apps/web-platform/server/dsar-export-allowlist.ts`) enumerates **database tables** and reaches none of this activity's four record shapes, none of which is a table. But an **enumeration path exists** here where none exists over a git history or an issue body: `apps/cla-evidence/scripts/inspect-evidence.sh by-contributor <login>` filters `signatures/` records on `.actor.login`, so it reaches a non-signer commenter as readily as a signer; `by-pr` and `by-quarter` cover the other axes; shape (1) is a single public JSON file and is trivially enumerable; and shape (3) is keyed by sanitised principal (`allowlist/<sanitised-principal>/<quarter>.json`), so it is enumerable by construction. **The honest Art. 15 route today** is a manual request to <legal@jikigai.com>, answered by running `inspect-evidence.sh by-contributor` against the archive and reading the public branch — an operator-only path requiring Doppler `prd_cla` credentials, with **no self-serve surface at `/dashboard/settings/privacy`**, because the subjects of this activity hold no Web Platform account — with the single present exception of the operator, who is a data subject of this activity under limbs (ii) and (iii) of §(c) and does hold one. **One shape is not enumerable by its own subject:** the erasure tombstone (shape (4)) is keyed by `prior_object_sha` and by no subject identifier, so a person named in a tombstone's `gdpr_request_ref` cannot be found by searching for themselves — `inspect-evidence.sh tombstone <object-sha>` presupposes knowing the sha. **Art. 20 portability does not apply** to any shape: the lawful basis is Art. 6(1)(f), and Art. 20 is available only for processing grounded on Art. 6(1)(a) consent or Art. 6(1)(b) contract; that a signer authored the comment text does not change that. **Art. 21(1) right to object — mandatory for Art. 6(1)(f) processing, and currently absent for the involuntary population.** `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md` § 8.1 carries named carve-outs for community-digest subjects, departed workspace members, LinkedIn-published content and operator-assisted sessions, and carries **no carve-out reaching this archive** — the nearest, the community-digest carve-out, is scoped by its own terms to handles and words appearing in the automated digests at Privacy Policy § 4.4 and does not reach the CLA evidence archive — so a non-signer commenter — who holds no account with Jikigai — is offered no route anywhere in the published corpus, and neither would an allowlisted natural person who had not signed. Same shape as the gap PA-31, PA-32 and PA-33 each record at **DEF-9** (#7126); tracked for the corpus at #7812. **Art. 17 interaction:** the erasure route is `apps/cla-evidence/scripts/gdpr-override.sh` per §(f) and §(g). Note that honouring an erasure **creates** shape (4), which the §(g) floor then seals — an Art. 17 request against this activity cannot be fully unwound. |

## Processing Activity 8 — Operational Telemetry & Breach-Detection Logs

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | (i) Detect, investigate, and respond to security incidents; (ii) anchor the **Art. 33 72-hour breach-notification clock** with auditable first-observed-at timestamps; (iii) operate the Soleur cc-dispatcher's `mirrorP0Deduped` cross-tenant + W4-orphan P0 mirror pipeline that flags severity-P0 anomalies for the on-call CLO/CTO rotation; (iv) general application error monitoring; (v) operate the secret-leak audit pipelines on infrastructure plumbing (Doppler, drift-guard); (vi) **Off-host long-tail operational log aggregation (Better Stack Logs, post-PR #4279)** for diagnostic recall beyond the 30 MB Hetzner Docker json-file buffer in §(f); enables operator query of journald lines older than ~24h without SSH per `hr-no-ssh-fallback-in-runbooks`. The Better Stack plane is decoupled from (ii) — Sentry remains the canonical Art. 33 first-observed-at anchor (see I-network learning `2026-05-19-sentry-url-routing-three-orthogonal-dimensions.md`); a multi-provider clock-anchor split decision is deferred to the 60-day re-decision in #4296. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | End-users of the Web Platform (incidental presence in error context, traces, and P0 mirror anchors). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | (i) **Sentry:** error messages and stack traces — may incidentally include `user_id`, request paths, request headers. User identifiers passed through the centralised silent-fallback helpers (`reportSilentFallback`, `warnSilentFallback`, `mirrorP0Deduped` in `apps/web-platform/server/observability.ts`) are HMAC-SHA256-pseudonymized at the emit boundary (Recital 26 — controller cannot re-identify from the hash without the server-side pepper held in Doppler, not shared with the processor). Server-side direct `Sentry.captureException` / `Sentry.captureMessage` payloads are sanitised through the `apps/web-platform/server/sentry-scrub.ts:scrubRecursive` boundary which renames top-level and nested `userId`/`user_id` keys to `userIdHash` via the shared `hashUserIdValue` primitive (ADR-029 §I4). Authenticated request handlers bind `Sentry.getCurrentScope().setUser({id: hashUserIdValue(user.id)})` at the per-user-rate-limit HOC (`apps/web-platform/server/with-user-rate-limit.ts`) and inline at each of the eleven helper-migration sites (canonical inventory: header comment block in `apps/web-platform/server/sentry-scrub.ts`), each wrapped in `Sentry.withIsolationScope(...)` so the binding cannot bleed across requests under the custom-server boot path. On the client side, `apps/web-platform/lib/client-observability.ts` (the `"use client"`-importable mirror of the server helper) **strips** `userId` / `user_id` / `email` keys from `extra` at the helper boundary before calling Sentry — the browser bundle cannot carry the server pepper, so structural strip + Sentry `beforeSend` backstop (`apps/web-platform/sentry.client.config.ts` `stripUserContextFromEvent` over `event.user`, `event.extra`, `event.contexts`, `event.breadcrumbs[*].data`) replace pseudonymization on this surface. A `piiStripped` sentinel marks events where a regression was caught. The cross-tenant DSAR-export breach-detection path (`mirrorCrossTenantViolation` in `apps/web-platform/server/observability.ts`) emits SHA-256-pseudonymous identifiers (`offendingUserIdHash` + `expectedUserIdHash`, salt-keyed via `SOLEUR_SENTRY_PII_SALT`) for breach anchoring under Art. 33; the field names are intentionally domain-distinct from the HMAC-pseudonymous `userIdHash` emitted on the pino/helper path. (ii) **pino stdout (Hetzner-resident, EU-only):** structured app logs — `conversation_id`, request metadata, and a `userId` field. From the deployment timestamp of #3698 forward, the pino `formatters.log()` rename hook in `apps/web-platform/server/logger.ts` renames top-level `userId` / `user_id` keys to `userIdHash` on every `logger.{error,warn,info,debug}` emission across `apps/web-platform/server/**` and `apps/web-platform/app/**` (HMAC-SHA256 with a server-side pepper held in Doppler and not shared with the processor — Recital 26 pseudonymisation, retained as pseudonym to support breach-investigation linkage under (b)(ii)). Nested keys (e.g. `extra.userId`, properties attached to Error instances) are not transformed by design — the boundary contract documented in `ADR-029` (rename-at-boundary) constrains the renamer to top-level keys, and the CI gate at `.github/workflows/pr-quality-guards.yml#userid-bypass-lint` blocks new direct-emission sites that would bypass it. Under the documented fail-safe path (rename-helper exception inside `formatters.log`), the log line is preserved with raw `userId` to maintain incident-response context, surfaced once per worker via an operator `console.warn` signal; runtime degradation is operator-detectable rather than silent. Pre-deployment historical lines remain on the Hetzner host until they age out per the retention window in (f). **From PR #4279 onward, Vector reads journald (which mirrors pino stdout) and ships journald + host_metrics to Better Stack Logs as a separate processor under §(d). The VRL `pii_scrub_drop_userdata` + `pii_scrub_structured` + `pii_scrub_string` transforms in `apps/web-platform/infra/vector.toml` provide defense-in-depth Art-9 user-content drop + `userId` → `userIdHash` rename + string-level scrub before egress (boundary contract: ADR-029 §I4 single-pepper reuse — the VRL transform imports `SENTRY_USERID_PEPPER` via `get_env_var()` and computes `encode_base16(hmac(..., "SHA-256"))` bit-for-bit equivalent to the TS `hashUserId` primitive used on the Sentry plane).** Sentry-event payloads from the helper boundary (`reportSilentFallback` / `warnSilentFallback` / `mirrorP0Deduped`) are pseudonymized as in (i); symmetric direct-capture coverage is enforced at the `apps/web-platform/server/sentry-scrub.ts:scrubRecursive` boundary which renames `userId`/`user_id` keys (case-insensitive) to `userIdHash` regardless of nesting depth, complementing the pino `formatters.log` top-level boundary. (iii) **cc-dispatcher P0 mirrors (`mirrorP0Deduped` cross-tenant + W4-orphan paths) and `reportSilentFallback`/`warnSilentFallback`:** `userIdHash` (pseudonymous identifier), `conversationId`, `first_observed_at` ISO timestamp — minimised to identifiers + clock anchor. (iv) **Sentry monitor classes processed (post-2026 split):** issue alerts (the (i) channel above) and **cron monitor check-ins** — vendor-hosted heartbeat for the 9 scoped scheduled GitHub Actions workflows (slug, status `in_progress\|ok\|error`, ISO timestamp; no pseudonymous identifier — workflow checks are job-scoped, not user-scoped — operational metadata, not Art. 4 personal data). **Sentry log ingestion (Logs product) is NOT enabled and no application log content is forwarded to Sentry**; if a future change introduces a Sentry log channel, this cell, DPD §2.3(m), Privacy Policy §5.10, GDPR Policy operational-telemetry bullet, and `apps/web-platform/server/sentry-scrub.ts` must be extended in advance. Migration audit evidence (Article 30 §5(2) accountability): `knowledge-base/legal/audits/sentry-migration-audit-<YYYY-MM-DD>.md`, regenerated at each `reusable-release.yml` run. **[2026-08-12 UPDATE (#7440 / ADR-184): the Vector framing above is an incomplete description of this Activity.** Not every path to Better Stack Logs source 2457081 traverses Vector. `soleur-registry` POSTs directly by `curl`: the pre-existing `SOLEUR_ZOT_DISK` and `SOLEUR_PRIVATE_NIC` reporters, and from #7440 the zot container-log shipper. The `pii_scrub_*` VRL transforms named above do NOT run on those paths and no `userIdHash` pseudonymisation occurs on them — **the Recital 26 basis stated in this cell is a Sentry/Vector-plane property and must not be read as covering the registry plane.** Categories on the registry plane: OCI image references and digests, HTTP method / path / status / latency / byte-count, `User-Agent`, and `clientIP`. **`clientIP` is shipped unredacted** (zot emits it as a top-level field; the emitter's redaction covers header objects, not top-level scalars). It is not Art. 4(1) personal data on the current topology: `hcloud_firewall.registry` carries zero inbound rules, so ingress is intra-`10.0.1.0/24` plus a Cloudflare tunnel whose origin is `tcp://10.0.1.30:5000` — every observable `clientIP` is an RFC1918 address of a host in the controller's own estate, identifying a machine and not a natural person. **That conclusion is TOPOLOGY-DEPENDENT.** Any inbound rule admitting public ingress to zot, or any configuration that lands a forwarded public address in a top-level zot field, converts `clientIP` into personal data on a path carrying no redaction, and requires this cell, §(g) and the emitter to be revisited. `X-Forwarded-For` is not in the emitter's header allowlist and is therefore redacted, which bounds — but does not close — the proxy case. Recorded as a re-evaluation trigger.**] **[2026-08-13 UPDATE (Ref #7455): the `clientIP` conclusion above is RE-ATTESTED on live traffic, not inherited.** The Art. 4(1) analysis in the block above was reasoned on 2026-08-12 against a channel carrying nothing. The channel went live 2026-08-12T20:54:12Z (see §(d)), so the conclusion now governs actual rows and is re-verified here against the topology as it stands after the host replace. **Both load-bearing invariants survive the replace and are re-verified in the IaC that the replace applied:** `hcloud_firewall.registry` in `apps/web-platform/infra/zot-registry.tf` still declares a firewall carrying a name and labels and NO `rule` block of any kind, so the public interface remains deny-all on every port; and `local.registry_private_ip` is still pinned to `10.0.1.30` and single-sourced, with the Cloudflare tunnel `ingress_rule` in `apps/web-platform/infra/tunnel.tf` still originating at `tcp://10.0.1.30:5000`, so a tunnelled pull continues to present the connector's private address to zot rather than the initiator's public one. Ingress therefore remains intra-`10.0.1.0/24` and every `clientIP` zot can observe remains an RFC1918 address of a host in the controller's own estate. **The conclusion is unchanged: `clientIP` on this plane identifies a machine and is not Art. 4(1) personal data.** **What this re-attestation does NOT cover, stated rather than implied:** it verifies the topology that determines which values are observable; it is not an observed-value audit of the rows actually shipped since 21:03:51Z. The topology-dependence recorded above is undisturbed and remains the governing re-evaluation trigger. Converting that dependence from a documented condition into a continuously-enforced invariant — an assertion on the readback path that every `clientIP` in the observed window is RFC1918, so a topology drift fails a probe instead of silently changing the category of a field already in flight — is an engineering decision referred to the CTO and tracked separately; this cell's basis does not wait on it. Attested at `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-08-counsel-review-7440.md` §11.**] |
| **Special categories** | None systematically. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(f) — legitimate interest in service security and integrity, balanced against the data-subject interest in confidentiality (mitigated by tight access scoping and short retention); Art. 6(1)(c) — legal obligation for the **Art. 33 (breach notification)** clock-anchor evidence captured by `first_observed_at`. |
| **(d) Recipients** | **Current recipients:** Sentry (processor — DE region, **Functional Software GmbH**, org slug `jikigai-eu`, ingest cluster `de.sentry.io`); Hetzner (processor — captures stdout); the on-call Jikigai engineering/CLO rotation (internal). **[2026-05-19 UPDATE: Prior entry of 2026-05-17 disclosing a "Recipient-drift after-the-fact" event (phantom-ingest to a notionally unowned third-party Sentry organization, org ID `4511123328466944`, window 2026-03-28 → 2026-05-16) is SUPERSEDED. | **(f) Retention** | Sentry: 90 days default (rolling). pino stdout: **json-file Docker driver, 30 MB rolling per container (max-size=10m × max-file=3)** pinned in `apps/web-platform/infra/cloud-init.yml:303-310`. Observed daily log volume: __TBD_OBSERVED_VOLUME__ (post-merge operator measurement; see runbook `knowledge-base/engineering/operations/runbooks/recover-userid-from-pino-stdout.md`). **Re-verification triggers:** annual review; on any change to `apps/web-platform/infra/cloud-init.yml` (daemon.json block); on any change to off-host log shipper (`apps/web-platform/infra/vector.toml`); on container restart policy change affecting log path. **Better Stack Logs (off-host copy, post-PR #4279): paid-tier default retention `__TBD_BETTERSTACK_RETENTION__` days for journald + host_metrics (operator measurement at FR1 amendment time; mirrors the `__TBD_OBSERVED_VOLUME__` pattern). Logs lines containing `userIdHash` (HMAC-SHA256, Doppler-held pepper) are pseudonymous under GDPR Recital 26; on user-account deletion, the hash is allowed to age out per processor retention — no active processor-side erasure call is required (same Art. 17 treatment as the Sentry plane).** P0 mirror records: retained for the **statutory 72-hour clock window + investigation-completion buffer**; documented retention to be tightened by counsel review (target ≤ 12 months unless an open investigation extends). **Note on Art. 17 (erasure):** the cc-dispatcher mirror and silent-fallback emissions carry `userIdHash` (pseudonymous under GDPR Recital 26). Sentry and Hetzner cannot re-identify the data subject without the Doppler-held server pepper. On user-account deletion, the hashed identifier is allowed to age out per processor retention; no active processor-side erasure call is required. |
| **(g) TOMs** | Access restricted to on-call rotation + CLO; Sentry user-context scrubbing rules for known PII fields; secrets and tokens redacted before logging; pino structured-log redaction filters; P0 mirror payloads carry **only** identifiers + first-observed-at clock anchor (no message bodies); audit trail of who accessed P0 mirrors is itself logged. **VRL `pii_scrub_drop_userdata` + `pii_scrub_structured` + `pii_scrub_string` transforms in `apps/web-platform/infra/vector.toml` (boundary control before Better Stack Logs egress — Art-9 user-content key drop, `userId` → `userIdHash` HMAC-SHA256 rename reusing `SENTRY_USERID_PEPPER` per ADR-029 §I4, regex backstop for non-pino kernel-oops lines with `/api/auth/callback/...` carve-out per UI-F1); CI gate `validate-vector-config` in `.github/workflows/apply-web-platform-infra.yml` enforces `vector validate` (schema) + `vector vrl` fixture tests (transform-body — BLOCKING) on every `vector.toml` or fixture change; bit-for-bit parity with the TS `hashUserId` primitive asserted by AC4 in `apps/web-platform/test/infra/vector-pii-scrub.test.sh`.** **Inline agent-facing read path (#5495, 2026-06-17 — §5(2) accountability):** `scripts/sentry-issue.sh` reads a Sentry issue/event by id for no-SSH debugging under a dedicated **read-only, least-privilege** token `SENTRY_ISSUE_RO_TOKEN` (scopes `[event:read, org:read]`; Doppler `soleur/prd`; minted as the `inline-read-prd` Internal Integration whose creation logs `sentry-app.add` to the Org Audit Log). GET-only (URL allowlist + issue-id charset validation; never the write-scoped token as steady state). Access posture asymmetry recorded: this read surfaces Sentry event **message/breadcrumb/tag/`user.*` values** that the ingest-time key-name scrub (`sentry-scrub.ts`) does NOT remove — unlike the Better Stack plane (Vector 3-stage `pii_scrub`), so the CLI/runbook carry an operator-hygiene caveat + optional `--redact`; the transfer into agent/operator context is covered by the existing Anthropic DPA. Runbook `knowledge-base/engineering/operations/runbooks/sentry-issue-read.md`. **[2026-07-27 UPDATE (#6982): redaction at the git-data emit boundary.** The shared Vector `pii_scrub_string` transform does **NOT** scrub a bare UUID in free text — it handles `userid=<token>` pairs, emails, bearer tokens and DSNs. On the git-data host that gap is load-bearing, because the repo identifier **is** the user identifier (repos are named `<workspace_id>.git` and `workspace_id === user_id` per the mig-053 N2 invariant), so an un-scrubbed repo path would ship a raw `auth.users.id` and defeat this Activity's Recital 26 pseudonymity basis. Mitigated **structurally rather than by convention**: every VARIABLE-CONTENT path on git-data goes through ONE emitter (`/usr/local/bin/git-data-emit`), which applies a bare-UUID rule and a repo-path rule internally on every path, **before** the `tail -c 180` cap (the cap is byte-wise, and a truncated-but-still-identifying prefix survives a literal grep). A value-based arm additionally substitutes the LUKS passphrase, which no pattern could match. The maintenance unit emits **aggregate counts only** — never a per-repo row. Enforced by behavioural tests (`apps/web-platform/infra/git-data-emit.test.sh`) that assert against the bytes leaving the process, each carrying a mutation arm proving the assertion can fail.**] **[2026-08-12 UPDATE (#7440 / ADR-184): redaction at the zot-log emit boundary, with no Vector in the path.** Because `soleur-registry` runs no Vector agent, the shared `pii_scrub_*` VRL transforms are structurally unavailable to this emitter and its own `redact()` is the whole boundary **for this emitter**. It is a **name ALLOWLIST over the header object, applied at any depth** (`jq walk`, container key compared case-folded), so a header this codebase has not anticipated is redacted by construction rather than by having predicted its name — the failure mode of a name-anchored denylist is silent leakage, the failure mode of an allowlist is an over-redacted diagnostic, and only one of those is recoverable. It **fails closed**: a JSON row that cannot be structurally redacted is dropped and accounted (`reason=redact_failed`) rather than shipped, and a non-JSON row whose credential-bearing header still carries a residual is refused. Measured against the pinned zot image, zot masks `Authorization` itself; this is defence in depth against a future zot that stops. Scope, stated rather than implied: the boundary covers the header object and the `Authorization`-shaped renderings a plaintext line can take — it is not a general free-text PII scrubber, and the Activity's basis for that is that zot's identity model carries no email or `user_id` (its accounts are the `zot-pull`/`zot-push` service principals). Enforced by behavioural tests asserting against the bytes leaving the process, each block paired with a non-vacuity control proving the assertion is not satisfied by a dropped or empty row (`apps/web-platform/infra/zot-log-shipper.test.sh` §T6/§T6b; 150/150 at attestation, with a harness canary and a 150-assertion floor). Validated additionally at development time against an 11-mutant battery (all RED against a GREEN baseline). Unlike `git-data-emit.test.sh` in the #6982 entry above, that battery is **not** resident in the suite. **It is NOT the whole boundary for the host, and that is recorded rather than left to inference.** The pre-existing `SOLEUR_ZOT_DISK` reporter POSTs to this same source and carries `zot_last_err`, a ≤300-character sample of zot's own log output (tiered: `panic:`/`fatal error`/signal/`runtime error`, then `level:error\|fatal`, then a `cannot\|failed to\|unable to` sweep, then a `docker logs --tail 3` fallback). That sample passes through the payload-integrity sanitizer only and through no `redact()` at all. On the fallback tier it is routinely an `info`-level `HTTP API` row carrying the `headers` object; zot self-masks `Authorization` on the pinned image but does not mask `Cookie`, `X-Api-Key` or arbitrary headers. Recorded as a known, bounded residual on a host that takes no public ingress; remediation tracked at #7500.**] |

## Processing Activity 9 — cc-soleur-go Router In-Process MCP Tool Surface (Phase 1 status: not exposed)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Recorded in advance per #2909 V2-13 Phase 1 (deny-by-default scaffolding). Phase 1 ships zero tool promotions; the legacy `agent-runner.ts startAgentSession` path retains the in-process `soleur_platform` MCP server, while the cc-soleur-go router (`cc-dispatcher.ts realSdkQueryFactory`) passes `mcpServers: readCcMcpAllowlist()` returning `{}` by default. Phase 2 (#3722, blocked-by Stage 6 #2939) tracks the actual promotion of 8 read-only Tier 1 candidates. Plausible tools (3) are PERMANENTLY blocked from the cc-router via `CC_ROUTER_TIER3_DENYLIST` (shared service-token cross-tenant credential by construction). |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | End-users of the Web Platform (when Phase 2 promotion occurs). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | **Phase 1: none — no tool surfaces personal data via cc-router.** Phase 2 candidates (per-tool, deferred to #3722 plan): **kb_share family** (user-authored KB entries, share tokens, share metadata); **conversations_lookup** (cross-conversation aggregation by `user_id` — closure-bound to requesting user); **GitHub tools (9)** — operator GitHub App installation tokens, issue bodies, commit author emails, repo metadata; **Plausible (3)** — PERMANENTLY blocked from cc-router. |
| **Special categories** | None systematically. |
| **Lawful basis** | Phase 1: N/A (nothing processed). Phase 2 candidates (per-family, deferred): kb_share + conversations_lookup → Art. 6(1)(b) contract; GitHub tools → Art. 6(1)(f) legitimate interest with balancing test documented at #3722 plan time. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Phase 1: none. Phase 2 recipients (deferred): GitHub Inc (US sub-processor); Plausible Insights OÜ (EU sub-processor — but PERMANENTLY blocked from cc-router, listed for legacy-path completeness). |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | Phase 1: none. Phase 2 (deferred): GitHub → DPF + SCCs Module 2 (US); Plausible → N/A if plausible.io EU-hosted, SCCs if self-hosted on US infra. |
| **(f) Retention** | Phase 1: N/A. Phase 2: tied to underlying stores (`conversations`, `messages`, `kb_shares` — covered by retention policies of Processing Activities 1, 2, 4) and to third-party processor lifecycles (GitHub 60-day audit log; Plausible per their published policy). |
| **(g) TOMs** | Phase 1: deny-by-default in code via `readCcMcpAllowlist()` (returns `{}` for empty env, throws on `CC_ROUTER_TIER3_DENYLIST` short-names); FR2 Sentry mirror at SDK iterator hook (`feature: "cc-mcp-tier"`) so unregistered-tool invocations are observable. Phase 2: per-tool TOMs documented at promotion time. |

## Processing Activity 10 — Tenant deploy substrate (orchestration-plane meta-audit log)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Orchestrate deploys on behalf of Soleur-operators authoring projects via Soleur agents; record evidence of Art. 28(3) sub-processor instructions (per-event attribution of "Soleur agent triggered deploy for founder X on tenant repo Y at time T"); evidence Art. 32 TOMs (RLS zero-policies, WORM trigger, retention sweep) per the orchestration plane's audit-log substrate. v1 single-tenant (Soleur-as-tenant-zero); multi-tenant trajectory captured in ADR-030. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Founder-operators (Soleur users acting as tenant operators). NOT end-users of tenant apps (their data does **not** flow through this substrate at v1). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | `founder_id` (UUID, pseudonymous controller-side identifier — FK `auth.users(id)` with `ON DELETE RESTRICT`); `target_repo` (text, charset+length-CHECK-constrained — Art. 5(1)(c) minimisation + log-injection defense); `target_workflow` (same); `gh_run_id` (bigint, non-PII GitHub workflow run identifier); `oidc_jti` (text, length-bounded per RFC 7519 §4.1.7 — non-PII GitHub OIDC JWT identifier); `trigger_outcome` (4 enum values: `queued` / `succeeded` / `failed` / `timeout`); `event_at` + `retention_until` (timestamps). No request bodies. No workflow payloads. No IP addresses. No end-user data. |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(f) — legitimate interest. Three-part test documented at `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-05-14-tenant-deploy-substrate-lia.md`: (i) **purpose** = operate the multi-tenant deploy substrate's orchestration plane + evidence Art. 28(3) sub-processor instructions; (ii) **necessity** = no less intrusive alternative (anonymous logs cannot evidence Art. 28(3)(a) at per-event granularity); (iii) **balancing** = data subject is the actor triggering the recorded event, data is low-sensitivity operational metadata, reasonable expectation of a record is overwhelming for any CI/CD platform. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Soleur internal (service_role only — REVOKE from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated + GRANT EXECUTE only to service_role on the writer RPC). **No third-party recipients at v1.** Sub-processors providing the trigger surface itself (GitHub, Doppler, Hetzner, Cloudflare) are recorded in their existing Processing Activity rows; they do not receive the contents of `public.tenant_deploy_audit`. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **No transfer.** Data resides in Supabase eu-west-1 (Ireland). No third-country transfer for the meta-audit-log substrate. |
| **(f) Retention** | **12 months** via the `tenant-deploy-audit-retention` pg_cron sweep (Art. 5(1)(e) storage limitation). Inline migration comment `-- RETENTION: 12 months via tenant-deploy-audit-retention pg_cron`. Aligned with Processing Activity 8 P0 mirror precedent (≤12mo absent open investigation). Art. 17 cascade `anonymise_tenant_deploy_audit(p_founder_id uuid)` UPDATEs `founder_id = NULL` (preserves row count and audit-trail integrity; does not DELETE) — runs BEFORE `auth.users` deletion per `ON DELETE RESTRICT` FK ordering. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **RLS with zero policies** on `public.tenant_deploy_audit` — service_role-only access control. (2) **WORM via BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE triggers** raising `P0001` except during the Art. 17 anonymise GUC window (`app.tenant_deploy_anonymise_in_progress` + `current_user = 'service_role'`) — integrity. (3) **SECURITY DEFINER writer RPC** with `SET search_path = public, pg_temp` (public FIRST per `cq-pg-security-definer-search-path-pin-pg-temp`) — injection defense. (4) **`::add-mask::` on Doppler/OIDC secrets** in workflows — confidentiality at log layer. (5) **`tenant-deploy-audit-retention` pg_cron sweep** — Art. 5(1)(e) storage limitation. (6) **GitHub Environments + required reviewers** on tenant repos — organizational TOM (load-bearing per external research; caps `workflow_dispatch` blast radius). (7) **`hetznercloud/tps-action` short-lived tokens** + **Doppler Service Account Identity OIDC** + **scoped CF account-API tokens** — pseudonymisation/minimisation of long-lived secrets. (8) **CHECK constraints** on `target_repo` + `target_workflow` charset/length — Art. 5(1)(c) data minimisation. (9) **`ON DELETE RESTRICT` FK** forces Art. 17 anonymise to run before auth-user deletion. (10) **REVOKE-from-named-roles + GRANT-only-to-service_role** per `2026-05-06-supabase-default-privileges-defeat-revoke-from-public.md`. (11) **Two-claim OIDC subject binding** (`repository_owner:<tenant-org>` + `environment:production`) — least-privilege trust scope. |

## Processing Activity 11 — Consent Records (T&C Acceptance Audit Ledger)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | GDPR Art. 7(1) demonstrability: persist evidence that each Web Platform user accepted a specific version of the Terms & Conditions at a specific time, against a specific document content fingerprint. Without this ledger, re-acceptance of a new `TC_VERSION` would overwrite the prior `users.tc_accepted_at` / `users.tc_accepted_version` columns and destroy the prior-version evidence. Brand-survival single-user-incident class. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Web Platform users (registered account holders). NOT plugin-only users (the Plugin operates locally and does not create Web Platform consent records). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | `user_id` (UUID, pseudonymous controller-side identifier — FK `public.users(id)` with `ON DELETE RESTRICT`; nullable to support Art. 17 anonymisation); `version` (text, length-CHECK-constrained 1-32 — the `TC_VERSION` accepted); `document_sha` (text, 64-char hex CHECK — SHA-256 of `docs/legal/terms-and-conditions.md` at acceptance time; non-PII content fingerprint); `accepted_at` (timestamp); `ip_hash` (text, length-CHECK 1-128, NULLABLE — reserved for forward-compat per the LIA pending in #3855); `user_agent` (text, length-CHECK 1-512, NULLABLE — same forward-compat reservation); `retention_until` (timestamp). |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(b) — performance of contract (the user's acceptance of the T&C is itself the contract); Art. 7 — evidence of consent grant. Retention required for demonstrability is a regulatory obligation under Art. 7(1) ("the controller shall be able to demonstrate"). |
| **(d) Recipients** | Soleur internal (service_role only — REVOKE from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated + GRANT EXECUTE only to service_role on `accept_terms` and `anonymise_tc_acceptances` RPCs). **No third-party recipients.** No outward disclosure absent regulatory demand. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **No transfer.** Data resides in Supabase `eu-west-1` (Ireland). |
| **(f) Retention** | **7-year envelope** via `tc_acceptances.retention_until` column default `now() + interval '7 years'`. Art. 5(1)(e) storage limitation. Rationale: French civil-litigation general limitation is 5 years (Code civil Art. 2224), B2B commercial obligations and tax-adjacent records are 10 years (Code de commerce Art. L123-22) — 7 years sits within the GDPR-defensible middle. **v1 ships WITHOUT the `pg_cron` retention sweep** (0 beta users + 7-year window means no row qualifies for deletion before 2033). Sweep tracked as a deferred-scope-out follow-on. Art. 17 cascade `anonymise_tc_acceptances(p_user_id uuid)` UPDATEs `user_id = NULL` (preserves row count + audit-trail integrity; does not DELETE) — runs BEFORE `auth.users` deletion per `ON DELETE RESTRICT` FK ordering. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **RLS with zero policies** on `public.tc_acceptances` — service_role-only access control. (2) **WORM via BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE triggers** raising `P0001` except during the Art. 17 anonymise GUC window (`app.tc_acceptances_anonymise_in_progress` + `current_user = 'service_role'`) — integrity. (3) **SECURITY DEFINER RPCs** (`accept_terms`, `anonymise_tc_acceptances`) with `SET search_path = public, pg_temp` per `cq-pg-security-definer-search-path-pin-pg-temp`. (4) **`UNIQUE(user_id, version)` + `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`** RPC-side idempotency — prevents fabricated duplicate consent rows. (5) **Hand-edited `TC_DOCUMENT_SHA` literal + CI guardrail** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml :: tc-document-sha-guard`) — content-integrity gate that prevents accepting a stale document SHA after T&C edits without operator-attested bump policy review. (6) **CHECK constraints** on `document_sha ~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$'`, `version` length, `ip_hash` length, `user_agent` length — Art. 5(1)(c) data minimisation + input validation. (7) **`ON DELETE RESTRICT` FK** forces Art. 17 anonymise to run before auth-user deletion. (8) **REVOKE-from-named-roles + GRANT-only-to-service_role** per `2026-05-06-supabase-default-privileges-defeat-revoke-from-public.md`. (9) **Middleware fail-closed on tcError** — redirects non-exempt paths to `/accept-terms?error=db_unavailable` on Supabase outage, mirrored to Sentry (no silent admit). (10) **WebSocket mid-session re-check** of `users.tc_accepted_version` against `TC_VERSION` for gated inbound message types with 30s in-process cache — bounds stale-consent agent traffic to ≤ 30 s after operator-initiated bump. |

## Processing Activity 12 — GitHub branch-protection state custody (CI policy substrate)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Operate the CI-policy substrate that gates merges to `main` (required status checks + branch-protection bypass-actor allowlist) on `github.com/jikig-ai/soleur`. Provide an auditable IaC trail of every change to the branch-protection policy via the union of (i) Terraform state in R2 (`github/terraform.tfstate`), (ii) git commit history under `infra/github/`, and (iii) GitHub's first-party audit log of the API caller. Closes the failure mode surfaced by PR #3886 (a `lint fixture content` regression merged because the check was not in the ruleset's required set). |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Soleur engineers (operator personas) who hold the fine-grained PAT used by `terraform apply`. NO end-users of any Soleur surface are subjects of this activity. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | None of substance. The processed artifacts are policy metadata: required-status-check `context` strings (workflow job names — public ABI), `integration_id` numerics (GitHub App installation IDs), `bypass_actors` shape (`actor_type` + role-id), `strict_required_status_checks_policy` boolean. The GitHub PAT bearer is the sole identifier reachable from this root and is operator-internal (not user-facing). The PAT itself is Doppler-resident at rest and never written to state plaintext (provider marks `token` sensitive). |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(f) — legitimate interest (operate the CI substrate that gates merges). Inline LIA (no separate file under `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/`) — divergence from PA10's file-reference posture is intentional because there is no personal data to balance against, only operator-attributable apply events. (i) **purpose** = enforce the merge gate that prevents regression-class events like #3886 from landing on `main`; (ii) **necessity** = there is no less intrusive alternative — the GitHub Rulesets API is the only authoritative surface for required-check enforcement; (iii) **balancing** = the only data subject is the operator running `terraform apply`, the data is policy metadata, and the operator's expectation of an audit trail for branch-protection mutations is overwhelming for any IaC platform. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Soleur internal (operator only). GitHub holds the live ruleset state as a sub-processor of the `jikig-ai` GitHub org's IaC; the ruleset configuration itself is public via `gh api repos/jikig-ai/soleur/rulesets/14145388` (branch-protection rules are publicly inspectable on the repo's `/rules` page). No third-party recipients. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | GitHub Inc. (US) — sub-processor under the existing GitHub Enterprise SCC + DPA already recorded in the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping section below for PA-* entries that touch GitHub. The PAT itself is Doppler-resident (EU) until apply time and is transmitted to the GitHub REST API at apply (TLS in transit). R2 state custody is Cloudflare Inc (US) — the `soleur-terraform-state` bucket sits on the same `default` (non-EU-tier) jurisdiction as `soleur-cla-evidence`, as `apps/cla-evidence/infra/iam.tf` records in its token resource-string. **[2026-08-20 CORRECTION (#7624): this cell previously read "R2 state custody is Cloudflare-EU". That was the same location-implies-jurisdiction error corrected in PA-7 §(e) — the transfer arises from the importer's identity, not the bytes' location. §(c) for this activity records no personal data of substance, so no Chapter V consequence follows and this is a record-accuracy correction rather than an exposure. Recorded rather than silently repaired, per this register's amendment convention.]** |
| **(f) Retention** | Indefinite while the policy is in effect. The Terraform state file in R2 is the source-of-truth; prior versions are retained per R2 bucket versioning (rollback path documented in `infra/github/README.md` Phase 5). Rotation events for the PAT (90-day cadence) live in the Terraform commit history; no separate audit log. **Art. 5(1)(e) (storage limitation) compatibility:** indefinite retention is appropriate because no personal data is processed — the storage-limitation principle constrains *how long personal data can be kept*, and PA12 processes only policy metadata + operator-attributable apply events. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **Fine-grained PAT scoped to single repo** (`jikig-ai/soleur`) with `Administration: Read+Write` only — least privilege per OWASP minimum-privilege guideline. (2) **90-day PAT rotation cadence** documented in `infra/github/README.md` §Rotation with a +75-day calendar reminder. (3) **PAT Doppler-resident** (`prd_terraform/GH_RULESET_PAT`) — never committed to git or written to state plaintext (`integrations/github` provider marks `token` sensitive). (4) **R2 backend versioning + TLS** (ADR-006) — state-file confidentiality + rollback substrate. (5) **`terraform plan` as mandatory pre-apply gate** with the canonical `terraform show -json \| jq` diff probe (Phase 2.3 of the runbook) — operator-attested per `hr-menu-option-ack-not-prod-write-auth`. (6) **`integration_id` pinned in Terraform config** (15368 GitHub Actions / 57789 CodeQL) — drift of an integration_id at apply time is a signal of upstream provider change and halts the plan. (7) **Provider pinned to `~> 6.10`** per repo-local learning `2026-03-19-github-ruleset-stale-bypass-actors.md`; `.terraform.lock.hcl` committed (currently `6.12.1`). (8) **No CI auto-apply** — apply is operator-only via the runbook; the auto-apply patterns in `apply-deploy-pipeline-fix.yml` / `apply-sentry-infra.yml` do NOT apply here because the ruleset mutation is a write-to-prod-policy event requiring human attestation. |

## Processing Activity 13 — Autonomous-draft runtime (Inngest trigger + CFO function — PR-F #3244)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Operate the server-side autonomous-draft runtime that converts a Stripe `invoice.payment_failed` event into a CFO-domain customer-response draft surfaced on the founder's `/dashboard` Today section. Draft-only (no send) — the founder decides whether to send, edit, or discard each draft. PR-F (#3244) ships substrate + one trigger; PR-G (#3947) gates cohort exposure. Brand-survival threshold: `single-user incident` (CPO sign-off carried from `2026-05-17-pr-f-inngest-trigger-layer-brainstorm.md`). |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Founder-operators (Soleur users on a paid Stripe plan whose `invoice.payment_failed` fires the trigger). NOT end-users of tenant apps. Customer email addresses received from Stripe are hashed at the webhook boundary (sha256) BEFORE the Inngest envelope is signed — the customer is NOT a data subject of this activity. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | `founderId` (UUID, pseudonymous controller-side identifier — `public.users.id`); `invoiceId` (Stripe identifier, non-PII); `customerEmailHash` (sha256 hex, irreversible); `amount` (int, cents); `currency` (3-char ISO); `failureCode` (Stripe-defined enum, non-PII); plus the `messages` row written by the CFO function — `tier='external_brand_critical'`, `status='draft'`, `source='stripe'`, `owning_domain='cfo'`, `draft_preview` (model-generated text), `urgency`, `trust_tier='draft_one_click'`. Cost telemetry rows in `audit_byok_use` (`token_count`, `unit_cost_cents`, `ts`) are written by the per-turn cap-check (lands fully wired in PR-G). |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None. Stripe customer email is hashed before persistence to either the Inngest store or the messages table; no cleartext email enters this processing path. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(b) — performance of contract (the founder's paid subscription includes the autonomous-draft runtime as a feature of the contract); Art. 6(1)(f) — legitimate interest for the cost-telemetry (`audit_byok_use`) writes that demonstrate per-tenant BYOK cap enforcement. Three-part test for the LI prong: (i) **purpose** = operate the per-tenant kill-switch that bounds Anthropic API spend per founder per hour; (ii) **necessity** = no less intrusive alternative (anonymous cost logs cannot evidence per-tenant cap enforcement at audit time); (iii) **balancing** = data subject is the founder, data is operational metadata + their own API spend, reasonable expectation of an audit trail is overwhelming for any BYOK-billed substrate. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Soleur internal (service_role for the CFO function's tenant writes through user-scoped JWT; tenant-client RLS for `/api/dashboard/today` reads). **No third-party recipients.** Anthropic is the recipient of the BYOK prompt + completion API calls under the founder's own API key — the founder controls that bilateral relationship via BYOK; Soleur does not transmit founder data to Anthropic outside of the founder's lease scope. **Self-hosted Inngest** server eliminates the sub-processor cycle that Inngest Cloud would have created (rationale in ADR-030 §Decision). |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **No transfer.** Inngest substrate is self-hosted on Hetzner (eu-central; existing Hetzner row in the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping). Self-hosted binary persists state in SQLite on the same Hetzner host; bound to `127.0.0.1` (no public attack surface). Supabase eu-west-1 (Ireland) for `messages` + `audit_byok_use`. Stripe webhook payload (US) is already covered by Processing Activity 3 (Subscription & Billing); the bridge to Inngest stays within EU residency. |
| **(f) Retention** | `messages` draft rows: founder controls — Send/Edit/Discard actions land in PR-G. Discarded drafts hard-delete; sent drafts move to existing message-retention envelope under Processing Activity 2. `audit_byok_use` rows: existing 12-month sweep (Processing Activity 8 P0 mirror precedent). Inngest event store (SQLite): 30-day rolling window via `inngest start --history-retention` (operator-managed; not personal-data-bearing at v1 because Stripe customer email is hashed BEFORE the envelope is signed). Art. 17 cascade for `messages` is via the existing PA-2 anonymisation path. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **"Drafts everywhere, sends nowhere" enforced by `messages_external_tier_status_check` CHECK constraint** (migration 046) — DB-level invariant that any future code attempting `INSERT … status='sent'` on an external-tier row is rejected with `ERRCODE=23514`. Widening requires explicit `DROP + CREATE` — caught at PR review. (2) **Per-tenant hourly cost cap** via `record_byok_use_and_check_cap()` plpgsql RPC with leading `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on `public.users` — closes the TOCTOU race two concurrent BYOK turns at cap-boundary would otherwise hit. (3) **BYOK lease opens INSIDE each step.run** — ALS context is local to the Inngest step boundary; the SDK's sync-escape check (`byok-lease.ts:133-139`) fails closed if the lease is captured outside its scope. (4) **JWT mint per step** via `getFreshTenantClient(founderId)` — never cached across step boundaries; the `is_jti_denied` deny-list (PR-D #3883) gates the mint. (5) **Single-pass verify-stripe-state invariant** — verify lives in the CFO function body, NOT a step.run-checkpointed result; any 6h-deadlettered retry re-enters from the top and re-verifies (closes stale-after-verify failure mode). (6) **Schema-gate as non-throwing step.run** returning `{deadletter:true}` — schema-version mismatches do NOT consume a retry budget. (7) **Inngest signature-verify required at startup** — `INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY` is mandatory at module load; route returns 401 on missing/malformed/stale signature BEFORE any function dispatches. (8) **Customer email hashed at webhook boundary** (sha256, irreversible) BEFORE the Inngest envelope is signed — no cleartext email enters the Inngest store or stdout logs. `payment_method` dropped entirely. (9) **`SOLEUR_FR5_ENABLED=false` default at merge** — dark-launch flag; operator flips after the self-hosted Inngest server health-checks. Stripe redelivery (up to 3 days) preserves at-least-once. (10) **`processed_stripe_events` unique-index dedup** at the webhook (migration 030) — backstops Inngest's 24h dedup window for Stripe retries beyond 24h. (11) **Inngest server bound to `127.0.0.1`** via systemd `--host 127.0.0.1` — no public ingress; forged-event attack surface eliminated. (12) **Page-level disclosure banner** (`RUNTIME_COST_DISCLOSURE` constant in `lib/legal/disclosures.ts`) rendered above the Today section — Art. 13 transparency that autonomous drafts incur BYOK Anthropic API cost. |

ADR-030 (`knowledge-base/engineering/architecture/decisions/ADR-030-inngest-as-durable-trigger-layer.md`) records the substrate decision (self-hosted Inngest OSS on Hetzner over Inngest Cloud) and the load-bearing invariants (I1–I5) backing TOMs (3), (5), (7), (1).
