## Processing Activity 14 — Per-tenant scope grants and audit viewer (PR-G #3947)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Operate the per-tenant authorization substrate that gates the autonomous-draft runtime (Processing Activity 13). One founder, one row per (action class, point-in-time): the founder authorizes Soleur to act on their behalf for a specific action class (e.g., `finance.payment_failed`) at one of three tiers (`auto`, `draft_one_click`, `approve_every_time`); the webhook predicate produces no Inngest event in the absence of an active grant. Companion `/dashboard/audit` viewer renders the resulting ledger (`audit_byok_use` rows + Inngest run history) per Art. 15 right of access. Brand-survival threshold: `single-user incident`. PR-G #3947 / draft PR #3984; ADR-033. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Founder-operators (Soleur users on the Web Platform). NOT end-users of tenant apps. The scope_grants ledger records the founder's authorization decisions; the audit viewer renders the founder's own runtime history (RLS self-select bounds the read to the authenticated founder). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | `scope_grants`: `id` (UUID), `founder_id` (UUID, FK to `public.users(id)` — nullable so Art. 17 anonymise can null it without DELETE), `action_class` (text, ≤64 chars; PR-G ships `finance.payment_failed` only), `tier` (one of `auto` / `draft_one_click` / `approve_every_time`), `granted_at`, `revoked_at`, `revoked_reason` (text, ≤256), `created_at`. Inngest envelope tier pin: `event.data.tier` (the grant tier active when the trigger fired — preserved on the event so the audit ledger renders consent-at-time). Audit viewer reads: `audit_byok_use` rows (existing Processing Activity 8) and Inngest run history (via server-only proxy at `/api/dashboard/runs`; raw `customerId` masked server-side; `authorizing_event` rendered only as the redacted summary). |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None. No special-category data enters this substrate; the scope grants record consent state, not the underlying decision content. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(b) — performance of contract (the scope grants ledger is the substrate by which the founder's contract performance authorizes Soleur to act on their behalf for specific action classes). Art. 22(2)(c) explicit consent applies for the `auto` tier (second-click acknowledgement at `/dashboard/settings/scope-grants` records the explicit consent required for solely-automated decision-making with legal/significant effects). Art. 22(3) human-review affordance inlined on every `/dashboard/audit` Inngest row (mailto:legal@jikigai.com) and via the email channel; ToS §3a.6 and Privacy Policy §8.3 disclose the right. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Soleur internal: founder-scoped Supabase reads via cookie-scoped RLS at `/dashboard/settings/scope-grants` and `/dashboard/audit`; service-role at the webhook predicate (the webhook has no founder JWT — `.eq("founder_id", founderId)` IS the tenant filter, not belt-and-suspenders). **No third-party recipients.** No new sub-processors introduced by PR-G (the Inngest substrate remains self-hosted per ADR-030; Anthropic remains the founder's bilateral BYOK relationship). |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **No transfer.** `scope_grants` lives in `public` schema on Supabase eu-west-1 (existing Supabase row in the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping). Audit viewer reads stay within EU residency: BYOK rows from Supabase eu-west-1, Inngest run history from the self-hosted Inngest server on Hetzner (eu-central, bound to `127.0.0.1`). |
| **(f) Retention** | `scope_grants` rows: **append-only, retained indefinitely** as a consent record (the substrate of the founder's authorization to act on their behalf). Tier change does NOT update or delete — re-grant inserts a new row, marks the previous row `revoked_at = now(), revoked_reason = 'tier_change'`. Revocation flips `revoked_at` only. Art. 17 erasure cascade via `anonymise_scope_grants(p_user_id)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC: sets `founder_id = NULL` on the user's rows (GUC + role-check trigger bypass); the row's tier history persists with a null discriminator. The cascade runs BEFORE `anonymise_tc_acceptances` at `server/account-delete.ts` (FK ordering — both target `public.users(id)` with `ON DELETE RESTRICT`). Inngest envelope tier pin: governed by Processing Activity 13's 30-day SQLite window. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **Append-only WORM ledger** — `scope_grants_no_mutate()` trigger on UPDATE rejects any change to non-revoke columns (`founder_id`, `action_class`, `tier`, `granted_at`, `created_at` and any previously-set `revoked_at`/`revoked_reason`) with `ERRCODE = P0001`; DELETE rejected unconditionally except via the GUC-gated anonymise bypass for the Art. 17 cascade. (2) **RLS self-select** — `scope_grants_owner_select` policy `auth.uid() = founder_id`; no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy (writes routed exclusively through SECURITY DEFINER RPCs). (3) **SECURITY DEFINER RPC discipline** — `grant_action_class`, `revoke_action_class`, `anonymise_scope_grants` all pin `SET search_path = public, pg_temp`; explicit `REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon` + `GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated` (grant/revoke) or `service_role` (anonymise). (4) **Per-grant deny-by-default at the webhook** — `apps/web-platform/app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:437` requires `isGranted(supabase, founderId, actionClass)` to return non-null AND `!isDenied(actionClass)` before `inngest.send`. The DB-level grant lookup is the load-bearing tenant gate (closes the K14 flip-in-PR-G risk vector). (5) **Consent-at-time pinning** — the Inngest envelope carries `data.tier = grant.tier`; the CFO function writes `messages.trust_tier` from the envelope, not from the current ledger state. A founder revoking after a trigger fires does NOT alter the recorded tier of the in-flight run. (6) **Server-only Inngest API credential** — `INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY` is bundled into the `/api/dashboard/runs` route only; lint test at `test/lint/inngest-key-server-only.test.ts` (TR7) grep-asserts the key is absent from `app/(dashboard)/**` and `components/**`. (7) **Belt-and-suspenders RLS filter** — every cookie-scoped query in the founder UI (audit viewer's BYOK section, scope-grants settings page) additionally pins `.eq("founder_id", user.id)` so any future RLS loosening still fails closed. (8) **Pessimistic UI + auto-tier acknowledgement** — `/dashboard/settings/scope-grants` UI does NOT optimistically update; selecting `auto` reveals an inline acknowledgement checkbox that gates submit (Art. 22(2)(c) explicit-consent UI primitive at single-user-incident threshold). (9) **Art. 22(3) "Request human review" affordance** inlined on every `/dashboard/audit` Inngest row (mailto:legal@jikigai.com) and via the email channel — ToS §3a.6 and Privacy Policy §8.3. (10) **Audit-viewer customer-id masking** — raw `customerId` masked server-side in `lib/inngest/list-runs.ts` (`maskCustomerId`); the masked summary is rendered exclusively by `components/audit/redacted-event-summary.tsx` (AC8 single-renderer invariant). |

ADR-033 (`knowledge-base/engineering/architecture/decisions/ADR-033-per-tenant-scope-grants.md`) records the substrate decision (Supabase WORM ledger over Doppler / in-process registry / JWT claim / global flag alone) and links to ADR-030 for the autonomous-draft substrate this gates.

## Processing Activity 15 — LinkedIn Company Page publication (Jikigai) (#4046)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Operate the Jikigai-owned Soleur LinkedIn Company Page as a marketing / community / case-study distribution channel for Soleur content. Posts are produced by `scripts/content-publisher.sh` (cron) against the LinkedIn Community Management API (developer app 229658411) using a 60-day-rotated `LINKEDIN_ORG_ACCESS_TOKEN` minted by an operator-authorized OAuth flow. Brand-survival threshold: `single-user incident` (CPO sign-off carried forward from `2026-05-19-linkedin-community-api-reapply-jikigai-brainstorm.md`). Microsoft business verification (Phase 5.4 of the PR #4047 runbook) is the **first cross-controller personal-data transfer** triggered by this activity and is the load-bearing reason this PA exists before Phase 5.4 (per CLO Critical fold-in). |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | (i) **Jikigai SARL gérant (Jean Deruelle)** — natural-person identity disclosed via K-bis extract during the Microsoft business-verification flow (controller-to-controller). (ii) **LinkedIn Page followers** of `https://www.linkedin.com/company/soleur/` — identifiers and engagement metadata are surfaced by LinkedIn Page Insights API to the Jikigai-controlled Page admin; Soleur consumes these only in aggregate for marketing analytics. (iii) **LinkedIn members who engage with Soleur Page posts** (commenters, reactors) — same Page Insights / engagement surface. NOT end-users of tenant apps. NOT Soleur founder-operators (those are covered by PA2 / PA8 / PA13 / PA14). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | (i) **K-bis extract contents** (gérant full legal name, SARL registered address, RCS Paris 927 585 729, capital structure metadata) plus tax registration and registered-address proof — submitted once to Microsoft Ireland Operations Ltd via the LinkedIn Page admin upload UI (no Soleur-side persistence beyond the operator's local file system at the upload moment). (ii) **Page-follower identifiers** (display name, profile URL slug). (iii) **Engagement metadata** (timestamps, reaction type, comment body if any) returned by LinkedIn Page Insights API. (iv) **Operational secrets**: `LINKEDIN_ORG_ACCESS_TOKEN` (60-day OAuth bearer, Doppler `prd` + GitHub Actions secret), `LINKEDIN_ORG_ID` (organisation URN). |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None expected. K-bis carries the SARL gérant's identity but not Art. 9 categories; tax / address proofs are operational not special-category. Page-follower engagement is by-design public on LinkedIn and does not contain Art. 9 disclosure absent the data subject's own posting choice. |
| **Lawful basis** | (i) **K-bis transfer to Microsoft Ireland / LinkedIn Ireland**: Art. 6(1)(c) — legal obligation (Microsoft's KYC-equivalent business-verification process is a condition of Community Management API access, which is in turn a condition of the LinkedIn Platform Terms; Jikigai must transmit corporate identity documents to satisfy the platform-side legal-due-diligence obligation). (ii) **Operating the Soleur Page for marketing**: Art. 6(1)(f) — legitimate interest (promote the Jikigai-owned Soleur product to Soleur's target audience; LIA documented at `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-05-19-linkedin-org-page-lia.md` (#4051)). (iii) **Engagement / Page Insights consumption**: Art. 6(1)(f) legitimate interest, joint-controller analysis pending (CNIL / *Wirtschaftsakademie* C-210/16; follow-up issue tracks the Art. 26 joint-controller arrangement assessment). |
| **(d) Recipients** | (i) **LinkedIn Ireland Unlimited Company** (independent controller for Page data; joint controller with Jikigai for Page Insights analytics per C-210/16 — assessment pending). (ii) **Microsoft Ireland Operations Ltd** (custodian of business-verification documents; Microsoft is LinkedIn's parent, but the verification flow's document custody is a Microsoft Ireland service distinct from LinkedIn's Page-data role). (iii) Soleur internal: `scripts/content-publisher.sh` reads tokens from GitHub Actions secrets and Doppler `prd`; the `scheduled-linkedin-token-check.yml` workflow consumes the introspection response to file/close per-token renewal issues. **No third-party recipients beyond Microsoft Ireland and LinkedIn Ireland.** |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | (i) **K-bis → Microsoft Ireland**: intra-EU (controller-to-controller, no Chapter V transfer mechanism required at the Microsoft Ireland custody point). Microsoft routes to US-region infrastructure under its **EU Data Boundary** commitments (https://aka.ms/EUDataBoundary) — onward transfer safeguards rest on Microsoft's published EUDB scope. (ii) **Page data → LinkedIn Ireland**: intra-EU for the controller relationship; LinkedIn operates EU-region for Page data with US fallback under DPF + SCCs Module 2 (LinkedIn / Microsoft DPF certifications). (iii) **Soleur-side token storage**: Doppler `prd` (US-based but SCCs M2+3 in force) and GitHub Actions secret store (US, GitHub DPF + SCCs M2). |
| **(f) Retention** | (i) **K-bis + tax/address proofs**: controlled by Microsoft Ireland under its EUDB commitments — typical KYC retention envelope (years). Jikigai does not retain a Soleur-side copy beyond the upload moment. (ii) **`LINKEDIN_ORG_ACCESS_TOKEN`**: 60-day vendor-mandated rotation; old tokens revoked at the LinkedIn developer portal as part of the `linkedin-setup.sh generate-token` flow. (iii) **Page posts / engagement metadata**: controlled by LinkedIn under its Page-data retention policies (controller-to-controller — Soleur does not set the envelope). (iv) **Rolling tracker #4046**: append-only operational log on GitHub Issues; closed (with `--reason completed`) when all queued posts are re-published post-approval. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **Doppler-only secret storage**: `LINKEDIN_ORG_ACCESS_TOKEN` lives in Doppler `prd` + GitHub Actions secrets; never committed; rotation is operator-driven via `plugins/soleur/skills/community/scripts/linkedin-setup.sh generate-token`. (2) **Per-token expiry monitor**: `.github/workflows/scheduled-linkedin-token-check.yml` introspects both `LINKEDIN_ACCESS_TOKEN` and `LINKEDIN_ORG_ACCESS_TOKEN` weekly; opens a per-token `[Action Required]` issue at expiry detection and closes it on next valid check. (3) **Vendor-blocked error class routes to rolling tracker, not per-post issues**: `classify_linkedin_error` in `scripts/content-publisher.sh` matches missing-token, HTTP 401, HTTP 403, and `w_organization_social` scope errors → `append_to_linkedin_tracker` (idempotent append to #4046). Closes the 9-issue accretion pre-#4046. (4) **DNS verification record managed in Terraform** (`apps/web-platform/infra/jikigai-com.tf`): narrow aliased `cloudflare.jikigai_com` provider backed by a `Zone:DNS:Edit on jikigai.com`-only API token; no `lifecycle.ignore_changes` so dashboard drift is surfaced at next `terraform plan`. (5) **Targeted-apply blast-radius safeguard**: Phase 5.2 of the runbook applies via `terraform apply -target=cloudflare_record.linkedin_verification` and follows with an untargeted `terraform plan` (same session) to confirm zero soleur.ai drift (AC24 / terraform-architect P1-4). (6) **`LINKEDIN_ALLOW_POST` flag** (`"true"` in the workflow env) is a posting kill-switch; flipping to anything else (or unset) routes posting to the rolling-tracker fallback without surfacing API errors. (7) **K-bis natural-person disclosure footnote (per CLO HIGH-2)**: The Microsoft business-verification flow transmits the K-bis extract which names the SARL gérant (Jean Deruelle) — controller-to-controller transfer of natural-person data per CNIL délibération SAN-2024-006; the data-subject category (i) above records this explicitly so the Privacy Policy and DPD (follow-up PR #4051) carry consistent disclosure under Art. 13(1)(e). |

**Amendment (2026-06-10):** the publication surface moved from the Jikigai Company Page (`linkedin.com/company/jikigai/`, org 112298380) to the dedicated **Soleur** Company Page (`linkedin.com/company/soleur/`, org 129094054) per the operator's brand-separation decision — same controller (Jikigai SARL), same developer app (229658411), same token, pipeline, scopes, recipients, lawful bases, and retention; zero posts had been published to the prior surface. LIA, Privacy Policy, and DPD amended in lockstep. The K-bis business-verification limbs above are historical record of the one-time Microsoft Ireland transfer (performed via the Jikigai Page admin flow) and are unchanged.

Legal-track PR **#4051** landed alongside this PA15 entry, satisfying the Art. 30(1) timing requirement before Phase 5.4 of the operator runbook (Microsoft business verification): LIA at `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-05-19-linkedin-org-page-lia.md`, Privacy Policy updated under Art. 13(1)(e) naming LinkedIn Ireland + Microsoft Ireland recipients with the Art. 17 carve-out for LinkedIn-published content (Privacy Policy §4.10 + §5.12 + §5.13 + §6 + §7 + §8.1 + §10), and Data Protection Disclosure updated to mirror (DPD §2.3(p) + §4.2 + §6.4 + §10.3).

The `## Joint Controllers (Art. 26)` section above remains "None" until a Page Insights API call is first issued; that triggers the Art. 26 joint-controller arrangement assessment (separate follow-up). Plan §"GDPR / Compliance Gate Output" Medium-2 tracks the trigger.

## Processing Activity 17 — GitHub-sourced multi-source priority signals (PR-H #3244)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Two distinct sub-purposes are inventoried under this PA: **(i) display-only signal ingestion** — surface high-confidence engineering, triage, and security signals as autonomous-draft cards in the founder's Daily Priorities Today section by ingesting GitHub repository activity via a GitHub App webhook (PR review pending, CI failed, P0/P1 issue opened, CVE / secret-scanning alert). Display-only: render as draft cards with "let CTO handle it" affordance; no automated downstream processing, no model training input. PR-H #4066. **(ii) workspace clone reconciliation — filesystem write side-effect (#4224)** — on receipt of a GitHub `push` webhook for the operator's default branch, the Soleur backend pulls the connected repository's `knowledge-base/` content into the operator's workspace clone at `userData.workspace_path` (`git pull --ff-only`, installation-scoped credentials, Inngest-coalesced per `installation_id`). Distinct from (i) because the data flow has a filesystem write side-effect — the repository content is materialised into the operator's workspace, not just rendered display-only. The operator's connected repo content (already controller-side) is the data flow; no third-party data subjects are introduced beyond those already enumerated under (i). Brand-survival threshold: `single-user incident`. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Founder-operators (Soleur users who installed the GitHub App on their own account/orgs). NOT end-users of any tenant app. Third-party repository contributors are in scope where their PR title / issue body content is rendered on the founder's own dashboard; that content is the upstream repo's, subject to the founder's own data-handling obligations. **[2026-07-31 CORRECTION (#7100).** This cell previously carved third-party contributors out on the ground that their content was rendered "**display-only**". That carve-out is **no longer accurate for the `jikig-ai/soleur` surface** and the word has been removed here: the same third-party repository and community content now **egresses to Anthropic PBC** under the Jikigai-keyed `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (PA-31) and **reappears in public GitHub issue comments and in committed digests** (PA-32). "Display-only" remains an accurate description of the PA-17 **webhook-to-dashboard** flow itself — no PA-17 code path was changed — but it is no longer a sound basis for excluding third-party contributors from this register's data-subject inventory, because other registered activities process the same content non-display-only. The `(b) Purposes` sub-purpose (i) wording "Display-only: … no automated downstream processing, no model training input" is true of PA-17's own flow and must be read as scoped to it.**] |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | `founderId` (UUID); GitHub `installation_id` (bigint, controller-side identifier); webhook delivery_id (`x-github-delivery` UUID); repository content rendered display-only via the `messages` row (`source='github'`, `tier='external_low_stakes'`, `status='draft'`, `source_ref`, `draft_preview` redacted via `redactGithubSourcedText` at render time per plan TR6 amendment). Audit telemetry rows in `audit_github_token_use` (`installation_id`, `repo_full_name`, `endpoint`, `ts`, `response_status`). Webhook dedup rows in `processed_github_events` (`delivery_id`, `received_at`). |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None by design. Operator AUP §4.7 prohibits Art. 9 / 10 content in repository submissions; the render-time `redactGithubSourcedText` strips credentials, emails, phones, UUIDs, JWTs, IPv4 — but does NOT claim to scrub free-text Art. 9 disclosures. ~~The display-only constraint is the load-bearing minimization~~ **[2026-07-31 NARROWING (#7100), applied at review: struck for consistency with the amendment three rows below, which withdraws exactly this over-claim and the term `display-only` with it. Leaving one instance struck and its twin standing is the marked/unmarked pattern a reader resolves as "unmarked = still live".]** The display constraint is *a* minimisation, not the load-bearing one. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(f) — legitimate interest. Three-part test (updated for ADR-044 cross-member scope, #4558; CLO-attested 2026-05-28 — supersedes the prior solo-founder-only test): (i) **purpose** = surface high-confidence priority signals derived from a workspace's connected-repository activity onto the dashboards of that workspace's members (the Workspace Owner and any Co-Members the Owner has admitted), for the benefit of operating the shared workspace. (ii) **necessity** = unchanged from the display-only ingest rationale — polling alternatives suffer latency drift, idle-tenant cost, and require a parallel audit primitive (ADR-036 §Rejected alternatives); no less-intrusive means surfaces the same signal. The widened-recipient set adds no new data collection — it is the same derived signal, rendered to the additional members the Owner already consented to admit under Art. 6(1)(a). (iii) **balancing** = data subjects are now the workspace's members (the Owner, plus Co-Members whose repository contributions feed the signal), not solely the solo founder; recipients are those same members at the workspace-ownership grain (no third-party recipient — GitHub remains the source). The Co-Members' reasonable expectation of in-workspace signal surfacing is high because their access was admitted by the Owner under a written employment/contractor relationship and a specific Art. 6(1)(a) attestation; the derived signal is operational metadata about repository activity, not special-category data (AUP §4.7 prohibits Art. 9/10 content; render-time `redactGithubSourcedText` is the minimization). The legitimate interest is not overridden by the members' interests because (a) access is consent-gated and bounded to the workspace, (b) the credential itself is never a derived-signal recipient (it is column-revoked and RPC-gated, migration 079), and (c) no pre-consent connection is in scope (migration 080 skips co-membered workspaces). **Residual risk fenced:** where the repository contains personal data of natural persons who are neither the Owner nor a Co-Member, the balancing is re-opened by the "first third-party-personal-data repository" re-evaluation trigger recorded in the #4558 counsel-review audit (`knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-05-counsel-review-4558.md`) before any such repo is brought into scope. **[2026-07-31 AMENDMENT (#7100) — THE TRIGGER HAS FIRED, AND IT FIRED BEFORE IT WAS HONOURED.** The `jikig-ai/soleur` repository has, since at least **2026-02-19** (the earliest committed digest, `knowledge-base/support/community/2026-02-19-digest.md`), carried personal data of natural persons who are neither the Workspace Owner nor a Co-Member — Discord guild members, Hacker News posters, GitHub issue/PR commenters and stargazers — collected and, in part, republished by the Anthropic-egressing Inngest fleet. Counsel review `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-07-counsel-review-7086.md` § Finding 3 reached the same conclusion. The Art. 14(3)(a) one-month clock on that population therefore expired around **2026-03-19**, so the trigger fired approximately five months before this amendment records it. **The re-opened balancing lives at PA-32** (community observation and republication) and in `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-07-31-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci-lia.md`, which concludes that Art. 6(1)(f) is **not available** for the republication limb as implemented. A new LIA alone would not have discharged a trigger recorded in *this* cell, which is why this amendment and the two below are made here.**] |
| **(d) Recipients** | Soleur internal (service-role for the webhook handler + Inngest dispatcher; tenant-client RLS for `/api/dashboard/today` reads). Anthropic is the recipient of LLM API calls under the founder's BYOK lease only when the strategy table delegates to a downstream "spawn agent" action — display-only cards do NOT consume BYOK budget. No third-party recipients beyond GitHub Inc. as the **source** (not recipient) — but for the outbound-API and publication flows where GitHub Inc **is** a recipient, see **PA-31 §(d)** and **PA-32 §(d)**, which state the divergence from this cell expressly rather than leaving the two to contradict each other silently *(cross-reference added 2026-07-31 at review, #7100)*. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | GitHub Inc. (Microsoft Corporation) is the **source** of the data, not a recipient. The data egresses GitHub via the webhook signed-payload to the Soleur webhook endpoint (US for SaaS callers; EU residency for self-hosted callers); thereafter remains on Soleur infra (Supabase eu-west-1 for the founder-owned `messages` row + audit ledger; self-hosted Inngest on Hetzner eu-central for the dispatcher). Standard Contractual Clauses cover GitHub Inc. as a sub-processor; the Soleur ↔ GitHub flow is bilaterally per-founder (the App is installable on the founder's own account/orgs). |
| **(f) Retention** | `messages` draft rows (PR-H drafts): TTL deferred to follow-up TS-05 (R5 mitigation; tracked at exit gate). Founder controls Send/Edit/Discard via PR-H+1 wiring. `audit_github_token_use` rows: retention envelope inherits from Processing Activity 8 (operational telemetry — 12-month sweep). Art. 17 cascade is automatic via `ON DELETE SET NULL` on the `founder_id` FK — `auth.admin.deleteUser` tears down the public.users row and Postgres scrubs every matching `audit_github_token_use.founder_id`. Explicit RPC `anonymise_audit_github_token_use(p_founder_id)` (migration 052) is available for account-suspend flows that anonymise WITHOUT auth-deletion; `server/account-delete.ts` invokes it for symmetry with 043/044/048 anonymise RPCs (defense-in-depth fallback if the SET-NULL cascade is bypassed). `processed_github_events`: natural cleanup via Postgres autovacuum + 30-day partition rotation; no explicit TTL daemon (the table is small; `delivery_id` text + `received_at` timestamptz; self-hosted Inngest's 24h `event.id` dedup window is FIXED, so the DB-side dedup is the load-bearing replay defense beyond 24h). |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **GitHub App installation token short-lived** + per-installation; `@octokit/auth-app` v8+ auto-refresh at `expires_at − 60s` internally — NO manual cache (vercel/next.js#65350). PAT off-table (ADR-036). (2) **Per-request Octokit factory** — `createGitHubAppClient(installationId, founderId)` instantiates a fresh `App` on every call; NO module-scope singleton (load-bearing test: AC14 fresh-import-per-test). `founderId` is captured in closure into the per-Octokit audit hooks (see TOM-(10)) for per-founder attribution; caller contract requires session-validated `founderId`. (3) **timingSafeEqual signature verification** on every webhook delivery; fail-closed 401 on mismatch / missing secret. (4) **PG_UNIQUE_VIOLATION dedup** via `processed_github_events.delivery_id` PRIMARY KEY (ADR-037) — `.insert()` plain (NO `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` because supabase-js returns `data:null` on the no-op path — the affected-row gate is unreliable). Catch `23505` → 200 duplicate. (5) **releaseDedupRow() pattern** on any 5xx error AFTER dedup INSERT — DELETEs the row so the GitHub redelivery can be processed cleanly. Without this, transient inngest.send failure silently drops events. (6) **Scope-grant gate** (PR-G ADR-033) — every event class checked via `isGranted(supabase, founderId, actionClass)`; fail-closed (200 without dispatch) on no-grant OR DB error; Sentry mirror per `cq-silent-fallback-must-mirror-to-sentry`. (7) **Render-time redaction** (per plan TR6 amendment); INSERT-time redaction is belt-and-suspenders. **[2026-07-31 NARROWING (#7100).** This measure was previously described as "the load-bearing Art. 14 gate". That over-claims and the phrase is withdrawn. `redactGithubSourcedText` (`apps/web-platform/lib/safety/redaction-allowlist.ts`) strips credentials, emails, phone numbers, UUIDs, JWTs, authorization headers and IPv4 addresses — its own docstring calls the set "conservative" — and it demonstrably redacts **neither usernames, handles and display names nor free text**. It is therefore a **credential- and contact-identifier scrub**, not an Art. 14 minimisation gate: it does not prevent a third party from being identified in rendered content, and it cannot substitute for Art. 14 information. Art. 14 for the third-party population is recorded at **PA-31 §(b)** and **PA-32 §(b)** as **engaged, undischarged and overdue since ~2026-03-19**, with remediation tracked at deferred item **DEF-1** (#7120).**] (8) **`Cache-Control: private, max-age=60`** on `/api/dashboard/today` minimizes Art. 14 surface and prevents re-redact-thrash on rapid refresh. (9) **CVE / secret-scanning cards render ID + severity only** by default (AC6); the redacted body is revealed only on explicit Edit-modal click. (10) **`record_github_token_use` SECURITY DEFINER RPC** is the sole write path to `audit_github_token_use`; REVOKE from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated; GRANT to service_role only; `SET search_path = public, pg_temp` pin per `cq-pg-security-definer-search-path-pin-pg-temp`. **PR-H ships the schema + RPC + Art. 17 cascade; PR-H+1 (#4098) wires the per-Octokit-call audit writer at the `server/github/app-client.ts` factory** — `octokit.hook.after("request", ...)` + `octokit.hook.error("request", ...)` route every Octokit response (success or error) through `recordGithubApiCall` in `server/github/audit-writer.ts`, which calls `record_github_token_use(p_founder_id, p_installation_id, p_repo_full_name, p_endpoint, p_response_status)` via service-role. Non-blocking on failure with mirror via `reportSilentFallback(feature=github-audit, op=record)` per `cq-silent-fallback-must-mirror-to-sentry`. **Best-effort caveat (tracking #4130):** the audit-write fires via fire-and-forget from the Octokit hook; under Vercel serverless / Inngest worker suspension, an in-flight write may be terminated before reaching Supabase, dropping the row. Runtime-context primitive (waitUntil thread-through) deferred to #4130; Sentry surface tracks any suspension-drop rate above zero, and zero production callers exist until #4124's spawn-agent button wiring lands. (11) **`SOLEUR_FR5_GITHUB_ENABLED=false` default at merge** — operator flips post-merge only after PR-G #3947 ships its scope-grant UI. (12) **Per-(founder, event-class) Inngest concurrency** via CEL key `event.data.founderId + ":" + event.name` — CI events do not queue behind PR-review events for the same founder. (13) **Provisioning via committed App Manifest** — operator clicks an internal-only static page that POSTs `apps/web-platform/infra/github-app-manifest.json` to `https://github.com/settings/apps/new` to pre-fill the App-creation form. The page renders an informational view on any GitHub callback (`code`, `installation_id`, `setup_action`); no credentials are received by Soleur via the manifest flow. Manifest-vs-live permission/event drift is detected hourly by `scheduled-github-app-drift-guard.yml` (Art. 33 latency primitive) with `permission_drift` / `permission_unexpected_grant` / `response_shape_unparseable` failure modes and a 24h manifest-touching-PR suppression window. Trade-off recorded: collapses 12-field form-fill to one-button click; operator paste step into Doppler UI intentionally preserved as the airgap that bounds blast radius for the App's identity credentials. See #4115. (14) **Workspace clone reconciliation — write-side TOMs (#4224, sub-purpose (b)(ii) above)**: (a) **Per-installation Inngest CEL concurrency** key `'wsr-' + event.data.installationId` (limit 1) — coalesces rapid pushes per installation; no cross-installation coalescing. (b) **`git pull --ff-only`** — non-fast-forward (force-push / rebased main) returns `ok:false, error_class:"non_fast_forward"` and surfaces to the operator via `KbSyncStatus` desync chip; the workspace is never overwritten destructively. (c) **Installation-scoped credentials** via `gitWithInstallationAuth` — the helper mints a fresh GitHub App installation token per pull; no PAT, no module-scoped cache. (d) **`workspace_path` resolved SERVER-SIDE from `session.user_id`** at the manual `POST /api/kb/sync` endpoint — the operator-supplied request body is ignored (Sharp Edge in the plan; AC3). (e) **Defense-in-depth `founderId → installation_id` re-verification** in `workspace-reconcile-on-push.ts` before invoking `syncWorkspace` — a stale Inngest event whose founder has uninstalled between dispatch and pickup is skipped + Sentry-mirrored without touching the FS. (f) **`reportSilentFallback` mirror on every failure class** (`feature:workspace-reconcile-push` + `feature:kb-route-helpers` + `feature:session-sync`) per `cq-silent-fallback-must-mirror-to-sentry` — drift-class telemetry for the 30-day cron-backstop ramp gate (#4228 deferred). (g) **Heterogeneous `kb_sync_history` JSONB cap 100** — every reconcile (webhook-push OR manual) appends a row with `{at, trigger, sha_before, sha_after, ok, error_class?, push_received_at, sync_completed_at}`; the column already exists (migration 017). |

**Sub-clause (c) — workspace-scoped repo-connection state + cross-member processing (ADR-044, migrations 079/080/081, #4543 / #4558).** As of ADR-044, the GitHub repo-connection state that drives sub-purposes (i) and (ii) above — `repo_url`, `repo_provider`, `github_installation_id`, `repo_status`, `repo_last_synced_at` — is relocated from the per-user `users` table to the `workspaces` table (additive migration 079; idempotent solo-only backfill 080; application-layer TS read-cutover in `current-repo-url.ts` / `resolve-installation-id.ts` — workspaces-only reads, no migration; Art. 17 erasure cascade migration 081, which re-CREATEs `anonymise_organization_membership` to null `github_installation_id` + `repo_last_synced_at` and set `repo_status='not_connected'` on a departing owner's workspaces, retaining `repo_url` so a promoted replacement owner can re-authorize the same repo). This changes the **grain** of the processing, not the recipients: **(1) New cross-member data flow.** Previously repo state was per-user, so a natural person who JOINED another user's workspace as a Co-Member could not reach the workspace owner's connected repository. Post-ADR-044, repo-connection state is workspace-scoped, so a Co-Member of a workspace can sync and access THAT WORKSPACE's connected repository AND the knowledge-base derived from it. The consequence is that a Workspace Owner's repository content and the repo-derived knowledge-base become accessible to, and processed for, the Co-Members of that workspace — at the workspace-ownership grain, not per-user. No new third-party recipient is introduced: GitHub Inc. (Microsoft Corporation) remains the **source** (not a recipient) as in (d)/(e) above; the new recipients are the workspace's own Co-Members, already enumerated as a data-recipient category under PA-2 §(d) (workspace co-member). **(2) Lawful basis for the Co-Member's processing of the owner's repo/KB data.** The Co-Member's ACCESS to the Workspace Owner's connected repository and derived knowledge-base rests on the Workspace Owner's Art. 6(1)(a) consent, captured in the migration-058 `workspace_member_attestations` row (the inviter attestation, PA-2 substrate): the Owner attests at invite-time that the invited member will access this workspace's connected repository and knowledge-base. The existing Art. 6(1)(f) legitimate-interest basis recorded under "Lawful basis" above continues to govern the repo-DERIVED signals only. This consent-for-access + legitimate-interest-for-derived-signals split is the same composition disclosed in Privacy Policy §4.11, DPD §2.3(u), and GDPR Policy §5.3 (no doc carries a different primary basis for the cross-member access). Backfill scope is consent-preserving by construction: migration 080 copies repo-connection state to **solo workspaces only** (the `w.id = u.id` join plus a member-count = 1 canary guard); any workspace that already had a Co-Member at backfill time is SKIPPED, so no pre-existing connection becomes Co-Member-accessible without a fresh Art. 6(1)(a) invite attestation post-flag-flip. The Art. 6(1)(a) basis therefore never operates retroactively on a connection established before the Owner's consent of record. **(3) Credential gate (Art. 32 TOM).** The `github_installation_id` is a GitHub App token grant (a CREDENTIAL). It is removed from the `authenticated` role's column grant — implemented as a table-level `REVOKE SELECT ON public.workspaces FROM authenticated` followed by a `GRANT SELECT` on the explicit non-credential column list that omits `github_installation_id` (a bare column-level `REVOKE SELECT (github_installation_id)` is a no-op while Supabase's default table-level grant exists, so the table-revoke-then-column-regrant is the only effective form) — so the row-level `workspaces_select_for_members` policy cannot expose it; the value is readable ONLY via the membership-checked SECURITY DEFINER RPC `resolve_workspace_installation_id(p_workspace_id)`, which returns NULL to non-members. **(4) Single source of truth.** Repo reads come from `workspaces` ONLY — no per-user (`users`-table) read-time fallback — so a Co-Member's sync resolves the workspace owner's repo deterministically rather than via the deleted installation-id heuristics (#4546/#4557). See ADR-044 (`knowledge-base/engineering/architecture/decisions/ADR-044-workspace-repo-ownership.md`), brand-survival threshold `single-user incident`; cross-references PA-2 §(d) (workspace co-member recipient category) and DPD §2.3(u).

ADR-036 (`knowledge-base/engineering/architecture/decisions/ADR-036-github-app-webhook-as-second-multi-source-ingress.md`) records the ingress decision (webhook over polling, App over PAT). ADR-037 (`knowledge-base/engineering/architecture/decisions/ADR-037-messages-source-ref-composite-unique-for-multi-source-dedup.md`) records the dedup primitive (partial-unique index + `PG_UNIQUE_VIOLATION` catch).

## Processing Activity 16 — Template-authorized autonomous send (action_sends ledger, PR-H #4077)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Record the cryptographic signature of every founder click on Send (or every autonomous infra.* action executed under the `auto_with_digest` tier) so the per-tenant action ledger carries an Art. 5(2) accountability artefact for each outbound. One row per send. Bridges the scope_grants consent substrate (Processing Activity 14) to the actual outbound delivery layer that PR-I (#4078) will integrate. Brand-survival threshold: `single-user incident`. PR-H #4077 / PR #4065; ADR-034. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Founder-operators (Soleur users on the Web Platform). NOT recipients of the outbound. Recipients' identifiers are stored as SHA-256 hashes only (`recipient_id_hash`); the raw values are not persisted by Soleur. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | `action_sends`: `id` (UUID), `user_id` (UUID, FK to `public.users(id)` ON DELETE RESTRICT — nullable to admit Art. 17 anonymise), `message_id` (UUID, FK to `public.messages(id)` with `UNIQUE(message_id)` defense-in-depth against double-send), `action_class` (text, ≤64 chars; one of the 11 entries in `ACTION_CLASSES` per ADR-034 §1), `tier_at_send` (one of `auto` / `draft_one_click` / `approve_every_time` / `auto_with_digest`), `template_hash` (SHA-256 hex), `per_send_body_sha256` (SHA-256 hex of the exact outbound body — server-derived from `messages.draft_preview`, not from client request), `recipient_id_hash` (SHA-256 hex of the recipient identifier — server-derived), `clicked_at`, `confirmed_typed` (boolean — `true` only for `approve_every_time` sends), `approval_signature_sha256` (canonical-JSON SHA-256 of `{founder_id, message_id, typed_value, per_send_body_sha256, recipient_id_hash, template_hash, tier_at_send}` — present only when `confirmed_typed=true`; reproducible from the persisted row, with `clicked_at` providing the wall-clock anchor separately), `grant_id` (UUID, FK to `public.scope_grants(id)` — pins the consent state active at the click). `messages.action_class` (text NULL with enum-absence CHECK matching `scope_grants` and `action_sends`) is the producer-declared literal added at migration 052 to bridge the producer→consumer hop. |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None. Body and recipient are stored as hashes (one-way SHA-256). The hashes are not directly attributable to natural persons without the input the founder typed; the input itself never enters the ledger. The `messages.draft_preview` column does carry text the founder reviewed, but it lives under the existing Processing Activity 2 (Conversation Data) retention bound, not this ledger. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(b) — performance of contract (T&C §3a "Agent Command Authority": the ledger is the substrate by which the founder's per-send authorization (1-click for `draft_one_click`, typed SEND for `approve_every_time`) and the platform's autonomous-tier execution under template-authorization (`auto_with_digest`) are recorded as Art. 5(2) accountability evidence). Art. 22(2)(c) explicit consent applies to the `approve_every_time` tier (the typed-SEND verbatim re-validation is the explicit-consent UI primitive at single-user-incident threshold). Art. 22(3) human-review affordance: founders may revoke a grant at any time at `/dashboard/settings/scope-grants`; for `auto_with_digest` infra.* actions the next-business-day digest review window (PR-I, #4078) IS the human-review path documented in Privacy Policy §8.3 and T&C §3a.2. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Soleur internal: founder-scoped Supabase reads via cookie-scoped RLS (`action_sends_owner_select` policy: `user_id = auth.uid()`); cookie-scoped writes via `action_sends_owner_insert` policy (the dashboard Send route at `apps/web-platform/app/api/dashboard/today/[id]/send/route.ts` is the only PR-H writer; PR-I will add the digest emitter as a second writer through the same `writeActionSend` helper per `hr-write-boundary-sentinel-sweep-all-write-sites`). **No third-party recipients.** No new sub-processors introduced by PR-H (the outbound delivery integrations — Bluesky API, marketing-email-blast, X publish, Slack DM — defer to PR-I and will register their own sub-processor entries when wired). |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **No transfer.** `action_sends` lives in `public` schema on Supabase eu-west-1 (existing Supabase row in the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping). PR-H ships no producer integrations — the outbound side of every entry that would touch a third-country processor (X, Bluesky, downstream marketing-email-blast vendors) is deferred to PR-I. |
| **(f) Retention** | `action_sends` rows: **append-only, retained indefinitely** as Art. 5(2) accountability evidence (the substrate of the founder's per-send consent + signature). Pure-reject WORM trigger (`action_sends_no_mutate`, mig 037 pattern — NO role-check bypass per learning 2026-05-18) on both UPDATE and DELETE; ERRCODE = P0001. Art. 17 erasure cascade via `anonymise_action_sends(p_user_id)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC: sets `user_id = NULL` and `recipient_id_hash = '__anonymised__'` on the user's rows via `SET LOCAL session_replication_role = 'replica'` to traverse the trigger (scoped to the function call). The cascade runs BEFORE `anonymise_scope_grants` at `server/account-delete.ts` (FK ordering: `action_sends.grant_id → scope_grants.id` is also `ON DELETE RESTRICT`). `messages.action_class` retention follows Processing Activity 2's parent `messages` table bound (no separate retention for the column). |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **Pure-reject WORM trigger** — `action_sends_no_mutate()` on UPDATE/DELETE raises P0001 unconditionally; ERRCODE'd for grep-discoverable forensics. Service-role does NOT bypass the trigger; only the dedicated SECURITY DEFINER `anonymise_action_sends` RPC traverses it via `session_replication_role`. (2) **RLS owner-only** — `action_sends_owner_select` and `action_sends_owner_insert` both pin `user_id = auth.uid()`; no FOR ALL USING (per learning 2026-04-18). (3) **DB CHECK enum-absence (defense-in-depth)** — `action_sends_action_class_not_locked`, `scope_grants_action_class_not_locked`, and `messages_action_class_not_locked` CHECK constraints reject any `action_class` matching `^(payment\|legal\|auth)\.` at the DB level. The 5th class (money/legal/credentials) is enforced by ABSENCE from `ACTION_CLASSES` at the TS layer (ADR-034 §2) PLUS this triple DB CHECK as belt-and-suspenders against indirect routes (RPC-from-JSON-payload, future config-file imports). Hard-rule lineage: `hr-menu-option-ack-not-prod-write-auth`. (4) **Server-derived signed surface** — the dashboard Send route derives `bodyContent` and `recipientIdentifier` from `messages.draft_preview` (NOT from client request body), so a compromised client cannot bind the approval signature to content the founder never saw. The signature payload covers `{founder_id, message_id, typed_value, per_send_body_sha256, recipient_id_hash, template_hash, tier_at_send}` (no `ts` — reproducible from the persisted row). (5) **Server-side typed-confirm re-validation + draft-hash echo** — for `approve_every_time` tier, the route handler verifies `confirmed_typed === true && typed_value === "SEND"` (case-sensitive, NO `.trim()` / `.normalize()` per Kieran P2-7) AND requires the client to echo `expected_draft_preview_hash` returned in the prior 409 response; the server re-reads the message row and rejects with a fresh 409 on hash mismatch (closes the Send→Edit→Send race). The client-side typed-confirm modal is the first defense (UX); the server re-validation + hash binding is the load-bearing TOM (security). (6) **Cookie-scoped client at click-time** — the dashboard Send route passes the cookie-scoped Supabase client to `isGranted` (Kieran P1-3 / AC12); RLS `scope_grants_owner_select` lets the founder self-read. Service-role is NOT used in the founder-initiated path; service-role remains the webhook-context-only credential. (7) **Re-checked at click-time grant** — the Send route re-calls `isGranted` at the moment the founder clicks Send, NOT at message-write time. A revocation race between draft and click fails closed (TR8). The `ActiveGrant` return now carries `id` alongside `tier` so the route reads `scope_grants` once per click (no duplicate SELECT race). (8) **UNIQUE constraints fail-closed on double-write** — `UNIQUE INDEX action_sends_message_unique` on `action_sends(message_id)` produces 23505 unique_violation on founder double-click / retry / archive-after-write split-brain; the route surfaces it as 409 already_sent. Partial `UNIQUE INDEX scope_grants_active_unique ON scope_grants(founder_id, action_class) WHERE revoked_at IS NULL` closes the concurrent-POST race in `grant_action_class`. (9) **Single write boundary** — every `action_sends` INSERT routes through `server/action-sends/write-action-send.ts` per `hr-write-boundary-sentinel-sweep-all-write-sites`. (10) **Code-static action-class registry** — `ACTION_CLASSES` is a frozen `as const` literal union (ADR-034 §1); producers declare the literal at the `isGranted` / `inngest.send` boundary. Lint test (`test/lint/action-class-typed-literals.test.ts`) rejects raw `string`/`any`-typed callers. Exhaustive `switch` + `assertNeverTier` at the route ensures a 5th tier addition trips tsc before reaching CI. (11) **Bounded backfill** — `messages.action_class` backfill on PR-F CFO drafts is bounded by `created_at < <deploy-ts>` (Kieran P2-1) so a future producer emitting a different class is not retro-labeled. |

ADR-034 (`knowledge-base/engineering/architecture/decisions/ADR-034-action-class-registry-static-literals-and-enum-absence.md`) records the registry decision (code-static literal union over runtime classifier / config-file registry / DB-table registry) and the 5th-class enum-absence enforcement.

## Processing Activity 18 — Template-authorization ledger (template_authorizations, PR-I #4078)

(Plan §Phase 10.1 referred to this as "PA-16" — the number was already taken by the action_sends ledger above. Numbered PA-18 to preserve the existing register's ordering without renaming a published PA.)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Record per-template authorization scope so that every `draft_one_click` send made under PR-H's `action_sends` ledger gates on a NOT NULL bounded `template_authorizations` row keyed by (founder_id, template_hash). The Send click on a labeled `draft_one_click` button IS the Art. 7(3) "specific" + "informed" consent act (first-send-IS-authorization, plan §Phase 4 §2). Subsequent sends gate on this row's bounds (`expires_at`, `max_sends`). Founder-initiated revoke via `/dashboard/settings/scope-grants` provides Art. 7(3) "as easily withdrawable as given". Brand-survival threshold: `single-user incident`. PR-I #4078; ADR-035. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Founder-operators (Soleur users on the Web Platform). NOT recipients of any outbound. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | `template_authorizations`: `id` (UUID), `founder_id` (UUID, FK to `public.users(id)` ON DELETE RESTRICT — nullable to admit Art. 17 anonymise), `template_hash` (SHA-256 hex of the canonical body_template; derived from the code-static `TEMPLATE_REGISTRY` at `apps/web-platform/server/templates/template-registry.ts`), `action_class` (text, ≤64 chars, same enum-absence regex as scope_grants / action_sends), `authorized_at`, `expires_at` (NOT NULL DEFAULT now()+90d — provisional bound; calibration deferred to #4217), `soft_reconfirm_at` (NOT NULL DEFAULT now()+30d), `max_sends` (NOT NULL DEFAULT 100), `revoked_at`, `revocation_reason` (paired-null + CHECK ∈ 8-value enum: `founder_revoked`, `quota_exhausted`, `expired`, `dsr_erasure`, `regulator_ordered`, `vendor_tos_revoked`, `policy_violation`, `quarantine_retroactive`), `grant_id` (UUID, FK to `public.scope_grants(id)` ON DELETE RESTRICT — pins the parent grant), `created_at`. `messages.template_id` (text, NOT NULL, shape CHECK `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`, length 1-64) is the producer-side discriminator; backfill to `'default_legacy'` for legacy rows. |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None. The hash is one-way SHA-256 over a code-static body_template; the body_template never includes any natural-person personal data (it is a pre-personalisation template). |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(b) — performance of contract (T&C §3a "Agent Command Authority"). The first-send-IS-authorization pattern reads under Art. 7(3): the founder's Send click on a labeled `draft_one_click` button — with PR-H's typed-confirm context for higher-friction tiers — constitutes informed consent. Art. 22(3) human-review affordance: founders may revoke a template authorization at any time from `/dashboard/settings/scope-grants`; the scope-grants page lists active authorizations with per-row Revoke buttons (component `template-authorization-row.tsx`). |
| **(d) Recipients** | Soleur internal: founder-scoped Supabase reads via cookie-scoped RLS (`template_authorizations_owner_select` policy: `founder_id = auth.uid()`); cookie-scoped writes via `template_authorizations_owner_insert` policy (no direct UPDATE/DELETE policies — all mutations route through SECURITY DEFINER RPCs that bypass the WORM trigger via `SET LOCAL session_replication_role='replica'`). **No third-party recipients.** No new sub-processors. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **No transfer.** `template_authorizations` lives in `public` schema on Supabase eu-west-1 (existing Supabase row in the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping). |
| **(f) Retention** | **Append-only, retained indefinitely** as Art. 5(2) accountability evidence. Pure-reject WORM trigger (`template_authorizations_no_mutate`, mig 053; mirrors mig 051 §(e)) on UPDATE/DELETE; ERRCODE = P0001. Art. 17 erasure cascade via `anonymise_template_authorizations(p_user_id)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC: sets `founder_id = NULL`, `revoked_at = COALESCE(revoked_at, now())`, `revocation_reason = COALESCE(revocation_reason, 'dsr_erasure')` via `SET LOCAL session_replication_role = 'replica'` (scoped to the function call). Cascade ordering at `server/account-delete.ts`: `anonymise_action_sends` → **anonymise_template_authorizations** → `anonymise_scope_grants` → `anonymise_tc_acceptances` → `auth.admin.deleteUser`. Ordering is SEMANTIC, not FK-driven (anonymise_* is UPDATE; ON DELETE RESTRICT does not fire) — `dsr_erasure` reason must land on child rows BEFORE the parent scope_grant's founder_id is nulled, otherwise Art. 5(2) attribution breaks. `messages.template_id` retention follows Processing Activity 2's parent `messages` table bound. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **Pure-reject WORM trigger** — `template_authorizations_no_mutate()` on UPDATE/DELETE raises P0001 unconditionally; only the dedicated SECURITY DEFINER RPCs (`authorize_template`, `revoke_template_authorization`, `anonymise_template_authorizations`) traverse it via `session_replication_role`. (2) **RLS owner-only** — owner-select + owner-insert pin `founder_id = auth.uid()`; no `FOR UPDATE` / `FOR DELETE` policies. (3) **Partial-UNIQUE first-writer-wins** — `template_authorizations_active_unique (founder_id, template_hash) WHERE revoked_at IS NULL` enforces "one active row per (founder, template_hash)"; `authorize_template` catches 23505 unique_violation and returns the winner's id (idempotent — learning 2026-05-03). (4) **NOT NULL bounded columns** — `expires_at`, `soft_reconfirm_at`, `max_sends` are NOT NULL with provisional defaults; the row cannot be inserted without bounds (Art. 7(3) "specific" — no unbounded consent). (5) **8-value revocation_reason CHECK** — paired-null + IN-list CHECK at the DB layer; mirrored by `REVOCATION_REASON_COPY` in `apps/web-platform/lib/messages/trust-tier-copy.ts`. Parity test `test/server/scope-grants/revocation-reason-exhaustive.test.ts` reads the mig 053 source and asserts set-equality with TS keys. The 8-value over-provision (vs. Simplicity's request to cut to 5) preserves Art. 5(2) attribution distinguishability for future revocation drivers. (6) **Single-query two-probe predicate** — `isTemplateAuthorized` at `apps/web-platform/server/templates/is-template-authorized.ts` runs AFTER `isGranted` for `draft_one_click` tier only; unconditional parallel fetch of the most-recent row + action_sends count; TS branches on the result. Fail-closed `PredicateException` on any DB error → 500 + Sentry capture (`kind:template_predicate_timeout`). 5s `Promise.race` timeout. (7) **First-send-IS-authorization** — on no-existing-row, the send route calls `authorize_template` RPC then proceeds to write `action_sends` in the same request; the Send click IS the consent act (plan §Phase 4 §2 + ADR-035). (8) **Auto-revoke best-effort side effect** — when the predicate detects an expired or quota-exhausted row with `revoked_at IS NULL`, it fires `revoke_template_authorization` fire-and-forget so the UI does not display lying rows. Failure logs via `warnSilentFallback` (pino + Sentry-warn) but does NOT mask the denial. (9) **Pino structured denial log** — every predicate denial emits `{template_hash, action_class, deny_reason, founder_id_hash}` to pino; NO Sentry mirror on routine denials per ADR-035 §Observability (denials are expected behavior under Art. 7(3), not silent fallbacks). (10) **No `current_user='service_role'` bypass anywhere** — every WORM-traversing path routes through `SET LOCAL session_replication_role='replica'` per learning 2026-05-18 (the broken pattern from mig 050). The `anonymise_template_authorizations` RPC uses `current_user` only OUTSIDE the trigger as an auth gate (gated by `auth.uid() IS NULL`) — mirrors mig 051 §(h) anonymise_action_sends precedent. (11) **Code-static template registry** — `TEMPLATE_IDS = ['default_legacy'] as const`, `TEMPLATE_REGISTRY` Record + `satisfies` parity (mirror of ADR-034's `ACTION_CLASSES` pattern). A new template requires a code edit + a passing test — not runtime-mintable (Art. 7(3) "specific"). ADR-035 records the decision. |

ADR-035 (`knowledge-base/engineering/architecture/decisions/ADR-035-template-registry-code-static.md`) records the registry decision (mirror of ADR-034 for template_hash discrimination).

## Processing Activity 19 — Workspace Member Removal Audit Ledger (`workspace_member_removals`, PR #4294)

(feat-dsar-departed-member-coverage, #4230. Mirrors the WORM-ledger substrate established by PA-PII Activities 14, 16, 18 — adapted from the per-template / per-action shape to the per-membership-event shape. Lands alongside the legal-scaffolding refresh that PR #4289 introduces for the team-workspace umbrella #4229.)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Maintain a tamper-evident record of every workspace-member removal event (workspace_id, removed_user_id, removed_by_user_id, removed_at) so (a) the removed user can exercise GDPR Art. 15 / 17 / 20 over their removal lineage after leaving a workspace they were previously a member of; (b) the workspace owner has a litigation-discovery audit trail evidencing wrongful-removal allegations; (c) Art. 30(1)(g) processing-lifecycle records cover the leave event symmetrically to the join event captured in `workspace_member_attestations` (PA-PII Activity not separately registered; tracked as a sub-activity of PA-1 + PA-2 team-workspace substrate). Brand-survival threshold: `single-user incident` — a departed member receiving an incomplete DSAR export, or a single cross-tenant leak of "person X removed person Y on $date" via a buggy RLS predicate, is brand-survival-relevant. ADR-039. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Two: (1) removed members (rows where `removed_user_id` matches the data subject); (2) workspace owners who performed the removal (rows where `removed_by_user_id` matches). The Art. 15 owner-of-record for the row is the removed user; the actor sees their own outgoing removals via the same allowlist entry but the row is keyed on `removed_user_id`. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | `workspace_member_removals`: `id` (UUID, PK — strictly immutable lineage), `workspace_id` (UUID, NULLable, FK to `public.workspaces(id)` ON DELETE SET NULL — orphan-org cleanup carve-out per ADR-039 §Invariants.1; the sister-table `workspace_member_attestations.workspace_id` at 058:43 keeps its pre-existing RESTRICT shape, tracked separately), `removed_user_id` (UUID, NULLable, FK to `public.users(id)` ON DELETE RESTRICT — PII column), `removed_by_user_id` (UUID, NULLable, FK to `public.users(id)` ON DELETE RESTRICT — PII column), `removed_at` (timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now() — strictly immutable lineage). No free-text content, no IP hashes, no user-agent — the removal event is purely structural-relational; severity is below `workspace_member_attestations` (which carries `attestation_text` + `ip_hash` + `user_agent`). |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None. Workspace membership is not a Special Category. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(c) — legal obligation: controllers must maintain records of processing activities affecting data subjects across the data lifecycle (Art. 30(1)). Removal from a workspace is a processing-relevant lifecycle event. Art. 5(2) accountability is the operational substrate for any downstream Art. 82(2) compensation claim. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Soleur internal: workspace co-members via cookie-scoped RLS (`removals_select_for_members` policy: `is_workspace_member(workspace_id, auth.uid())`). Departed members do **not** see their own removal row via this RLS policy (`is_workspace_member` returns FALSE post-removal) — they reach it through the DSAR export pipeline, which runs under service-role with `assertReadScope(rows, expectedUserId, "workspace_member_removals", { ownerField: "removed_user_id" })`. Rationale in ADR-039 §Invariants.4: the row is co-member audit metadata, not the departed user's profile data; the gated surface for the departed user is the DSAR bundle, not the live workspace UI. **No third-party recipients.** No new sub-processors. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **No transfer.** `workspace_member_removals` lives in `public` schema on Supabase eu-west-1 (existing Supabase row in the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping). |
| **(f) Retention** | **36 months on `removed_at`**, then row-state-bypassed pg_cron DELETE. Deviates from the 24-month PA-PII envelope tracked in `compliance-posture.md` for `dsar_export_audit_pii`. Rationale: Art. 82(2) limitation horizon varies by member-state (FR Code Civil Art. 2224 = 5y; DE BGB §195 = 3y; UK Limitation Act 1980 §11A data-protection = 6y); 36 months covers the shortest-jurisdiction floor (DE 3y) without indefinite PII surface. ADR-039 records the rationale. Art. 17 erasure cascade via `anonymise_workspace_member_removals(p_user_id)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC: NULLs `removed_user_id` AND `removed_by_user_id`, preserves lineage (`id`, `workspace_id`, `removed_at`). Cascade ordering at `server/account-delete.ts`: `anonymise_workspace_member_attestations` (3.90) → **anonymise_workspace_member_removals (3.905)** → `anonymise_workspace_members` (3.91) → `anonymise_organization_membership` (3.92) → `auth.admin.deleteUser`. Both FK columns are ON DELETE RESTRICT — skipping 3.905 would leave Art. 17 erasure broken for any user who has ever been removed from a workspace. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **WORM trigger** — `workspace_member_removals_no_mutate()` on BEFORE UPDATE + BEFORE DELETE. Two bypass paths: (a) UPDATE: structural-shape — lineage columns immutable; PII columns transition NOT NULL → NULL only (mirrors 058's pattern per learning 2026-05-18-worm-trigger-bypass-role-check-fails-under-postgrest-routing); (b) DELETE: row-state — TG_OP='DELETE' AND OLD.removed_at < now() - interval '36 months' (mirrors 043's pattern per learning 2026-05-15-worm-trigger-blocks-pg-cron-retention-sweep — pg_cron runs as `postgres`, not `service_role`, so role-gated bypasses silently fail). (2) **REVOKE INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated on the table** — no owner-insert RLS policy (per learning 2026-05-21-worm-ledger-rls-owner-insert-policy-is-an-rpc-bypass). All inserts route through EXACTLY TWO SECURITY DEFINER bodies: `remove_workspace_member` (revocation_reason='removed') AND `update_workspace_member_role` (revocation_reason='role-changed', mig 067 / #4307); verified by `grep -rE "INSERT INTO public.workspace_member_removals" apps/web-platform/supabase/migrations/` returning hits only in `062_workspace_member_removals_and_remove_rpc_update.sql` and `067_workspace_member_revocation_lookup.sql`, AND the migration-shape lint at `test/supabase-migrations/067-workspace-member-revocation-lookup.test.ts` enforcing exactly two `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` bodies in mig 067 contain that INSERT (F1, #4307 plan-review). (3) **Atomic INSERT-then-DELETE inside `remove_workspace_member` SECURITY DEFINER body** — plpgsql function semantics guarantee that an uncaught exception (FK violation, constraint violation) on the INSERT aborts and rolls back the DELETE in the same transaction (AC2; verified at migration-shape lint in `test/supabase-migrations/062-workspace-member-removals.test.ts`). (4) **`anonymise_workspace_member_removals(p_user_id)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC** — REVOKE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT EXECUTE TO service_role only. Only the account-delete cascade can invoke. Pinned `search_path = public, pg_temp` per `cq-pg-security-definer-search-path-pin-pg-temp`. (5) **Row-state retention sweep** — pg_cron 'workspace-member-removals-retention-sweep' daily at 04:00 issues `DELETE FROM workspace_member_removals WHERE removed_at < now() - interval '36 months'`; the trigger's DELETE bypass allows only those rows. (6) **DSAR allowlist + per-row WHERE** — `dsar-export-allowlist.ts` entry `{ ownerField: "removed_user_id", article: "15" }`; `dsar-export.ts` adds the export block keyed on `.eq("removed_user_id", expectedUserId)`; `assertReadScope` enforces per-row ownership match before the row enters the bundle. (7) **Symmetric attestation export co-change** — pair-edit: `dsar-export.ts` switches `workspace_member_attestations` query from `.eq("invitee_user_id", X)` to `.or("invitee_user_id.eq.X,inviter_user_id.eq.X")` with two-arm defensive scope check so the departed user's INVITER-side rows (e.g., they invited Bob before leaving) appear in their bundle. Otherwise the inviter-side rows would be silently omitted. (Kieran P1-1.) (8) **Cascade-order defense-in-depth** — `anonymise_workspace_member_removals` runs BEFORE `auth.admin.deleteUser()` per the existing 058 cascade pattern; failure to thread this order leaves Art. 17 erasure broken on the new ON DELETE RESTRICT FKs. (9) **Single-jurisdiction storage + Supabase RLS substrate** — workspace_member_removals lives in `public` schema, EU-only; same Supabase row as the rest of the personal-data plane. (10) **Revocation lookup at middleware** — `revoked_after timestamptz` + `revocation_reason text` columns (mig 067 / #4307) populated by `remove_workspace_member` AND `update_workspace_member_role` RPCs at INSERT-time. Middleware at `apps/web-platform/middleware.ts` calls `public.check_my_revocation(jwt_iat)` on every authenticated request — per-request lookup, no cache, fail-CLOSED 503 on DB unavailability. User-global predicate (no `current_organization_id` dependency) closes the multi-workspace cross-leak vector documented in #4307 plan-review F5. Together with the Custom Access Token Hook's natural-refresh re-evaluation (mig 060) AND the same RPCs' atomic clear of `user_session_state.current_organization_id` (F6), this closes the Art. 6 lawful-basis termination window AND the Art. 32 entitlement-change confidentiality TOM gap noted at #4307. |

## Processing Activity 20 — Workspace membership audit log (`workspace_member_actions`, #4231)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(b) Purposes** | Append-only audit trail for every workspace membership mutation (add, remove, role-change) on `public.workspace_members`. Answers regulator inquiries ("did the controller — workspace owner — manage member access lawfully?" under Art. 5(2) accountability), member-dispute scenarios ("Harry says he was never removed"), and SOC2/SOX forensic reconstruction. Blocks the `TEAM_WORKSPACE_INVITE_ENABLED` flag-flip for any non-jikigai org until in place. **Controller/Processor split:** the workspace owner is the controller for membership data within their workspace; Soleur is the processor (per parent #4229 Decision 11). Sibling to PA-19 (`workspace_member_removals`, #4230 / PR #4294) which covers the per-removal event-class; PA-20 covers the per-mutation full-history class (add + remove + role-change) on `workspace_members` directly. Brand-survival threshold: `single-user incident` — one mis-written owner-check predicate that leaks one workspace's membership history to a peer-workspace member is unrecoverable for trust. #4231; ADR-038 (PA-2 / PA-18 sibling). **#4307 amendment (mig 067):** role-change events route through the new `update_workspace_member_role` SECURITY DEFINER RPC. The RPC issues `PERFORM set_config('workspace_audit.actor_user_id', auth.uid()::text, true)` at body top so the existing PA-20 §(g)(3) trigger-driven writer captures the actor instead of NULL (F2). |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Workspace members (controllers' employees / contractors / collaborators). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | `workspace_member_actions`: `id` (UUID), `workspace_id` (UUID, FK `public.workspaces(id)` ON DELETE RESTRICT), `actor_user_id` (UUID, FK `public.users(id)` ON DELETE RESTRICT — **NULLable** to admit Art. 17 anonymise), `target_user_id` (UUID, FK `public.users(id)` ON DELETE RESTRICT — NULLable), `action_type` (text, CHECK ∈ {`added`, `removed`, `role_changed`}), `old_role` / `new_role` (text, NULLable), `attestation_id` (UUID, FK `public.workspace_member_attestations(id)` ON DELETE RESTRICT — NULLable), `created_at` (NOT NULL DEFAULT now()). PII columns are UUID identifiers + the role enum + the controller-derived `workspace_id`. |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None. |
| **Lawful basis** | **Art. 6(1)(c)** — legal obligation under Art. 5(2) accountability (controllers must demonstrate lawful processing of membership decisions on demand). **Art. 6(1)(f)** — legitimate interest in operational integrity, forensic reconstruction across enterprise records-retention windows, and protection of data subjects from controller misconduct. **LIA balancing test:** (a) data is internal-use-only — NEVER used for product analytics / sales / ML training / feature decisions (purpose-limitation clause); (b) 7-year retention is bounded by SOX evidentiary horizon, not open-ended; (c) data subjects retain Art. 15 / 17 / 20 rights via the DSAR cascade (export + anonymise); (d) the audit trail itself **protects data subjects** from undocumented controller decisions — the asymmetry favours processing. |
| **(d) Recipients** | Soleur internal: workspace owner via `list_workspace_member_actions(p_workspace_id, p_limit, p_cursor, p_cursor_id)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC — owner-check joins `organizations.owner_user_id` through `workspaces.organization_id`, returns empty (no error, no table-existence leak) for non-owners. Sentry (operational alerts, pseudonymised per TR13: `workspace_id` + `action_type` only, NEVER `target_user_id`) + Better Stack (Supabase log routing for `audit_orphan_actor` warnings and `audit_retention_purge` cron run-cadence). No third-party recipients. No new sub-processors. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **No transfer.** `workspace_member_actions` lives in `public` schema on Supabase eu-west-1 (existing Supabase row in the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping). Sentry telemetry routes to the DE region (existing Sentry sub-processor row). |
| **(f) Retention** | **7 years.** Rationale: SOX evidentiary horizon for accountability under Art. 5(2), enabling forensic reconstruction across the longest plausible enterprise records-retention period a customer might invoke. Defensible against Art. 5(1)(e) data-minimisation challenge — 7y is bounded, not open-ended; the alternative (indefinite retention, as for PA-18 attestations) would not be proportionate for the mutation-frequency expected from this table. `pg_cron` job `workspace-member-actions-retention` runs daily at 04:00 UTC and invokes `purge_workspace_member_actions()` (SECURITY DEFINER wrapper with `SET LOCAL session_replication_role='replica'` so the pure-reject WORM trigger does not silently block the DELETE — see learning `2026-05-15-worm-trigger-blocks-pg-cron-retention-sweep.md`). Observability via `cron.job_run_details` (auto-populated) + `RAISE LOG 'audit_retention_purge ...'`. **Art. 17 erasure cascade** via `anonymise_workspace_member_actions(p_user_id)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC: NULL-sets `actor_user_id` + `target_user_id` for every row referencing the departing user; lineage columns (`id`, `workspace_id`, `action_type`, `old_role`, `new_role`, `created_at`, `attestation_id`) preserved. Idempotent. Cascade ordering at `server/account-delete.ts`: `anonymise_organization_membership` (3.92) → **anonymise_workspace_member_actions** (3.93) → `auth.admin.deleteUser` (4). FK is `public.users(id)` ON DELETE RESTRICT — the cascade would abort without anonymisation. **Art. 16 rectification** is NOT supported by design: append-only WORM has no rectification mechanism (would require either a `corrects_id` column or a parallel correction-row pattern; both are v2 designs and the v1 use case has no observed need). PA-20's Art. 6(1)(c) + 6(1)(f) basis supports a derogation argument under Art. 23 for proportionate restriction of Art. 16 where rectification would defeat the audit's evidentiary purpose. Re-evaluate when the first regulator/customer requests rectification. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **RLS-zero-policies + named-role REVOKE matrix** — `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` + zero `CREATE POLICY` + explicit `REVOKE INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, SELECT FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated, service_role` (per learning `2026-05-06-supabase-default-privileges-defeat-revoke-from-public`). All reads route through the owner-check SECURITY DEFINER RPC; all writes are trigger-driven from `public.workspace_members`. (2) **Pure-reject WORM trigger** — `workspace_member_actions_no_mutate()` BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE raises P0001 unconditionally; bypass via `SET LOCAL session_replication_role='replica'` in the three SECURITY DEFINER wrapper RPCs (`anonymise_workspace_member_actions`, `purge_workspace_member_actions`, the in-migration backfill block). Pattern source: mig 037 `audit_byok_use_no_mutate` + mig 051 `action_sends_no_mutate`. NO `current_user='service_role'` role-check branch anywhere (per learning `2026-05-18-worm-trigger-bypass-role-check-fails-under-postgrest-routing.md`). (3) **Trigger-driven writer (impossible-to-forget)** — `workspace_members_audit` AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `workspace_members` SECURITY DEFINER, `SET search_path = public, pg_temp`. Reads actor via the `workspace_audit.actor_user_id` GUC set by `invite_workspace_member` + `remove_workspace_member` (re-CREATEd in mig 063 to prepend `PERFORM set_config('workspace_audit.actor_user_id', COALESCE(auth.uid()::text, ''), true)`; `set_config` form is mandatory because `SET LOCAL <key> = <expr>` rejects non-literals). Trigger NEVER falls back to `auth.uid()` — under SECURITY DEFINER context `auth.uid()` returns the definer (postgres), silently capturing the wrong actor; NULL is the correct empty value (TR10a). (4) **Owner-only reader RPC** — `list_workspace_member_actions(p_workspace_id, p_limit, p_cursor, p_cursor_id)` SECURITY DEFINER; keyset-cursor paginated `(created_at, id)` to disambiguate same-tick rows from bulk-insert audit emissions; owner-check joins `organizations.owner_user_id` through `workspaces.organization_id`; returns empty (no error, no leak) for non-owners and nonexistent workspaces. (5) **Sentry mirror on orphan-actor signal** — when the trigger reads NULL for the actor GUC AND `session_user = 'authenticated'` (origin-role caller, not service_role/postgres), emits `RAISE LOG 'audit_orphan_actor workspace_id=% action=%'` (PII-scrubbed; `target_user_id` deliberately omitted per GDPR T-06 fold-in). Routes through Supabase logs → Vector → Better Stack; alerts on >5 events/24h. Production-NULL from authenticated is the silent-audit-gap regression signal for a future RPC that mutates `workspace_members` without setting the GUC. (6) **Backfill bypass + concurrent-write protection** — migration backfill block uses `LOCK TABLE public.workspace_members IN SHARE MODE` (permits SELECT, blocks INSERT) so concurrent app-server writes during the migration window are not double-audited (once via the new AFTER trigger, once via the backfill SELECT); `SET LOCAL lock_timeout='30s'` + `SET LOCAL statement_timeout='5min'` bound the apply window; `SET LOCAL session_replication_role='replica'` for the WORM bypass; `NOT EXISTS` discriminator for idempotency on re-apply; ASSERT on (backfilled + pre-existing) == workspace_members count enforces AC8 parity. (7) **Anonymise cascade re-CREATE** — mig 063 re-`CREATE OR REPLACE`s mig 058's `anonymise_workspace_members` to prepend `SET LOCAL session_replication_role='replica'` before its DELETE, so the new AFTER trigger does NOT fire during the account-delete cascade. Without this, the cascade DELETE would create net-new `removed` audit rows with `target_user_id=<userId>` for a user requesting Art. 17 erasure — orphan PII if step 3.93 then failed. (8) **DSAR cascade three-file wiring** — `dsar-export-allowlist.ts` registers the table with `additionalOwnerFields: ['target_user_id']` for OR-semantics; `dsar-export.ts` reads via two `.eq()` chains (one per column) with per-chain `assertReadScope` and merge-dedupe; `account-delete.ts` step 3.93 invokes `anonymise_workspace_member_actions` BEFORE `auth.admin.deleteUser` (fatal-on-failure given the RESTRICT FK). Lint extension at `test/dsar-worker-per-row-where.test.ts` accepts `.eq()` on any declared owner column AND inverse-lints that every declared column has ≥1 read chain (catches the Art. 15 silent-drop regression class). (9) **Retention purge observability** — `purge_workspace_member_actions()` emits `RAISE LOG 'audit_retention_purge table=workspace_member_actions deleted_count=%'`; `cron.job_run_details` records start/end timestamps + return value; Better Stack monitor (post-merge, configured per `knowledge-base/engineering/operations/runbooks/cron-retention-monitor.md`) pages on missing successful run in 26h — a silently-skipped retention sweep would be an undetected Art. 5(1)(e) breach. (10) **Sentinel sweep CI** — `apps/web-platform/scripts/check-workspace-members-write-sites.sh` `git grep`-enumerates every INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on `public.workspace_members` under `apps/web-platform/` and asserts each routes through a GUC-setting SECURITY DEFINER RPC or is a documented admin-tool / test-fixture path with NULL-actor expectation. (11) **Purpose limitation clause** — data shall NOT be used for product analytics, sales, ML training, feature decisions, or operational research outside the named audit purpose; LIA balancing test depends on this constraint and any deviation re-opens the legitimate-interest analysis. (12) **No `current_user='service_role'` bypass anywhere** — every WORM-traversing path routes through `SET LOCAL session_replication_role='replica'` (mirrors PA-18 §(g)(10)). |

ADR-038 (`knowledge-base/engineering/architecture/decisions/ADR-038-workspace-membership-substrate.md`) records the workspace-membership substrate decisions (parent design; PA-20 inherits the SECURITY DEFINER + named-role REVOKE conventions).
