## Processing Activity 31 — Anthropic-egressing Inngest function fleet (#7100)

(feat-one-shot-7100-art30-eval-fleet-register. Authored to close the Art. 30(1) incompleteness found by counsel review `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-07-counsel-review-7086.md` § Finding 1 — "the register carries PA-1 through PA-30 and **no entry covers the claude-eval cron fleet at all** … That is a live Art. 30(1) incompleteness, not a stylistic one." The register's own § 0 already declares `jikig-ai/soleur` an in-scope surface. Jikigai is **controller** and Anthropic PBC is an Art. 28 **processor**: every member runs under the Jikigai-controlled `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` under the ADR-033 **I2** invariant stated in each file header — "Operator `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` only; never founder BYOK", build-time-enforced by `apps/web-platform/test/server/cron-no-byok-lease-sweep.test.ts` — so this is **not** the BYOK independent-controller carve-out at § 0 "Out-of-scope". This entry covers the **internal egress** operation only; publication to a public surface is a distinct operation under Art. 4(2) and is recorded separately at **PA-32**, which two of these members also appear in for that other operation. The title says "function fleet", not "cron fleet", because three of the 21 members are not crons. **Brand-survival threshold: single-user incident.**)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(a) Purpose** | Operate Jikigai's own engineering, product, marketing, legal and community functions by transmitting repository content, GitHub issue and pull-request content, community-platform content and workflow state to the Anthropic API for model reasoning under the Jikigai-controlled `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. **Scope predicate — membership is by CREDENTIAL AND TRANSPORT, not by helper name:** every Inngest function module in `apps/web-platform/server/inngest/functions/` matching `{cron,oneshot,event}-*.ts` that reaches the Anthropic API under the Jikigai-controlled `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, whether by spawning the Claude Code CLI (`spawnClaudeEval`, or an inline spawn via `resolveClaudeBin`) or by direct HTTPS call to `api.anthropic.com/v1/messages` (`postAnthropicMessage`). Content categories are a **property of members**, not the membership criterion — a content test is not mechanically checkable and would exclude `cron-anthropic-credit-probe`, whose payload is the literal `"ping"`, while the snapshot includes it. **Dated member snapshot — 21 modules, measured 2026-07-31** on this branch; two independent predicates (`/ANTHROPIC_API_KEY/` and `/spawnClaudeEval\s*\(\|resolveClaudeBin\s*\(\|postAnthropicMessage\s*\(/`) agree **byte-for-byte**, and every command with its output is recorded in the LIA provenance section: `cron-agent-native-audit`, `cron-anthropic-credit-probe`, `cron-architecture-diagram-sync`, `cron-bug-fixer`, `cron-campaign-calendar`, `cron-community-monitor`, `cron-competitive-analysis`, `cron-compound-promote`, `cron-content-generator`, `cron-daily-triage`, `cron-follow-through-monitor`, `cron-growth-audit`, `cron-growth-execution`, `cron-legal-audit`, `cron-roadmap-review`, `cron-seo-aeo-audit`, `cron-ux-audit`, `cron-weekly-release-digest`, `event-ship-merge`, `oneshot-f2-defer-gate-review`, `oneshot-recheck-4217-calibration`. Of these, **18 reach Anthropic through the Claude Code CLI** — **16** via `spawnClaudeEval`, and **2 (`cron-daily-triage`, `cron-follow-through-monitor`) via an inline `spawn(resolveClaudeBin(), …)` that bypasses the helper** — and **3 by direct HTTPS** (`cron-anthropic-credit-probe.ts:83`, `cron-weekly-release-digest.ts:259`, `cron-compound-promote.ts:589`). 18 + 3 = 21, so the breakdown closes over the snapshot. **[2026-07-31 CORRECTION (#7100), applied at review.** This sentence previously read "15 reach Anthropic through the Claude Code CLI — 13 via `spawnClaudeEval`", which sums with the 3 HTTPS members to **18, not 21**, leaving three registered members with no stated transport. 13 and 15 are correct **cron-scoped** figures — they are what the LIA provenance table and `anthropic.md` measure, both of which pin their commands to `"$D"/cron-*.ts` — but this cell's antecedent is the 21-**module** snapshot, which includes three non-crons. Scoped to `cron-*.ts` alone the figures remain 15 CLI (13 + 2); scoped to the 21 members they are 18 CLI (16 + 2). The three members the old wording dropped are exactly the three this entry's own preamble names as the reason the title says "function fleet" and not "cron fleet": `event-ship-merge`, `oneshot-f2-defer-gate-review` and `oneshot-recheck-4217-calibration`, each of which calls `spawnClaudeEval`. Correcting a scope-mismatched figure with another scope-mismatched figure is the failure this entry exists to end. **]** **20** modules import `_cron-claude-eval-substrate` — that count is **not** scoped to the 21 members: it is 18 of the members plus `cron-skill-freshness` (an importer that makes no Anthropic call, excluded at (iv) below) plus a type-only import in `_cron-shared.ts`. The three direct-HTTPS members import `_cron-shared`, not the substrate. The figure repeated downstream as "15 crons call `spawnClaudeEval`" is **wrong**: 13 do. "15 crons egress to Anthropic via the CLI" is correct, and scoping this record by the boundary crossed rather than by the function name that usually crosses it is why the two direct spawners are inside it. **Deliberate exclusions, each with its reason, so the next reader who re-runs the grep does not conclude the register is incomplete again:** (i) `agent-on-spawn-requested` — runs under the **operator's BYOK** key, not the Jikigai key → recorded at **PA-22**; (ii) `cron-anthropic-cost-report` — dials `api.anthropic.com/v1/organizations/cost_report` under a **different credential**, `ANTHROPIC_ADMIN_KEY`, and *receives* org-billing aggregates rather than transmitting repository, community or personal data; (iii) GitHub Actions workflows carrying the same Jikigai key → recorded at **PA-33**; (iv) `cron-skill-freshness` — clones the repository through the shared substrate helpers but invokes **no** Anthropic call (its header records "I2 — Trivially satisfied: no claude"). `cron-workspace-gc` names the substrate in a comment only and is not an importer. |
| **(b) Categories of data subjects** | Three tiers, with materially different Art. 14 postures. **(i) Repository / engineering population** — commit authors, issue authors, pull-request authors and reviewers on `jikig-ai/soleur`, whose content is already published in a public repository. Art. 14 is engaged and is substantially satisfied by context; the natural channel (README / CONTRIBUTING / issue templates) is tracked at deferred item **DEF-1** (#7120). **(ii) Community population — involuntary third-party data subjects** — members of the Jikigai-operated Discord guild (a **semi-private** source: readable only with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`), Hacker News posters, GitHub issue/PR commenters, and GitHub stargazers. They have no relationship with Jikigai and did not supply their data to Jikigai. **Art. 14 is ENGAGED, UNDISCHARGED and OVERDUE**: Art. 14(3)(a) required the information within one month of obtaining, the earliest committed digest is `knowledge-base/support/community/2026-02-19-digest.md`, so the obligation has been unmet since approximately **2026-03-19** and accrues on each run of the live limb. **No Art. 14(5) exemption is claimed** — 14(5)(a) is unavailable, and 14(5)(b) disproportionate effort is **not established** for Discord members (Jikigai operates the guild; a pinned notice costs nothing) or for GitHub commenters (reachable via the repository's own README / CONTRIBUTING / issue templates and by `@`-mention); it is arguable only for Hacker News posters and drive-by stargazers, and even there 14(5)(b) requires the information to be made publicly available, which it is not. Multi-channel remediation is tracked at **DEF-1** (#7120); see the per-arm analysis in the LIA. **(iii) The Soleur operator** (`ops@jikigai.com`) — the sole dogfooder, whose own commits, issue comments, prompts and run telemetry are processed. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | **Transmitted to Anthropic (model input):** repository source and git metadata read by the agent after spawn under its own `Read`/`Glob`/`Grep` grants; commit messages and authorship metadata; GitHub issue and pull-request titles, bodies, author handles and comment bodies (`cron-daily-triage` runs `gh issue view` over up to 200 open issues); community-platform content collected by `cron-community-monitor` — raw Discord guild message objects, Hacker News post and comment text, GitHub contributor lists, GitHub comment snippets and author handles, and stargazer usernames with starred dates; workflow and CI state; the literal string `"ping"` for `cron-anthropic-credit-probe` (**nil personal data** — named here rather than omitted, because non-exhaustive enumeration is the failure mode this entry exists to fix). **Persisted controller-side:** Inngest event and run payloads (see §(f)); cost markers; a bounded stdout/stderr tail forwarded to Sentry (see §(d)). **The load-bearing distinction — output minimization is NOT input minimization.** `cron-community-monitor`'s prompt directives — "…only — never list individual followers, commenters, or likers" and "Summarize and aggregate -- do not store raw message transcripts. Brief contextual quotes (under 100 chars) with attribution are acceptable." — constrain the **published digest**. They do **not** constrain the **model input**: the raw Discord messages, the untruncated Hacker News comment text and the GitHub comment bodies are in the model's context regardless. The same prompt additionally carries **counter-directives that mandate** identifiers in the published output — an instruction to report "new stargazers in the period (username and starred date)" and an instruction to emit a "sub-section with a markdown table: `\| User \| Issue/PR \| Comment \|`" — and the first directive above is scoped in source **only to the LinkedIn section** of the digest, not digest-wide. The published-output limb and its full treatment are at **PA-32 §(c)**. **No input-side projection exists at the collectors:** `plugins/soleur/skills/community/scripts/discord-community.sh` emits raw Discord API message objects with no `jq` field projection, and `plugins/soleur/skills/community/scripts/hn-community.sh` emits `comment_text` untruncated. Tracked as an Art. 25(1) gap at deferred item **DEF-3** (#7124). |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | **None sought; may arrive unsolicited.** Free-text Discord messages, Hacker News comments and GitHub issue bodies structurally may carry health, belief, political or other Art. 9 content, and no collector applies a content filter. Reasoned in the manner of PA-27 rather than denied. Controls, stated at their true strength: (i) the sources are technical-project channels where such content is not expected; (ii) the operator AUP § 4.7 prohibits Art. 9 / 10 content in repository submissions — an **organisational** measure binding contributors, not the community population; (iii) the Anthropic-side copy expires with the 30-day retention window in §(f). **Unlike PA-27 there is NO closed output allowlist and NO system-prompt Art. 9 omission instruction on this path** — PA-27's own DPIA residual (a) records that "a prompt instruction is a claim, not a mechanism"; here there is not even the claim. **Named residual (accepted, single-operator scale):** unsolicited Art. 9 content reaching Anthropic, and — via PA-32 — a published artefact from which it cannot be erased. Recorded as residual (a) of the DPIA screening memo `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-07-31-dpia-screening-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci.md`. For the avoidance of doubt: Discord guild membership and GitHub contributor identity are **not** Art. 9 special categories and are not treated as such here. No Art. 10 data. |
| **Lawful basis** | **Art. 6(1)(f) legitimate interest** for the third-party populations (b)(i) and (b)(ii) — full three-part test at `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-07-31-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci-lia.md`, Arm A (repository / engineering) and Arm B1 (community collection and egress), both concluding that legitimate interest **prevails**, Arm B1 narrowly and conditionally on the Art. 14 obligation in §(b) being discharged. **Art. 6(1)(b)** performance of contract for the operator population (b)(iii), mirroring PA-22's treatment of the operator alongside the third-party limb, with Art. 6(1)(f) for the derived cost- and run-telemetry writes. **Art. 28 is not Art. 6.** The Anthropic entry in the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping below is a **vendor record** establishing Art. 28(3) processor terms; it does not and cannot supply a lawful basis. A lawful basis is a controller-side Art. 6 determination and it lives here and in the LIA. Art. 28 coverage is unaffected either way — the DPA auto-incorporates via Anthropic's Commercial Terms § C at the account/Services level, not per workload (counsel review #7086 § Finding 1). **The PA-17 / #4558 trigger has fired.** PA-17's balancing fences a residual behind a "first third-party-personal-data repository" re-evaluation trigger recorded in `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-05-counsel-review-4558.md`; population (b)(ii) **is** that population and has been in scope since at least 2026-02-19, so the trigger fired roughly five months before it was honoured. It is honoured by the LIA's re-opened balancing and by the dated amendment to PA-17 in this same change. **Art. 22: negative determination.** No decision producing legal or similarly significant effects on a natural person is taken by automated means; the fleet classifies issues, code and activity, not people. Re-opened if any member gains authority to block, ban, restrict or rank a natural person. |
| **(d) Recipients** | **Anthropic PBC** (US, Art. 28 processor) — recipient of every model input enumerated in §(c), under the Jikigai-keyed `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (NOT operator BYOK — distinct from PA-22). **GitHub Inc** (US, processor) — every CLI member receives a scoped GitHub App installation token as `GH_TOKEN` and reads and writes repository, issue and pull-request content through the GitHub API, so GitHub Inc receives that content as API request and response payloads. This is a **different framing from PA-17**, where GitHub is expressly "the **source** (not recipient)"; the difference is real and is stated rather than left to silently contradict — PA-17 describes an inbound webhook, this describes outbound API calls. GitHub Inc as **host of the published digest and of public issue comments** is recorded at **PA-32 §(d)**. **Sentry (Functional Software GmbH)** (DE, processor) — recipient of a **bounded stdout/stderr tail that may contain third-party identifiers**. `formatTailForSentry` (`apps/web-platform/server/inngest/functions/_cron-shared.ts`) forwards a truncated tail through `redactGithubSourcedText` (`apps/web-platform/lib/safety/redaction-allowlist.ts`), which strips emails, phone numbers, UUIDs, IPv4 addresses, JWTs, authorization headers and credential shapes but **not** usernames, handles, display names or free text — and the fleet's stdout is collector JSON containing exactly those. **PA-27's sentence "Sentry/Better Stack are NOT recipients of content" is true for PA-27 and FALSE here**, because PA-27's protection is `SENSITIVE_KEY_NAMES` key-name scrubbing of a known payload shape, which does not apply to an arbitrary CLI stdout tail. Copying it would be an over-claim. **Self-hosted Inngest event/run store (a third PII surface, not a third-party recipient)** — operator-managed, localhost-bound, on EU infrastructure; see §(f). **Supabase Inc** (EU) where a member writes routine-run telemetry (PA-29 substrate). **Better Stack s.r.o.** (CZ, processor; ingest cluster `eu-fsn-3`, DE) — **[2026-07-31 ADDITION (#7100), applied at review.** This recipient was missing. The cell already stated that PA-27's sentence *"Sentry/Better Stack are NOT recipients of content"* is **FALSE here**, and then omitted Better Stack from its own enumeration before closing with "No other third-party recipients" — asserting and denying the same fact within one cell. The path is direct: `_cron-claude-eval-substrate.ts` logs **every** stderr line of the spawned Claude CLI at pino `error` level; `apps/web-platform/infra/vector.toml` ships the `soleur-web-platform` container's journald at pino level ≥ 40 to the Better Stack sink. Note the emitter-side redaction here is `redactToken` (installation-token only) — **not** the `redactGithubSourcedText` this cell credits on the Sentry path — so Better Stack receives the same class of content with **strictly less** redaction at source than Sentry does, bounded only by Vector's `pii_scrub_*` chain, whose `pii_scrub_drop_userdata` drops `body`/`content`/`message`/`prompt`/`userInput` and does **not** reach pino's default `msg` key where the stderr text lands. **]** **Discord Inc** (US) — recipient of the `cron-weekly-release-digest` output, which posts via `postDiscordWebhook` to `DISCORD_RELEASES_WEBHOOK_URL`; corroborated independently by the Tier-2 egress allowlist entry for `discord.com`. The payload is passed through `sanitizeReleases` (a PII strip) and is designed to carry no personal data; it is enumerated anyway on the same principle that keeps the nil-PII `cron-anthropic-credit-probe` in §(a), and the sanitizer is recorded as the bounding measure in §(g) so a regression that began shipping handles would be visible against a stated control rather than against silence. Discord appears in this register elsewhere only as a *source*; this is the first place it is recorded as a *recipient*. With those two added, the enumeration is closed: no other third-party recipients, and no new sub-processor is introduced by this entry — Better Stack and Discord Inc are both already-recorded processors of this controller. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **US — Anthropic PBC:** EU-US Data Privacy Framework + SCCs Modules 2+3 + UK IDTA + Swiss Addendum; DPA auto-incorporated via Commercial Terms § C (effective 2025-02-24); snapshot at `knowledge-base/legal/data-processing-agreements/anthropic.md`. **US — GitHub Inc:** EU-US DPF + SCCs Module 2 under the Customer Data Protection Agreement (the mechanism already recorded in the Vendor / Sub-Processor Mapping row below; this entry claims it rather than assuming it). **Intra-EU, Chapter V not engaged:** Sentry (DE region), the self-hosted Inngest store (Hetzner `hel1`, Finland), Supabase (`eu-west-1`, Ireland). **Standing of the DPF, verified 2026-07-31:** the adequacy decision (EU) 2023/1795 is in force; the General Court dismissed *Latombe v Commission* (T-553/23) on 3 September 2025 and an appeal is pending before the Court of Justice as **C-703/25 P**. The SCCs are the standing fallback if the DPF is invalidated; the C-703/25 P ruling is a re-evaluation trigger. **Not a Chapter V transfer:** publication of a digest to a world-readable repository (*Bodil Lindqvist*, C-101/01, 6 November 2003) — see **PA-32 §(e)**, which must not be conflated with the Anthropic egress recorded here. |
| **(f) Retention** | **Anthropic PBC — 30 days, default.** The **Zero-Retention amendment is UNSIGNED** (`zero_retention_amendment: unsigned` in `knowledge-base/legal/data-processing-agreements/anthropic.md`), so Anthropic's default 30-day API request/response retention for safety review applies to **all** fleet egress — including raw Discord guild message content, Hacker News comment text and GitHub comment bodies — not only to the PA-27 surface it was previously reasoned about. Jikigai cannot shorten this window; it can only expire, or be removed by signing the amendment. Re-evaluation trigger at 90 days unsigned from this entry's date. **Self-hosted Inngest event/run store — NO automatic deletion.** The fleet's step payloads and run logs land in the same operator-managed, localhost-bound store already recorded for PA-21/22/27 ("the Inngest server does not automatically delete old database rows"); the copy persists until operator-side store maintenance. Recording only Anthropic's 30 days would imply a retention story this activity does not have. **Sentry** — the bounded tail inherits PA-8's operational-telemetry envelope. **Supabase / `routine_runs`** — per PA-29. **The published digests are PERMANENT.** 80 digests committed between 2026-02-19 and 2026-06-08 to a public repository with 2 forks; **zero deletions have ever occurred**; **no erasure routine exists and none is implementable** against git history and forks. That is an Art. 5(1)(e) storage-limitation and Art. 17 **finding**, and it is recorded as such — with the measurements — at **PA-32 §(f)**, which owns the publication operation. It is named here so that no reader takes Anthropic's 30 days to be the whole retention story for data this fleet handles. **Art. 17 path:** for the Anthropic-side copy, none beyond expiry of the 30-day window; for the Inngest store, operator-side store maintenance; for the published corpus, **none — see PA-32 §(f) and deferred item DEF-4**. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | **(1) Deny-by-default `PreToolUse` command allowlist — on **16 of the 18** CLI members, NOT all of them **[2026-07-31 CORRECTION (#7100), applied at review: previously "13 of the 15", which inherited the cron-scoped denominator corrected in §(a). Re-measured on this branch: 16 of the 18 CLI members call `setupEphemeralWorkspace`, which is what writes `.claude/settings.json` and registers the hook; the 2 that do not are `cron-daily-triage` and `cron-follow-through-monitor`, exactly the two this row already names. Of the 16 hook-registered members, **13** carry a non-empty `CRON_BASH_ALLOWLISTS` entry (13 top-level keys at `_cron-claude-eval-substrate.ts` › `CRON_BASH_ALLOWLISTS`, all `cron-*`); the remaining 3 — `event-ship-merge`, `oneshot-f2-defer-gate-review`, `oneshot-recheck-4217-calibration` — resolve through `CRON_BASH_ALLOWLISTS[cronName] ?? []` to an **empty** allowlist, i.e. hook-registered and fully bash-denied, which is fail-closed and stricter than the 13, not weaker. The old wording understated this measure's coverage while overstating its denominator.]**.** `buildCronEvalSettings` registers `cron-bash-allowlist-hook.mjs` per spawn under a `*` catch-all matcher, reading the per-cron `CRON_BASH_ALLOWLISTS` entry; secret-path and argument-injection forms are denied even when the leading verb is allowlisted. **(2) `runHookSelfTest` fail-closed spawn probe** — runs an allowed form and a denied form through the real hook before the agent spawns and **aborts the cron** if the hook is unreachable, mis-registered, or has lost its fail-closed catch-all. **(3) THE TWO MEMBERS THAT HAVE NEITHER.** `cron-daily-triage` and `cron-follow-through-monitor` never call `setupEphemeralWorkspace`, so they receive **no** `.claude/settings.json`, **no** `PreToolUse` hook registration, **no** `CRON_BASH_ALLOWLISTS` entry and **no** `runHookSelfTest`; they run from the prod container CWD (`/app`), not an ephemeral clone (`cron-daily-triage.ts`: "This cron never clones, so `gh` runs from the prod container CWD /app (no .git)"). Their only tool control is their own `--allowedTools` string, and the substrate's committed Phase-0 probe evidence records that this is **not** a containment boundary: headless `claude --print` "does **NOT** fail-close non-allowlisted commands via `--allowedTools`/`defaultMode` — only an explicit `permissions.deny` rule OR a `PreToolUse` hook blocks, and an unhooked tool class / a crashed hook FAILS OPEN." **Their sole compensating control is the Tier-2 nftables egress firewall (measure 4).** Whether they run under the CLI's own sandbox is **not determinable from source**, and this record asserts it in neither direction. Tracked at deferred item **DEF-6** (#7123). **(4) Tier-2 nftables egress firewall** — a destination allowlist enforced on the host, **outside the agent's process tree entirely** (`apps/web-platform/infra/cron-egress-nftables.sh`, `cron-egress-allowlist.txt`, `cron-egress-firewall.service`, with a post-apply assertion and an enforcement probe). The strongest measure here, and the only one that binds the two unhooked members. **(5) The grandchild-process gap — two measures at two layers, stated as such.** The allowlist constrains the **instructions the model may issue** (e.g. `bash plugins/soleur/skills/community/scripts/community-router.sh`); once an allowlisted script runs, its own child `curl` / `gh api` calls are **grandchild OS processes that the hook does not gate** — the substrate says so in source: "the router's child curl/gh-api are grandchild OS processes gated by the egress firewall, NOT this hook". Egress from that process tree is constrained **only** at the network layer, by a destination-only, **content-blind** firewall. Neither layer inspects what is sent. **(6) Ephemeral per-run workspace with a GC backstop** — but **teardown is FAIL-OPEN**: `teardownEphemeralWorkspace` wraps its `rm` in try/catch and mirrors the failure to Sentry rather than failing the run, so "the workspace is destroyed at run end" would over-claim; the GC backstop is what makes the claim true eventually. **(7) Per-member explicit environment allowlist — relative, not small.** Most members pass **5** variables (`PATH`, `HOME`, `NODE_ENV`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `GH_TOKEN`), a few adding one or two run-scoped values; **`cron-community-monitor` passes 18**, including 13 third-party platform secrets (Discord, Bluesky, LinkedIn, X) plus the GitHub installation token. The range is the honest statement. **(8) NO PII SCRUB EXISTS on Anthropic-bound content on this path.** PA-22 records `sanitizePromptString` plus email scrubbing at prompt assembly as its Art. 32 measure; **that measure does not exist here**, and on the CLI path it structurally could not, because the agent reads files itself after spawn under its own `Read`/`Glob`/`Grep` grants — there is no prompt-assembly chokepoint to scrub at. `redactGithubSourcedText` is applied **only** to the outbound Sentry tail (§(d)), never to Anthropic-bound content. This is stated affirmatively because inheriting PA-22's TOM would be a materially false Art. 32 statement. **(9) Prompt directives are organisational measures at best.** Any allowlist entry justified in source by "the prompt forbids X" is a directive, not a technical control, and is not counted as one here. **(10) Named accepted residual — no register-vs-code drift guard.** Nothing in CI asserts that the 21-member snapshot in §(a) still equals the code. The repository contains the counter-example: `scripts/check-pa-22.sh` was written to guard the PA-22 entry and is wired into **no** workflow (`git grep check-pa-22 .github/` returns zero hits). Tracked at deferred item **DEF-2** (#7125), which folds that script's assertion into the same sweep. **Art. 22 brief:** negative determination stated in §Lawful basis; no separate brief. **DPIA status:** screening memo at `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-07-31-dpia-screening-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci.md` — **full DPIA NOT required for PA-31** (two WP248 rev.01 criteria engaged: innovative technology; involuntary/vulnerable subjects, partially), with five named accepted residuals and pinned re-screening triggers. **Vendor DPA file:** `knowledge-base/legal/data-processing-agreements/anthropic.md`. **LIA:** `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-07-31-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci-lia.md`. |
| **(h) DSAR (Art. 15 / 20)** | **Not reachable by the automated self-serve export, and the honest reason is structural.** `DSAR_TABLE_ALLOWLIST` (`apps/web-platform/server/dsar-export-allowlist.ts`) enumerates **database tables**; this activity's personal-data surfaces are a third-party API's transient request log, a self-hosted event store, a Sentry tail and — for two members — files and issue bodies. None is a table, so no allowlist entry can reach them. **The honest Art. 15 route today** for a member of population (b)(i) or (b)(ii) is a manual request to `legal@jikigai.com`, answered by (a) confirming the categories of processing from this entry, (b) a manual `git grep` of the repository for the requester's handle, and (c) a manual search of issue bodies — with **no completeness guarantee**, because there is no enumeration path over git history and issue bodies. **Art. 20 portability does not apply**: none of this data was provided by the data subject to Jikigai on the basis of consent or contract. Recording this as an exclusion with a canonical rationale, in the manner of `routine_runs` under PA-29, would understate it — the correct record is that the machinery does not reach this surface. Tracked at deferred item **DEF-8** (#7126). **Art. 21(1) objection route:** as of 2026-07-31 `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md` §8.1 carries a community-digest carve-out providing one for this population; what remains open at **DEF-9** (#7126) is whether an objection can be honoured in substance against an append-only published corpus, rather than merely received *(corrected 2026-08-01 at ship — this cell still read "none exists in any of the nine legal documents" after the same change added the route)*. That residual matters for Art. 6(1)(f) processing; tracked at deferred item **DEF-9** (#7126). |

## Processing Activity 32 — Community observation and republication (#7100)

(feat-one-shot-7100-art30-eval-fleet-register. The **public-output** counterpart to PA-31. Split from it because publication is a distinct operation under Art. 4(2) — "disclosure by transmission, dissemination or otherwise making available" — and because two cells cannot be reconciled in one row: PA-31's recipients are contracted processors while this activity's recipient is **the general public**, and PA-31's retention is a 30-day processor window while this activity's is **permanent with no erasure routine**. Two members' public-output limbs are governed here: `cron-community-monitor` (sub-purpose (i) — the committed digest) and `cron-daily-triage` (sub-purpose (ii) — public GitHub issue comments). Both also appear in PA-31 for their **egress** limb; a member appearing in two entries for two operations is correct under Art. 4(2) and is the reason PA-31's snapshot, not this entry's member list, is the drift-guard target. **This entry records that a live, materialised limb of the processing has NO available lawful basis** — see §Lawful basis. **Brand-survival threshold: single-user incident.**)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(a) Purpose** | **(i) Community-awareness digest.** `cron-community-monitor` collects activity across six platforms — Discord (a Jikigai-operated, bot-token-gated guild), GitHub (issues, pull requests, contributors, stargazers), Hacker News, Bluesky, LinkedIn and X — reasons over it with the Anthropic API, and **writes a digest file into `knowledge-base/support/community/` in the public `jikig-ai/soleur` repository**, committed by `claude[bot]` / `soleur-ai[bot]` under auto-merge with **no human gate**. **(ii) Public triage comments.** `cron-daily-triage` reads up to 200 open issues, runs `gh issue view` on each, and **writes a public `gh issue comment`** carrying the model's priority, type, domain and reasoning onto third parties' issues. The stated purpose of (i) is **operator awareness** of community activity; the stated purpose of (ii) is transparent, timely triage. The purpose that would have to be asserted to justify naming individuals in the published output of (i) — a public, positioning-oriented community record — is **not** the purpose the digest was built for, which is why the Art. 5(1)(b) / Art. 6(4) compatibility question in §Lawful basis arises at all. |
| **(b) Categories of data subjects** | **(i) Members of the Jikigai-operated Discord guild** — a **semi-private** source, readable only with `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN`; **(ii) GitHub issue and pull-request commenters**; **(iii) GitHub stargazers**; **(iv) Hacker News posters**; **(v) X (formerly Twitter) posters** and **(vi) Bluesky posters** — **[2026-07-31 ADDITION (#7100), applied at review.** §(a) of this entry describes the collector as spanning **six platforms**, and the DPIA screening memo names X and Bluesky, but this cell enumerated only four third-party populations. The omission is not cosmetic: `plugins/soleur/skills/community/scripts/x-community.sh` emits `author_id`, `author_username` and `author_name`, and `bsky-community.sh` emits `author_handle` and `author_name`, so both collect **per-person identifiers**. Left unlisted, these two populations sat outside this entry's §(c) categories, outside the Art. 14 remediation scope at #7120, and outside the R1–R5 minimisation at #7119 — none of which would have removed an X or Bluesky handle from a published digest. **]**; **(vii) the Soleur operator**, whose own activity also appears. Populations (i)–(vi) are **involuntary third-party data subjects** in the PA-27 sense and more strongly: they have no relationship of any kind with Jikigai, were never asked, and have no notice. **Art. 14 is ENGAGED, UNDISCHARGED and OVERDUE since ~2026-03-19**, with **no Art. 14(5) exemption claimed** — the analysis, the per-population 14(5)(b) table, and the multi-channel remediation are at PA-31 §(b) and in the LIA §Art. 14; the remediation is deferred item **DEF-1** (#7120). **The outward-facing record currently contradicts this entry.** `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md` § 4.4 tells the public that repository interaction data "is not controlled by Soleur", and `docs/legal/gdpr-policy.md` § 2.2 describes Anthropic requests as using "the user's own API key" — both **affirmatively false** for this activity, which ingests that data under the Jikigai key, sends it to Anthropic and republishes part of it. This is the same failure class as counsel review #7086 § Finding 4, which was rated BLOCKING. **[2026-07-31, RESOLVED — status corrected at review.** This sentence previously read "Correcting both is source-side work **outside this change's scope** and is tracked at deferred item **DEF-1a** (resolved in this PR); **until it lands**, a data subject reading the published policy is told something this register contradicts" — three mutually exclusive tenses in one sentence, in a statutory record. The actual position: `privacy-policy.md` §4.4 was corrected in this change; `gdpr-policy.md` §2.2's Anthropic bullet was **not**, and survived verbatim in both the canonical and the Eleventy mirror while the tracker read `Done`. Both are now corrected, together with a **third** published document neither statement named — `data-protection-disclosure.md` §2.2(b) and its mirror — and `LEGAL_DOC_SHAS` is repinned for all three. No published policy now contradicts this entry. **]** **(vii)** the operator is processed under Art. 6(1)(b). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | **Published to a permanent public surface:** Discord member handles, real first names where volunteered, join dates and inferred affiliation; **a verbatim quoted private-guild message attributed to a handle, disclosing the speaker's employer context**; GitHub commenter handles with comment snippets in a `\| User \| Issue/PR \| Comment \|` table; GitHub stargazer usernames with starred dates; Hacker News poster handles and post/comment references; and, for sub-purpose (ii), the model's reasoning about a third party's issue, published under Jikigai's bot identity on that third party's issue thread. **Two distinct snippet mechanisms, cited precisely rather than merged:** the collector caps the GitHub comment snippet at **`.[:120]`** characters (`plugins/soleur/skills/community/scripts/github-community.sh:498`, `body_snippet`), while the prompt authorises quotes "**under 100 chars**" — different mechanisms, different bounds, and the collector governs what the digest actually has available. **Output minimization is NOT input minimization, and here the directives are also self-contradicting.** The minimization directives are quoted verbatim at PA-31 §(c); the same prompt **mandates** the identifiers in the published output — "new stargazers in the period (username and starred date)" and a "sub-section with a markdown table: `\| User \| Issue/PR \| Comment \|`. Each row shows…" — and the "never list individual followers, commenters, or likers" directive is scoped **in source only to the LinkedIn section** of the digest. A record quoting the minimization directives without quoting the counter-directives would misdescribe the system. **Not published:** the raw message transcripts, which nevertheless reach Anthropic — recorded at PA-31. |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | **None sought; may arrive unsolicited — and here the consequence is worse than at PA-31.** Free text from a guild, from Hacker News or from an issue body may carry Art. 9 content, no collector filters it, and **there is no closed output allowlist on the publication path** (contrast PA-27's `MAIL_CLASS_ALLOWLIST`, which structurally bounded what the model could emit). An Art. 9 disclosure that reaches a committed digest is subject to the same Art. 17 impossibility as everything else in §(f) — it cannot be erased. **Named residual (accepted only as an interim posture, not as a steady state):** recorded as residual (a) of `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-07-31-dpia-screening-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci.md`, with remediation R4 (a code-level redaction pass before persistence) as the mechanism that would bound it. Discord guild membership and GitHub contributor identity are **not** Art. 9 special categories. No Art. 10 data. |
| **Lawful basis** | **Sub-purpose (i) collection limb and sub-purpose (ii) reading limb: Art. 6(1)(f)** — legitimate interest, LIA Arm B1 (`knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-07-31-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci-lia.md`), which concludes that legitimate interest prevails narrowly and conditionally. **Operator population: Art. 6(1)(b).** **REPUBLICATION LIMB — Art. 6(1)(f) IS NOT AVAILABLE AS IMPLEMENTED, AND NO OTHER ART. 6 BASIS IS.** The assessment fails at the **NECESSITY** limb and does not reach balancing (LIA Arm B2). The digest's purpose is operator awareness of community activity; naming the stargazer, naming and quoting the Discord member, and tabulating commenter handles with snippets is **not necessary** to that purpose, because an aggregate count plus a link to the source issue serves it identically. Art. 6(1)(f)'s necessity limb is strict — CJEU **C-13/16 *Rīgas satiksme*** (4 May 2017), on the materially identical Art. 7(f) of Directive 95/46/EC, treats necessity as an autonomous concept requiring that the interest cannot reasonably be achieved by less intrusive means, and EDPB **Guidelines 1/2024** on Art. 6(1)(f) treat necessity as a cumulative requirement assessed **before and independently of** balancing. Where a less intrusive means achieves the purpose, legitimate interest is unavailable **regardless of how the balancing would come out**. Balancing would fail anyway on the Discord limb: the guild is bot-token-gated, and a member's Recital 47 reasonable expectation is that their message stays in the guild — not that it is quoted verbatim, attributed, and committed to a world-readable repository that has been forked twice. No other basis is available: there is no contract with these subjects (6(1)(b)), no consent has been sought (6(1)(a)), and no legal-obligation, vital-interest or public-task ground applies. **The decisive internal precedent is Jikigai's own, and it points the same way.** PA-30's lawful-basis cell justifies the beta-CRM's structured store expressly over "**git-committed PII, an Art. 17 impossibility**", and its LIA (`knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-07-07-beta-crm-lia.md`, §Necessity ¶1) states that third-party PII is "**never committed to git**" because "git-committed third-party PII would be an Art. 17 erasure impossibility (git history is permanent)… The DB boundary is load-bearing, not incidental." This activity does precisely what Jikigai rejected in writing for a different activity — and does it on a **public** repository rather than into a private table. Asserting a clean basis here while PA-30 denies it there would leave the register holding both positions. **Conditions under which this changes:** remediation **R1–R5** — drop the mandated stargazer username list in favour of an aggregate count; replace the `\| User \| Issue/PR \| Comment \|` table with an issue link and a count; move the aggregate-only directive to digest scope and delete the attributed-quotation licence; add a **code-level** redaction pass over the digest file and issue body before persistence; and adopt a retention/erasure posture for published artefacts or record explicitly that none is possible. Tracked as deferred item **DEF-0** (#7119; blocking; body drafted at `knowledge-base/project/specs/feat-one-shot-7100-art30-eval-fleet-register/deferred-issues.md`). **With R1–R4 landed the published digest would carry no third-party identifiers, the necessity objection would fall away, and Art. 6(1)(f) would become available for the remaining aggregate publication. Until then this limb should be minimised or cease, and this cell records that it has no lawful basis.** **Art. 5(1)(b) / Art. 6(4) purpose compatibility — separately unsatisfied.** Discord data is obtained for the purpose of operating a community; republishing it as a public positioning artefact is a **further purpose**, and the Art. 6(4) compatibility factors run against it: the link between purposes is weak, the context of collection is a semi-private guild, the data includes communications content, the consequence is permanent public disclosure, and there are no safeguards such as pseudonymisation. **Art. 22: negative determination.** `cron-daily-triage` auto-labels and comments on third parties' issues, but the decision concerns an **issue**, is reversible by any maintainer or by the author's reply, and produces no legal or similarly significant effect on the commenter. Re-opened if any member gains authority to block, ban, restrict or rank a natural person. **Discord Developer Policy (contractual, non-GDPR):** Discord's developer terms constrain retention and republication of member data obtained through the API; this activity's permanent republication of guild content is on the far side of an ordinary reading of them. Recorded as a contractual limb requiring review, on the PA-15 precedent of recording platform-terms constraints in the register. **Non-EU thresholds:** CCPA/CPRA (no California consumer relationship, no sale or share, thresholds unmet) and Québec Law 25 (no Québec establishment, thresholds unmet) are **not met** — stated rather than left silent. |
| **(d) Recipients** | **THE GENERAL PUBLIC.** The digest is a file in a world-readable git repository and the triage comments are public GitHub issue comments; anyone on the internet, and every clone and both forks, is a recipient. This is a recipient **category**, not a processor, and there is no contract, instruction or safeguard governing it. **GitHub Inc** (US) — **recipient and host** of that public surface, holding the repository, its full commit history, the issue bodies and GitHub's retained issue-edit history. **This inverts PA-17's framing**, in which GitHub is expressly "the **source** (not recipient)" and "GitHub Inc. (Microsoft Corporation) remains the **source**": for this activity GitHub is unambiguously a recipient and a host, and the two entries are stated to be describing different flows rather than left to contradict each other silently. **Anthropic PBC** (US, Art. 28 processor) — recipient of the collection limb's model input; recorded at **PA-31 §(d)**, not duplicated here. **Sentry** — bounded tail, per PA-31 §(d). No safeguard bounds onward use by the general public; none is available. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **Publication to a world-readable surface is NOT a Chapter V transfer.** In *Bodil Lindqvist* (C-101/01, judgment of 6 November 2003, ECLI:EU:C:2003:596) the Court held that the concept of transfer to a third country does not cover the loading of data onto an internet page, even where the data thereby becomes accessible to persons in third countries. No SCC, DPF listing or transfer instrument therefore covers, or needs to cover, the act of publication — **and, critically, none of them mitigates it either**. A reader must not conclude that the Anthropic SCCs reach this operation. **The Chapter V limb of this activity is the Anthropic egress**, recorded at **PA-31 §(e)** under EU-US DPF + SCCs Modules 2+3 + UK IDTA + Swiss Addendum. **GitHub Inc as host** (US): EU-US DPF + SCCs Module 2 under the Customer Data Protection Agreement — a real transfer mechanism for the hosting relationship, which does **not** convert the public disclosure into a governed one. The distinction between the two is the whole point of this cell. |
| **(f) Retention** | **Indefinite / permanent. No erasure routine exists, and none is implementable. This is an Art. 5(1)(e) storage-limitation FINDING, recorded as a finding rather than left blank.** Measured on this branch on 2026-07-31, with the commands recorded in the LIA provenance section: **80** digests in `knowledge-base/support/community/` spanning **2026-02-19 → 2026-06-08**; **45** carrying the `\| User \| Issue/PR \| Comment \|` commenter table; **65** referencing stargazers; **zero** deletions in that directory across the repository's entire history (`git log --diff-filter=D` returns 0 commits); the repository is **public** with **2 forks** (`gh repo view --json forkCount,isPrivate` → `{"forkCount":2,"isPrivate":false}`). **Why Art. 17 is not merely unimplemented but not implementable:** git history is append-only, so deleting a digest in a new commit erases nothing — the data persists in every prior commit object, in GitHub's commit UI, in every clone, and in **both forks**; `git filter-repo` plus a force-push breaks downstream clones, **does not reach forks**, and does not reach public issue bodies, whose edit history GitHub retains. **Art. 17 path: NONE.** A Discord member or GitHub commenter has no route today to have their handle or quoted comment removed from the published corpus. Tracked at deferred items **DEF-4** (#7119; erasure path, or a documented finding that none is possible) and **DEF-0 / R5**. **Live-state note:** the public-issue-comment limb of sub-purpose (ii) is live (issue comments observed 2026-07-28…30); the digest file-write limb of sub-purpose (i) is **live and scheduled**: `cron-community-monitor` is registered in the Inngest manifest on a daily `0 8 * * *` schedule with `COMMUNITY_DIGEST_DIR` still pointing at `knowledge-base/support/community/`, and no digest file has landed since 2026-06-08. The absence of recent output is a **persistence observation, not evidence of dormancy**, and the cause is not established. **[2026-07-31 CORRECTION (#7100), applied at review: this previously read "appears to have produced nothing since 2026-06-08 and its live state should be re-verified at merge". Both halves were wrong to ship — the live state is determinable from source and is now stated flatly, and "should be re-verified at merge" embedded an unperformed operator step inside an Art. 30 record. A controller hedging whether its own scheduled function runs reads, to a supervisory authority, as not knowing.]** **That reduces nothing legally** — the 80 published digests remain published, and the retention finding above concerns what has already been disclosed. **[2026-08-12 AMENDMENT (#7471) — one distribution channel narrowed; nothing retired.** The "every clone" limb of this cell had an industrial source: the documented plugin install path was `claude plugin marketplace add jikig-ai/soleur`, which materialises the **whole monorepo** — this cell's digest corpus under `knowledge-base/support/community/` included — onto every installer's machine, where no erasure attempt reaches it. A **second, narrowed channel now exists beside that one**: `jikig-ai/soleur-marketplace`, a dedicated public distribution repository whose plugin entry is a `git-subdir` source resolving to `plugins/soleur` only. The subtree boundary was verified as a boundary — by direct file-count comparison of paths that exist at both levels, with repo-root markers confirmed absent — rather than inferred from payload size (`knowledge-base/project/specs/feat-one-shot-7471-plugin-delivery-path/measurements.md` §1.0), and the delivered payload was then measured against the published repository (ibid. §2B). The digest corpus, this register itself and the counsel-review memoranda all live under **repo-root** `knowledge-base/`, which that payload does not contain; an install made through the new channel therefore distributes no further copy of them. **What this does NOT do, stated so the cell cannot be read as a discharge.** **(1) The whole-repo channel is not retired, and closing it is not within Jikigai's power.** `jikig-ai/soleur` remains a valid marketplace, every existing install stays on it and keeps cloning everything, and that entry carries client-side `autoUpdate: true` which **cannot be revoked remotely** (ibid. §1.6/2B.6). **(2) Nothing already distributed is recalled, and nothing becomes erasable.** Every prior clone, both forks, and every existing install retain what they hold; the Art. 17 analysis above is unchanged in every respect and the published corpus recorded above is undiminished. **(3) This is a delivery-payload minimisation change only.** It bears on the Art. 5(1)(c) / Art. 25(2) limb of the distribution path, and only for installs made through the new channel. It creates no erasure path and **must not be cited as satisfying remediation R5** or deferred item **DEF-4** (both #7119 / DEF-0), which remain open exactly as recorded above. **]** |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | **Stated at their true strength, which for the publication limb is weak.** **(1) The upstream containment measures apply to the collection limb only** — the `PreToolUse` allowlist, `runHookSelfTest`, the Tier-2 nftables egress firewall, the ephemeral workspace and the per-member environment allowlist are recorded at **PA-31 §(g)**, including the finding that `cron-daily-triage` — one of this entry's two members — has **none** of the hook-based measures. **(2) There is NO output allowlist and NO human gate on the publication path.** PA-27 bounded its equivalent risk with "no tools + a closed `MAIL_CLASS_ALLOWLIST`"; here neither bound exists **and** a publication tool does: the digest auto-merges to a public repository and the issue comment auto-publishes, both without human review. **(3) Prompt-injection to public publication — a genuine Art. 32(1)(b) finding, not a quality risk.** The digest is written by a model over attacker-controllable input (anyone may post in the guild, comment on an issue, or post to Hacker News), and the output reaches a public surface under Jikigai's bot identity with no gate. Tracked at deferred item **DEF-7** (#7122); overlaps remediation R4. **(4) The prompt directives are NOT technical measures.** The minimization directives quoted at PA-31 §(c) are organisational at best — PA-27's own DPIA residual (a) records that "a prompt instruction is a claim, not a mechanism" — and here they are additionally contradicted by counter-directives in the same prompt. **(5) The snippet cap is a collector-side technical bound, and it is the only one.** `github-community.sh:498` truncates the GitHub comment body at 120 characters before the model ever sees it. It bounds volume; it does not bound identification. **(6) No redaction is applied before persistence.** `redactGithubSourcedText` is applied only to the outbound Sentry tail; nothing scrubs the digest file or the issue body, and it would not scrub handles if it were applied, since its set excludes usernames, handles, display names and free text. **DPIA status: FULL DPIA REQUIRED — and overdue.** The screening memo `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-07-31-dpia-screening-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci.md` finds **six** WP248 rev.01 criteria engaged for this activity (systematic monitoring; data of a highly personal nature; matching/combining across platforms; involuntary subjects with no relationship and no notice; innovative technology with a publication path and no human gate; and processing whose chosen mechanism forecloses Art. 17 / 15 / 20 / 21), against a WP248 threshold of two. Art. 35(1) requires the assessment **prior to** the processing, which began 2026-02-19, so it is **overdue by approximately five months**. Scoped at deferred item **DEF-5** (#7121). The memo records that R1–R4 collapse four of the six criteria at once, so remediation is very likely the better path than assessing a limb this entry records as having no lawful basis. **LIA:** `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-07-31-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci-lia.md`, Arm B2. **Vendor DPA file:** `knowledge-base/legal/data-processing-agreements/anthropic.md` (collection limb only — no vendor record governs publication). |
| **(h) DSAR (Art. 15 / 20)** | **Not reachable by the existing machinery, and the reason is the same mechanism that forecloses Art. 17.** `DSAR_TABLE_ALLOWLIST` (`apps/web-platform/server/dsar-export-allowlist.ts`) covers **database tables**; a committed digest file and a GitHub issue body are neither, and there is no enumeration path over git history or issue bodies. A DSAR from a Discord member is therefore **currently unanswerable by the automated export**. **The honest Art. 15 route today** is a manual request to `legal@jikigai.com`, answered by confirming the categories in this entry, a manual `git grep` of the repository for the requester's handle, and a manual search of issue bodies — with **no completeness guarantee**. **Art. 20 does not apply** (the data was not provided by the subject to Jikigai under consent or contract). Tracked at deferred item **DEF-8** (#7126). **Art. 21(1) right to object** — mandatory for any Art. 6(1)(f) processing, and **partially closed by this change**: as of 2026-07-31 `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md` §8.1 carries a community-digest carve-out giving this population an Art. 15/17/21 route against Jikigai, with the manual-search and no-erasure limits stated. What remains open at **DEF-9** (#7126) is defining what a *truthful and complete* Art. 15 response says, and whether an objection can be honoured in substance rather than merely received. **[2026-08-01 CORRECTION at ship: this cell read "absent: none of the nine legal documents provides an objection route", which this same change had already falsified — the authoritative cell was corrected and the cell quoting it was not, the pattern DEF-2 (#7125) exists to guard.]** For this activity the point is sharper than a missing route: even a successful objection has no remedy, because §(f) records that erasure is not implementable. |

## Processing Activity 33 — Jikigai-keyed Anthropic API surface in GitHub Actions CI (#7100)

(feat-one-shot-7100-art30-eval-fleet-register. The CI counterpart to PA-31, recorded separately because the trigger model, the data subjects and the controls differ. Counsel review `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-07-counsel-review-7086.md` § Finding 4 rated as **BLOCKING** a record that described this surface as dead when it is live. This entry is scoped so it cannot repeat that error: **membership keys on the presence of `secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, not on the use of `claude-code-action`** — an entry scoped to the action would have been false on the day it was written, because two live jobs reach Anthropic with no action involved. Jikigai is **controller**; Anthropic PBC is an Art. 28 **processor**. **Brand-survival threshold: single-user incident.**)

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(a) Purpose** | Run model-assisted checks and repairs inside GitHub Actions on `jikig-ai/soleur` under the Jikigai `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: **(i)** automatically repairing a red dependency-cruiser import-boundary constraint gate on the pull request that broke it (`fix-constraints-stage-a`, fix-only, artifact-producing, no write token — an Art. 32 network-and-information-security interest in the Recital 49 sense); **(ii)** two paid single-turn model gates inside `ci.yml` that verify a sandbox-capture invariant (`sandbox-canary-capture-gate`) and a `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` propagation invariant (`plugin-root-propagation-gate`, whose own comment reads "Creds-gated (one paid Haiku turn)"); **(iii)** an empirical `PreToolUse`-hook verification run against a hardcoded synthetic prompt (`test-pretooluse-hooks`); **(iv)** historically, automated pull-request review (`claude-code-review`, currently disabled). **Scope predicate — membership keys on the SECRET, not on the action:** every file **in this repository** that supplies `secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` to a GitHub Actions surface — i.e. the output of `grep -rl 'secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY' .` excluding `knowledge-base/**` (which contains prose references to the secret's name, not carriers of it). **[2026-07-31 WIDENING (#7100), applied at review.** This predicate previously read `grep -rl 'secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY' .github/` and was **under-inclusive**: it missed `plugins/soleur/skills/operator-digest/assets/operator-digest.workflow.yml`, a committed workflow that is provisioned into the separate Jikigai-controlled private repository `jikig-ai/operator-digest` and runs there on a weekly schedule under the Jikigai key. A `.github/`-scoped predicate cannot see a workflow that this repo *ships* but does not *run*, so the surface was registered in no activity at all while this entry asserted a dated, complete member snapshot. Widening the predicate makes the surface **self-detecting** — re-running it now returns the asset — rather than leaving detection to whoever happens to notice. Member (7) below records it. **]** This predicate survives action renames and the eventual replacement of `claude-code-action`; the action used becomes a **per-member attribute** rather than the membership test. |
| **(b) Categories of data subjects** | **(i) Same-repo pull-request authors and reviewers** — contributors whose branch head is checked out and read by the agent, and whose PR metadata (title, branch name, head SHA) is transmitted. **Fork pull-request authors are excluded by construction**: forks receive no secrets, so `.github/actions/anthropic-preflight/action.yml` — the shared credential-liveness probe, named once here rather than per caller — fails and the agent step is skipped. **(ii) Third parties named in the checked-out tree** (commit authors in git history, names in committed files) reachable by the agent's `Read`/`Grep`. **(iii) The Soleur operator**, as the author of most pull requests on this repository. **Art. 14:** engaged and materially satisfied by context — contributors open pull requests knowingly against a public repository with published contribution terms and visible CI — but the Art. 14(1) information is nevertheless best stated expressly in `CONTRIBUTING`, which is part of deferred item **DEF-1** (#7120). `test-pretooluse-hooks` processes a hardcoded synthetic prompt and therefore has **nil personal data**; it is named rather than omitted, because non-exhaustive enumeration is the failure mode this entry exists to fix. |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | Contributor-authored source at the pull-request **head SHA**, read by the agent under its own tool grants; git history and authorship metadata reachable in the checked-out tree; pull-request metadata (title, branch, head SHA, actor login) available in the Actions context; for the two `ci.yml` gates, sandbox-capture and plugin-propagation probe inputs plus a single model turn each; for `test-pretooluse-hooks`, a hardcoded synthetic prompt only. **Honest scope note:** `fix-constraints-stage-a` is **path-filtered at the trigger** (`apps/web-platform/app/**`, `components/**`, `server/**`, and the two dependency-cruiser configuration files), but once the job is triggered the agent's `Read`/`Grep` reach the **whole** checked-out tree, not only the changed paths. The path filter bounds *when* the surface activates, not *what* it can read. |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | **None sought; may arrive unsolicited** in a commit message, an issue reference or a committed file. Reasoned rather than denied, in the manner of PA-27. Controls: the operator AUP § 4.7 prohibits Art. 9 / 10 content in repository submissions (an **organisational** measure); the surface is same-repo-only, so the contributor population is small and known; the Anthropic-side copy expires with the 30-day window in §(f). No output allowlist exists on this path. Named residual, at the same bar as PA-31. Contributor identity is **not** an Art. 9 special category. No Art. 10 data. |
| **Lawful basis** | **Art. 6(1)(f) legitimate interest** for the third-party contributor population — LIA Arm C at `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-07-31-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci-lia.md`, concluding that legitimate interest **prevails**: the contributor's code is the artefact under assessment so necessity is satisfied, and a contributor who opens a pull request against a public repository with published contribution terms and visible CI has a Recital 47 expectation that comfortably covers automated processing of the branch. Recital 49 additionally recognises network and information security as a legitimate interest, which is the specific interest served by (a)(i) and (a)(ii). **Art. 6(1)(b)** for the operator population. **Art. 28 is not Art. 6** — see PA-31 §Lawful basis. **CLA coverage is an OPEN question, not a resolved one.** PA-7 records the Individual and Corporate CLA as the mechanism by which contributors grant Jikigai an IP licence. Whether that grant extends to **transmitting contributor-authored source to a third-party AI processor** is a contract-scope question the CLA text was not written to answer, and it is distinct from the GDPR question assessed here — an IP licence is not a lawful basis, and a lawful basis is not an IP licence. Recorded as outstanding counsel-review item 3 of the LIA and as item 7 of `## Register Maintenance` below; cross-references **PA-7**. **Art. 22: negative determination** — the surface evaluates code and CI state, not persons. |
| **(d) Recipients** | **Anthropic PBC** (US, Art. 28 processor) — recipient of the checked-out tree content and pull-request metadata that the agent transmits, under the Jikigai `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (NOT operator BYOK). **GitHub Inc** (US, processor) — operator of the Actions runners on which every member executes and custodian of the checkout, the workflow logs and the produced artifacts. **Dated member snapshot — 6 files / 7 job-level surfaces, measured 2026-07-31 (member (7) added 2026-08-01 at review, when the predicate was widened)** via `grep -rl 'secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY' .github/`; of these **3 files** use `claude-code-action` (`grep -rn 'uses: anthropics/claude-code-action' .github/`) — `fix-constraints-stage-a.yml`, `claude-code-review.yml` and `test-pretooluse-hooks.yml` — which is why membership keys on the secret rather than on the action. **[2026-07-31 CORRECTION (#7100), applied at review.** The enumeration below previously ran (1)–(5) and was **not** the output of the predicate it declares: it listed `ci.yml` **twice** (once per job) and omitted `.github/workflows/claude-code-review.yml` entirely, so it covered four distinct files while claiming five, and the "3 use `claude-code-action`" figure could not be reconciled against it — only 2 of the listed items are action callers. `claude-code-review.yml` is now member **(6)**. This is the same non-exhaustive-enumeration defect this entry's preamble says it exists to prevent, and it mattered concretely: `claude-code-review.yml` holds the secret and is one API call from live, so re-enabling it would have recreated the Art. 30(1) gap with nothing tripping, and the DEF-2 (#7125) drift guard would have been built against the wrong target set. **]** **(1)** `.github/workflows/fix-constraints-stage-a.yml` — trigger `pull_request` (`opened`/`synchronize`/`reopened`, path-filtered) + `workflow_dispatch`; uses `anthropics/claude-code-action@5aa6f47e977fec3321f32c06d8076b1f503f832c` (v1.0.161); **active**; PR-head-SHA checkout; `permissions: contents: read`. **(2)** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` → job `sandbox-canary-capture-gate` — triggers `push:[main]`, `pull_request` **and** `merge_group`; step-level `env: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, **no** `claude-code-action`. **(3)** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` → job `plugin-root-propagation-gate` — same triggers; step-level `env:`; in-source comment "Creds-gated (one paid Haiku turn)". **(4)** `.github/workflows/test-pretooluse-hooks.yml` — `workflow_dispatch` only; uses `claude-code-action`; hardcoded synthetic prompt ⇒ **nil personal data**. **(5)** `.github/actions/anthropic-preflight/action.yml` — composite action; the shared credential-liveness probe (a one-token Messages API request) invoked by the other members; it names `claude-code-action` only in its `description:` string and is **not** an action caller. **(6)** `.github/workflows/claude-code-review.yml` — uses `anthropics/claude-code-action` with `secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; workflow state **`disabled_manually`** as at the dated read below, and therefore **not currently egressing** — but it is a member, because membership keys on possession of the secret and not on run state. Re-enabling it is a single GitHub API call that touches no file in this repository, so a source-only inspection cannot detect the transition; uploads artifacts at `retention-days: 90`. It is enumerated here rather than described only in the exclusions prose so that the member set equals the predicate's output.

  **Why a disabled workflow is still a member.** Every other entry in this register scopes membership to what a surface *can* do with the credential it holds, not to what it did last week. A disabled-but-keyed workflow is the exact state in which an Art. 30 record goes stale silently: nothing in the repository changes when it is re-enabled, so a reader re-running the predicate months later finds a live egress surface the register never listed.

  **(7)** `plugins/soleur/skills/operator-digest/assets/operator-digest.workflow.yml` — **runs in a different repository.** This file is the provisioning source for `.github/workflows/operator-digest.yml` in the separate, **private**, Jikigai-controlled repository `jikig-ai/operator-digest` (created 2026-06-12), provisioned by `provision-operator-digest-repo.sh`, which reads the key from Doppler `soleur/prd`. It uses `anthropics/claude-code-action` with `secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` on `schedule: '0 13 * * 5'` and checks out the **public** `jikig-ai/soleur` repository. Live state read from the GitHub API on **2026-08-01**: workflow **`active`**; scheduled runs 2026-07-10, 2026-07-17 and 2026-07-24 **succeeded**, and a run on **2026-07-31** — the same date this entry's other members were measured — **failed**. So this surface was live throughout the period PA-31 and PA-33 were authored.

  **What egresses, sourced to the committed asset.** The agent tool allowlist is `Write,Read,Glob,Grep,Bash(date:*),Bash(ls:*),Bash(gh pr list:*),Bash(gh issue list:*),Bash(git log:*)`. So the model receives pull-request and issue **titles, bodies and author handles**, and `git log` **author name and email**, for the public repo — third-party personal data, under the Jikigai key, with Anthropic PBC as processor on the same DPA as every other member. `Read`/`Grep` reach the checked-out tree, so repository source is in scope too; this is the same honest concession PA-33 already makes for `fix-constraints-stage-a`.

  **Bounds that are determinable from source, and are stated rather than assumed.** (i) **No artifacts**: the workflow contains zero `upload-artifact` steps, so artifact retention is **nil by construction** — only the Actions *log* limb applies, under the private repo's retention settings. (ii) **The digest is not published publicly**: it is posted via `gh issue create -R jikig-ai/operator-digest`, i.e. into the private repo, and the working file is `rm -f`'d on both branches. (iii) **The agent cannot post**: `gh issue create` is deliberately **absent** from the allowlist, and the asset says so in terms — the workflow's own later step does the posting. That is a materially tighter bound than a public-publication limb, and it is why this member sits in PA-33 (internal CI-class egress) rather than in PA-32 (republication).

  **Named residual — asset-vs-deployed drift.** The above is read from the **committed provisioning source**. The deployed copy in `jikig-ai/operator-digest` was not diffed against it, so a divergence introduced directly in that repository would not be visible here. Recorded as a residual rather than left implicit; re-establish by diff rather than copying this paragraph forward. Registering the surface from committed source with a named residual strictly dominates the prior state, which was no entry at all under an assertion that the enumeration was complete.

  **Two further `secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` hits under the widened predicate are NOT members**, and are named so the next reader does not re-derive the question: `plugins/soleur/skills/constraint-scaffold/references/fix-constraints-stage-a.template` and `plugins/soleur/skills/schedule/SKILL.md` are **scaffolding templates emitted into tenant repositories**. They carry the literal so that a *tenant's* workflow references *the tenant's own* secret; no Jikigai-held credential flows through them, and any egress they cause is the tenant's own processing under the tenant's controllership, not Jikigai's. **Workflow state, and why it is dated.** Read from the GitHub API on **2026-07-31** via `gh api repos/:owner/:repo/actions/workflows`: `fix-constraints-stage-a` **active**, `CI` **active**, `Test: PreToolUse Hooks` **active**, `Claude Code Review` (`.github/workflows/claude-code-review.yml`) **`disabled_manually`**. **Workflow state is GitHub API state and is NOT determinable from source inspection** — the disabled workflow's YAML looks identical to an active one. Counsel review #7086 § Finding 4 found a BLOCKING false statement in exactly this space; the date and method above are the record of how this one was established, and they must be re-established rather than copied forward. Because `ci.yml` fires on `push:[main]` and `merge_group` as well as `pull_request`, **this activity is not a PR-only surface** and must not be described as one. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | **US — Anthropic PBC:** EU-US DPF + SCCs Modules 2+3 + UK IDTA + Swiss Addendum; DPA auto-incorporated via Commercial Terms § C. **US — GitHub Inc:** EU-US DPF + SCCs Module 2 under the Customer Data Protection Agreement, covering the Actions runner, the checkout and the workflow logs. DPF standing verified 2026-07-31: adequacy decision (EU) 2023/1795 in force; *Latombe v Commission* (T-553/23) dismissed by the General Court on 3 September 2025; appeal pending as **C-703/25 P**; the SCCs are the standing fallback. No other new transfer. |
| **(f) Retention** | **Anthropic PBC — 30 days, default**, under the **unsigned** Zero-Retention amendment (see PA-31 §(f) and `knowledge-base/legal/data-processing-agreements/anthropic.md`). **GitHub Actions** — workflow run logs and produced artifacts under the repository's Actions retention settings (GitHub's default is 90 days for logs and artifacts); the `fix-constraints-stage-a` fix artifact is consumed by Stage B and expires at its own pinned **`retention-days: 1`** (`.github/workflows/fix-constraints-stage-a.yml`, the `fix-constraints-patch-*` and `fix-constraints-giveup-*` uploads), **not** with the 90-day default **[2026-07-31 CORRECTION (#7100), applied at review: this previously read "expires with that window", attaching the fix artifact to the preceding 90-day sentence. The 90-day pin in that workflow belongs to a different artifact (`api-spend-*`). The error was conservative — actual retention is 89 days shorter than recorded — but it is still a wrong Art. 30(1)(f) statement, and it forfeited credit for a real storage-limitation measure the record can properly claim.]**. **The checked-out tree is ephemeral** — it exists only for the life of the runner and is not persisted by Jikigai. **Art. 17 path:** the contributor's underlying content lives in the repository and in GitHub's own systems and is subject to the repository's own lifecycle; the Anthropic-side copy has no path beyond expiry of the 30-day window; the Actions logs expire with the retention setting. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | **(1) `permissions: contents: read`** on the agent-bearing job in `fix-constraints-stage-a` — the workflow holds **no write token**; the write capability lives exclusively in the privileged `fix-constraints-stage-b` (`workflow_run`), which never executes the untrusted tree (Git Data API, no checkout, no apply). **(2) PR-head-SHA checkout with `persist-credentials: false`** — running untrusted PR-head code in the `pull_request` context is CodeQL's designated untrusted-context pattern, and no credential is left on disk for it to reach. **(3) Fork exclusion by construction** — forks receive no secrets, so the keyless preflight fails and the agent step is skipped. This is the load-bearing bound on the third-party population. **(4) Pinned action SHA** — `anthropics/claude-code-action@5aa6f47e977fec3321f32c06d8076b1f503f832c` (v1.0.161), pinned by commit rather than by tag, on all three action-using members. **(5) Concurrency and loop guards** — one agent dispatch per head SHA with `cancel-in-progress`, so a push-storm cannot fan out N paid runs; and a loop guard preventing Stage B's own follow-up branches from re-dispatching Stage A. **(6) Credential-liveness preflight** — `.github/actions/anthropic-preflight/action.yml` probes the Messages API with a one-token request and sets `ok=false` on billing or transient failure, so an unavailable key degrades to a skipped step rather than a failed job. **(7) Fix-only scope** — Stage A never grows the suppression baseline and re-verifies the gate is green before uploading. **Honest limitations recorded rather than implied:** the trigger is path-filtered while the agent's `Read`/`Grep` reach the whole checked-out tree (§(c)); the two `ci.yml` jobs run on `push:[main]` and `merge_group` as well as pull requests (§(d)); and no PII scrub is applied to Anthropic-bound content on this path either — PA-22's `sanitizePromptString` TOM is **not** inherited here, for the same reason as at PA-31 §(g)(8). **DPIA status:** screening memo at `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-07-31-dpia-screening-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci.md` — **full DPIA NOT required for PA-33** (one WP248 rev.01 criterion engaged: innovative technology; the subjects initiated the processing themselves by opening a pull request). **LIA:** Arm C of `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-07-31-claude-eval-fleet-and-ci-lia.md`. **Vendor DPA file:** `knowledge-base/legal/data-processing-agreements/anthropic.md`. **Named accepted residual:** the 5-member snapshot in §(d) has no mechanical guard; a sixth `.github/` file gaining the secret would not trip anything. Tracked at deferred item **DEF-2** (#7125). |
| **(h) DSAR (Art. 15 / 20)** | **Not reachable by the automated self-serve export**, for the same structural reason as PA-31 §(h): `DSAR_TABLE_ALLOWLIST` enumerates database tables, and this activity's surfaces are a third-party API's transient request log and GitHub-hosted Actions logs. **The honest Art. 15 route** for a contributor is a manual request to `legal@jikigai.com`, answered by confirming the categories in this entry and by identifying the pull requests they authored (which are themselves public and self-serve on GitHub) — a materially more complete answer than PA-32's, because the underlying content is the contributor's own published pull request. **Art. 20** does not apply. **Art. 21(1):** an objection route for contributors is part of deferred item **DEF-9** (#7126); in practice a contributor may also object by not opening a pull request, which is not an adequate substitute for a stated route. Cross-references **PA-7** for the CLA-signature record, which has its own Art. 15 / 17 posture. |
