## Processing Activity 34 — Dogfooding limb of operator-assisted Soleur runs against alpha-tester content (#7331)

**Scope note.** This record covers the **controller** limb only — Jikigai's own product-learning purpose within an operator-assisted run. The **processor** limb of the same run (the tester-instructed work) is recorded separately at Art. 30(2) in `knowledge-base/legal/article-30-2-register.md`, because a single run served both purposes. See `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-08-06-alpha-tester-controller-processor-determination.md` §6 (dual purpose) and §9 (role collision). This activity is **session-bound**; the ongoing collaborator-access observation is the separate PA-35.

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(a) Controller** | Jikigai SARL, 25 rue de Ponthieu, 75008 Paris, France — **RCS Paris 927 585 729**. Contact: legal@jikigai.com. |
| **(b) Purposes** | Observing how Soleur performs against a real third-party codebase during operator-assisted (guided-onboarding) runs, in order to improve the product — Jikigai's **own** purpose, determined by Jikigai and not instructed by the tester. Distinguish from the instructed limb of the same run (the work the tester asked for), where Jikigai is a **processor** and which is recorded at Art. 30(2). |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Natural persons whose personal data is present in the alpha tester's repository content. On the evidence reconstructed for the 2026-08-06 session, principally **company officers named in RNE (*Registre National des Entreprises*) *pouvoirs* fixture data**. **Not** the tester's venture-database records: the reconstruction found no database records were processed (`knowledge-base/project/specs/feat-one-shot-7331-alpha-tester-terms-dpa/session-scope-reconstruction.md`). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | Names and corporate-office roles of company officers appearing in fixture files; any incidental personal data present in repository source, configuration or documentation. **Residual, recorded not concealed:** the reconstruction shows a fixtures directory listing surfaced a French business-register (RNE) *pouvoirs* sample fixture (filename not reproduced — the counterparty's repository is private and this one is public) but shows no evidence its contents were read; a read cannot be positively excluded, so this category is recorded on the **precautionary** footing rather than as an established fact. |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None identified. Public-register officer data is not an Art. 9 category. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(f) legitimate interests — product improvement. **Assessed in `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-08-06-alpha-tester-repo-observation-lia.md`.** Note the determination's §6 residual: the two purposes were served by one indivisible set of operations, and a regulator could hold that inextricability makes the whole run controllership. The LIA is scoped defensively on that assumption. |
| **(d) Recipients** | **Anthropic PBC** (US) — as **Jikigai's** sub-processor for this limb, because the run egressed under a Jikigai `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. This is the inverse of the BYOK case, where Anthropic is the *user's* processor. No other recipient. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | US transfer to Anthropic PBC. Safeguards: Anthropic's DPA auto-incorporated via Commercial Terms §C, carrying Art. 28(3) terms, EU-US DPF certification, SCCs Modules 2+3, and IDTA. **Open condition:** confirm the account is on **Commercial** and not Consumer Terms (determination §5.3, condition C6). |
| **(f) Retention** | **A 30-day Anthropic-side retention window applies** to content egressed under the Jikigai key, because `knowledge-base/legal/data-processing-agreements/anthropic.md` records `zero_retention_amendment: unsigned`. For the 2026-08-06 session that window expires approximately **2026-09-05**. Jikigai retains no separate copy of tester content for this purpose beyond the operator's local working tree. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) **Behavioural control, effective immediately (determination C1):** no further Jikigai-keyed runs against tester content until an Art. 28(3) instrument exists — the operator uses the tester's machine and the tester's key, or does not run. This is the control that closes the exposure forward and it depends on nobody drafting anything. (2) Content confined to the operator's local machine and the Anthropic API leg; no Jikigai server persistence. (3) TLS in transit to the Anthropic API. (4) Signing the Anthropic Zero-Retention amendment, or reverting to the tester's key, removes the (f) retention window **for future runs**; the 2026-08-06 window lapses on its own schedule and Jikigai cannot shorten it, signed or not. **(5) NEGATIVE — encryption at rest is ABSENT.** The operator workstation is **not** full-disk encrypted (verified 2026-08-06: root filesystem is a plain partition, no LUKS or dm-crypt layer), so tester content in the operator's local working tree is unencrypted at rest and a lost or stolen workstation would expose it. Recorded here because the same absence is disclosed at `knowledge-base/legal/article-30-2-register.md` P-1 (d)(5) and annex §7.3a for the **same** workstation and the **same** run — asymmetric disclosure of one absence across two registers is the #6588 class one step down. |
| **(h) DSAR (Art. 15 / 20)** | Manual route only, to `legal@jikigai.com` — a third party's repository content is not a table in `DSAR_TABLE_ALLOWLIST` (`apps/web-platform/server/dsar-export-allowlist.ts`), so the self-serve export cannot reach it. **No completeness guarantee.** Art. 17 is partly unimplementable for this activity: content that egressed to Anthropic under the Jikigai key lapses only with the 30-day window (§(f)) and Jikigai cannot compel earlier deletion. Art. 21(1) objection is available immediately. |
| **Status** | **Recorded retroactively.** The processing occurred on 2026-08-06; this record was written 2026-08-06 on discovery. Art. 30 requires the record to exist for processing carried out — it did not exist at the time of the run, and that gap is the subject of the determination rather than something this row cures. |

## Processing Activity 35 — Ongoing collaborator-access observation of an alpha tester's private repository (#7331, #1442)

**Scope note.** Distinct from PA-34: that record is **session-bound** (one guided-onboarding run); this one is **continuous and open-ended**. Collapsing the two would leave a live, ongoing activity described by a record whose scope is a single past event. PA-32 (community observation and republication) does **not** cover this — PA-32 is scoped to **public** activity and this repository is **private**.

| Art. 30(1) limb | Entry |
|---|---|
| **(a) Controller** | Jikigai SARL, 25 rue de Ponthieu, 75008 Paris, France — **RCS Paris 927 585 729**. Contact: legal@jikigai.com. |
| **(b) Purposes** | Measuring alpha-programme knowledge-base growth for Jikigai's own validation metrics (#1442), by reading the git history of the `knowledge-base/` tree in an alpha tester's **private** repository under operator collaborator access. Jikigai determines this purpose entirely; the tester does not instruct it. |
| **(c) Categories of data subjects** | Natural persons whose personal data appears in the observed repository tree — the tester's own personnel and any third parties named in committed content. Includes **git commit authorship** (names and email addresses in commit metadata). |
| **(c) Categories of personal data** | Git commit authorship (name, email, timestamp); file and directory names; any personal data present in the committed `knowledge-base/` content that is read. |
| **Special categories (Art. 9 / 10)** | None identified. |
| **Lawful basis** | Art. 6(1)(f) legitimate interests. **Assessed in `knowledge-base/legal/legitimate-interest-assessments/2026-08-06-alpha-tester-repo-observation-lia.md`.** The LIA must record — and does — that a **less intrusive means exists and was considered and declined**: a tester-supplied `git log --stat` yields the same single metric without repository access. The operator elected access on measurement-fidelity grounds, not because the alternative was unavailable. An LIA that omitted the rejected alternative would fail the balancing test it claims to perform. |
| **(d) Recipients** | None. Observation is by the operator; no onward disclosure. Aggregate metrics derived from it may inform internal validation records. |
| **(e) Third-country transfers + safeguards** | None for the observation itself (GitHub hosts the repository; the tester is the GitHub customer for their own repository). If observed content is ever fed to a Soleur agent under a Jikigai key, that egress falls under PA-34's (d)/(e) and is gated by C1. |
| **(f) Retention** | No systematic copy retained. Derived aggregate metrics (commit/file/directory counts) are retained in Jikigai validation records. **Access itself terminates at end of alpha** — see the offboarding step in `knowledge-base/engineering/operations/runbooks/alpha-tester-onboarding.md`. |
| **(g) TOMs (Art. 32)** | (1) Access is repository-scoped GitHub collaborator access, not organisation-wide. (2) Read-only in practice; no writes to tester repositories for this purpose. (3) **Art. 14 notice** to involuntary third-party data subjects, claiming Art. 14(5)(b) disproportionate effort in writing (see the LIA). (4) **NEGATIVE — encryption at rest is ABSENT.** Local clones of the observed repository sit on the operator workstation, which is **not** full-disk encrypted (verified 2026-08-06). This activity is continuous, so the exposure is standing rather than session-bound. (5) **Recommended de-escalation, recorded because it is cheaper than every control above it:** re-derive the metric from **non-personal aggregates** (commit counts, file counts, directory growth) rather than reading repository content, which removes this activity from Art. 4(1) scope almost entirely while preserving the metric. Declining or revoking the access does the same and is also free. The operator has elected to retain and paper (decision UC-1, 2026-08-06); the cheaper options are recorded so the election stays a choice rather than becoming a default for testers 2–10. |
| **(h) DSAR (Art. 15 / 20)** | Manual route only, to `legal@jikigai.com`. The observed material is a third party's git history, not a Jikigai table, so no allowlisted export reaches it; **no completeness guarantee**. Art. 17 is implementable here in the way it is not for PA-34 — revoking access and deleting local clones leaves no systematic copy. Art. 21(1) objection available immediately, and an objection is a strong candidate to be honoured given the necessity limb's recorded weakness. |
| **Status** | **Live and ongoing** as of 2026-08-06. Unlike PA-34 this is not a closed past event, which is why the determination classes closing it as the higher-priority remediation. |

## Register Maintenance

- **Review cadence:** Quarterly + event-driven (new processing activity, new sub-processor, new third-country transfer, new technology, new lawful basis, supervisory-authority engagement).
- **Custodian:** Soleur CLO role.
- **Source-of-truth references:**
  - `knowledge-base/legal/compliance-posture.md` — vendor DPA status, active compliance items.
  - `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md` — outward-facing disclosures (must remain consistent with this register).
  - `docs/legal/gdpr-policy.md` — GDPR-specific disclosures and data-subject rights procedure.

> **Related documents:** This Article 30 register references the Privacy Policy, GDPR Policy, Data Protection Disclosure, and Cookie Policy. Consistency with `docs/legal/privacy-policy.md` and `docs/legal/gdpr-policy.md` must be re-verified on every update. For the processor-relationship characterisation of locally-installed plugin usage, see `docs/legal/data-protection-disclosure.md` — Soleur plugin usage is **not** a processor relationship **when the plugin runs on the user's own machine, under the user's own API key, for the user's own purposes** (Posture A). That characterisation remains accurate for the configuration it describes; it is **narrower than**, not contradicted by, the operator-assisted case recorded at PA-34 and PA-35, where a Jikigai credential or a Jikigai purpose is involved. Do not read it as a general statement about every way Soleur may touch a user's content. See `knowledge-base/legal/audits/2026-08-06-alpha-tester-controller-processor-determination.md` §4.
